| `imagingAndCalculation.reportLevel` | "None" \\| "Brief" \\| "Short" \\| "Detailed" \\| "Full" |  |  |
| `imagingAndCalculation.selectedElementsListing` | "ShowAlert" \\| "ListAll" \\| "UseFilters" |  |  |
| `floorPlanCutPlane` | object |  | Floor Plan Cut Plane settings (Document > Floor Plan Cut Plane) |
| `floorPlanCutPlane.cutHeight` | number |  | Cut plane height relative to the current story (m) |
| `floorPlanCutPlane.topLevel` | number |  | Top of the displayed range, relative to the story given by topStoryOffset (m) |
| `floorPlanCutPlane.topStoryOffset` | integer |  | Top level's story, relative to the current story (1 = one above) |
| `floorPlanCutPlane.bottomLevel` | number |  | Bottom of the displayed range, relative to the story given by bottomStoryOffset (m) |
| `floorPlanCutPlane.bottomStoryOffset` | integer |  | Bottom level's story, relative to the current story (0 = current, -1 = below) |
| `floorPlanCutPlane.fixedLevel` | number |  | Absolute display limit level (m, relative to Project Zero) |
| `layouts` | object |  | Layout preferences |
| `layouts.masterItemColor` | object |  | READ-ONLY: Archicad 26 ignores changes of the master item color (passing it fails) |
| `layouts.masterItemColor.red` | number | yes |  |
| `layouts.masterItemColor.green` | number | yes |  |
| `layouts.masterItemColor.blue` | number | yes |  |
| `layouts.useOwnMasterColor` | boolean |  |  |
| `layouts.adjustDrawingFrameToViewZoom` | boolean |  |  |
| `environment` | object |  | Session switches (not stored in Project Preferences). autoGroup and exportTolerance are read-only |
| `environment.autoIntersect` | boolean |  | Auto-intersect elements (Design > Auto Intersection) |
| `environment.suspendGroups` | boolean |  | Suspend Groups (edit grouped elements individually) |
| `environment.autoTextEnabled` | boolean |  | Autotext conversion on: '<KEY>' placeholders in texts are shown as values |

### `get_geo_location`

Returns the project location: latitude/longitude (degrees), altitude (m), northDirection (degrees, CCW from the +X axis; 90 = north points to +Y/up on the plan), timeZoneMinutes, summerTime, dateTime and sun {azimuth, altitude} used for sun studies, surveyPoint {position (project coordinates, m), visible, locked, projectOriginInSurveyCoordinates}, geoReference (IFC CRS / map conversion: eastings, northings, orthogonalHeight...) and editable.

### `set_geo_location`

Changes the project location (Options > Project Preferences > Location / Survey Point). Give only the fields to change. Changing northDirection or the survey point needs an unlocked Survey Point (pass unlockSurveyPoint: true to unlock temporarily). Date/location changes also recompute the sun position. Returns the new location (as get_geo_location).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `latitude` | number |  | Degrees, + = North |
| `longitude` | number |  | Degrees, + = East |
| `altitude` | number |  | Altitude above sea level (m) |
| `northDirection` | number |  | Direction of geographic North on the plan in degrees, CCW from the +X axis (90 = up/+Y) |
| `timeZoneMinutes` | integer |  | Time zone offset from UTC in minutes, e.g. 240 = UTC+4 |
| `timeZoneOffset` | integer |  | Archicad's time zone disambiguation index (keep the value from get_geo_location) |
| `summerTime` | boolean |  | Daylight saving time (+1 h) |
| `dateTime` | object |  | Date and local time for the sun position (given parts only) |
| `dateTime.year` | integer |  |  |
| `dateTime.month` | integer |  |  |
| `dateTime.day` | integer |  |  |
| `dateTime.hour` | integer |  |  |
| `dateTime.minute` | integer |  |  |
| `dateTime.second` | integer |  |  |
| `displayUnits` | object |  | How Archicad displays these values (does not change the API units) |
| `displayUnits.north` | "DecimalDegree" \\| "DegreeMinSec" |  |  |
| `displayUnits.altitude` | "Meter" \\| "DecFoot" |  |  |
| `displayUnits.longitudeLatitude` | "DecimalDegree" \\| "DegreeMinSec" |  |  |
| `surveyPoint` | object |  | Survey Point position in project coordinates (m); z optional |
| `surveyPointVisible` | boolean |  | Show the Survey Point marker |
| `surveyPointLocked` | boolean |  | Lock/unlock the Survey Point permanently |
| `unlockSurveyPoint` | boolean |  | Temporarily unlock a locked Survey Point for this change (re-locked afterwards) |
| `geoReference` | object |  | IFC map conversion / coordinate reference system data |
| `geoReference.name` | string |  | Coordinate reference system name, e.g. 'EPSG:32638' |
| `geoReference.description` | string |  |  |
| `geoReference.geodeticDatum` | string |  | e.g. 'WGS84' |
| `geoReference.verticalDatum` | string |  |  |
| `geoReference.mapProjection` | string |  | e.g. 'UTM' |
| `geoReference.mapZone` | string |  | e.g. '38N' |
| `geoReference.eastings` | number |  | Easting of the Survey Point in the map CRS (m) |
| `geoReference.northings` | number |  | Northing of the Survey Point in the map CRS (m) |
| `geoReference.orthogonalHeight` | number |  | Height of the Survey Point above the vertical datum (m) |
| `geoReference.xAxisAbscissa` | number |  | X-axis direction vector, easting component (cos of the rotation) |
| `geoReference.xAxisOrdinate` | number |  | X-axis direction vector, northing component (sin of the rotation) |
| `geoReference.scale` | number |  | Map scale factor (1 = none) |

### `rebuild_model`

Refreshes the current (front) window: 'Rebuild' (default, View > Refresh > Rebuild), 'Regenerate' (Rebuild & Regenerate: recomputes all element geometry — use after library/parameter changes or when the display looks stale) or 'Redraw' (repaint only).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | "Rebuild" \\| "Regenerate" \\| "Redraw" |  | Default Rebuild |

### `undo`

Undos the last Archicad operation(s), exactly like Edit > Undo. Every connector create/modify tool call is ONE undo step (its name ends with '(Claude)'), so steps: 1 reverts one whole tool call. The Archicad 26 API has no undo function: this triggers Archicad's Edit menu item (macOS). Returns {performed, requested, undone (menu titles, e.g. 'Undo Create walls (Claude)'), next}. dryRun: true only reports what would be undone next. Note: undo also reverts changes made by the user or other tools.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `steps` | integer |  | Number of steps (default 1) |
| `dryRun` | boolean |  | Only report the current Edit > Undo menu title/enabled state and the last run |
| `force` | boolean |  | Perform even if Archicad reports the menu item as disabled (use only if the state looks stale) |

### `redo`

Redos the last undone Archicad operation(s), exactly like Edit > Redo. Every connector create/modify tool call is ONE undo step (its name ends with '(Claude)'), so steps: 1 reverts one whole tool call. The Archicad 26 API has no redo function: this triggers Archicad's Edit menu item (macOS). Returns {performed, requested, redone (menu titles, e.g. 'Redo Create walls (Claude)'), next}. dryRun: true only reports what would be redone next. Note: undo also reverts changes made by the user or other tools.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `steps` | integer |  | Number of steps (default 1) |
| `dryRun` | boolean |  | Only report the current Edit > Redo menu title/enabled state and the last run |
| `force` | boolean |  | Perform even if Archicad reports the menu item as disabled (use only if the state looks stale) |


## stories

### `get_stories`

Lists all stories (floors) of the project, bottom to top: {stories: [{index, displayNumber, name, level (m above Project Zero), height (m to the next story; may be missing for the top story), floorId (stable id), showOnSections, isCurrent, reservedByOtherUser (Teamwork, only when true)}], firstIndex, lastIndex, currentIndex, count, skipNullFloor, ghostStory}. Call this first for any story-related work: element tools place elements by storyIndex (elevations are relative to the home story), and story names are localized. displayNumber is the number shown in the Navigator: when skipNullFloor is true (e.g. the Russian template) index 0 is shown as '1.', so 'the 3rd floor' is displayNumber 3 = index 2 — always pass the index (or name) to other tools. Indexes change when stories are inserted/deleted; floorId does not. Use atLevels to convert absolute elevations to {storyIndex, offsetFromStory}, and includeElementCounts to see what is on each story.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `includeElementCounts` | boolean |  | Also return elementCount and elementsByType ({Wall: 12, ...}) per story, counted by home story (slower on big projects) |
| `atLevels` | number[] |  | Absolute elevations (m above Project Zero) to look up: returns levelLookup [{level, storyIndex, storyName, storyLevel, offsetFromStory}] |

### `create_stories`

Inserts new stories (Story Settings > Insert Above/Below). Items are applied in order, each one seeing the stories created by the previous items, so several 'Top' items stack upwards. Default: a new top story with the height of the current top story. height is the height of the NEW story; level only (Above/Below) fills the gap between two stories without moving anything. Inserting shifts the indexes of the stories above (floorId stays stable) — re-read indexes from the returned 'stories' before using them. Returns {results: [{story, movedStories?: [{floorId, index, name, levelBefore, level}]} | {error}] in input order, stories: [all stories after the change]}. Story changes are applied immediately (not via Archicad's undo); a failed item is reverted by the add-on. Example: {stories: [{name: 'Этаж 4', height: 3}, {name: 'Кровля', height: 2.5}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `stories` | object[] | yes | Stories to insert, applied in order |
| `stories[].name` | string |  | Name of the new story (default: empty, Archicad then shows only the number) |
| `stories[].height` | number |  | Height of the NEW story (distance to the story above it), in m. Default: the height of the reference story (the top story for 'Top', the bottom story for 'Bottom', relativeTo for 'Above'/'Below'), or 3 m. Giving height makes room for the new story: the stories above move up (below the ground floor: the stories below move down). |
| `stories[].level` | number |  | Elevation of the new story in m above Project Zero. 'Top': must be above the current top story (default: top level + top height; the old top story's height becomes level - its level). 'Above'/'Below' with level but WITHOUT height: the new story fills the gap between its neighbours and nothing moves (level must lie between them). 'Bottom': the new story ends at the old lowest story, so give level OR height. |
| `stories[].position` | "Top" \\| "Bottom" \\| "Above" \\| "Below" |  | Where to insert: 'Top' (default) = above the highest story, 'Bottom' = below the lowest story, 'Above'/'Below' = directly above/below the story given in relativeTo |
| `stories[].relativeTo` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Reference story for position 'Above'/'Below' (required for those, not allowed with 'Top'/'Bottom') |
| `stories[].relativeTo.name` | string | yes |  |
| `stories[].showOnSections` | boolean |  | Show the story level line on sections/elevations ('Show on Sections' column of Story Settings; default true) |

### `modify_stories`

Changes existing stories, patches applied in order: rename, change level (elevation above Project Zero; only this story moves unless moveStoriesAbove), change height to the next story (the stories above move), toggle 'show on sections'. Only the given fields change. Elements keep their position relative to their home story, so changing levels/heights moves them vertically (and changes the height of walls/columns linked to a moved story). Returns {results: [{story, movedStories?} | {error}], stories: [all stories after the change]}; a failed patch is reverted.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `stories` | object[] | yes | Patches: {story, ...fields to change} |
| `stories[].story` | integer \\| string \\| object | yes | Story to change (index, current name, or {floorId}) |
| `stories[].story.name` | string | yes |  |
| `stories[].name` | string |  | New name |
| `stories[].level` | number |  | New elevation in m above Project Zero. By default ONLY this story moves: the story below and this story change height, the stories above stay (so it must stay between its neighbours). With moveStoriesAbove: true the stories above move by the same amount (this story keeps its height). Elements move with their home story. |
| `stories[].moveStoriesAbove` | boolean |  | Only with level: true = move all stories above together with this story (default false = only this story moves) |
| `stories[].height` | number |  | New height to the next story, in m (like 'Height to next' in Story Settings): the stories above move up/down. On the top story it only changes its height value. |
| `stories[].showOnSections` | boolean |  | Show the story level line on sections/elevations ('Show on Sections' column of Story Settings) |

### `delete_stories`

Deletes stories AND EVERY ELEMENT whose home story they are. Use ONLY when the user explicitly asks to delete stories. Run with dryRun: true first to see how many elements each story holds. All references are resolved before anything is deleted (so [2, 3] means the current stories 2 and 3); at least one story must remain. Indexes of the remaining stories shift. By default Archicad closes the gap (the stories above a deleted story move down); keepLevels: true keeps every remaining story at its elevation instead (the story below a deleted one gets taller). Returns {results: [{deleted: story+elementCount} | {error}], movedStories?, stories: [remaining], warning?} or, with dryRun, {dryRun, wouldDelete, remainingCount}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `stories` | integer \\| string \\| object[] | yes | Stories to delete |
| `dryRun` | boolean |  | true = change nothing, only report the stories and element counts that would be deleted |
| `keepLevels` | boolean |  | true = the remaining stories keep their elevations (default false: Archicad moves the stories above down to close the gap) |

### `set_current_story`

Makes a story the current one and (by default) shows its floor plan, like double-clicking the story in the Navigator. New elements are placed on the current story when no storyIndex is given, and floor plan screenshots/zooms show it. Returns {currentStory, windowChanged}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `story` | integer \\| string \\| object | yes | Story to go to (index, name, or {floorId}) |
| `story.name` | string | yes |  |
| `openFloorPlan` | boolean |  | Switch to the floor plan window first (default true). false = only change the current story, keep the active window |


## attributes

### `get_attributes`

Lists Archicad attributes of one type with their settings: {index, name, guid, folder?, ...type fields} using the SAME field names that the create_*/modify_attributes tools accept. Summary fields per type — Layer: hidden, locked, wireframe, intersectionGroup; Pen: color, width (mm); Line: lineType, scaleWithPlan, period; Fill: fillType, usage, bitmapPattern, spacingX/Y (pattern units × spacingX = meters), percent; Composite: totalThickness, skinCount, usage; Surface: color, transparency, reflection, fill, texture; LayerCombination: active, layerCount; ZoneCategory: code, color, stamp; BuildingMaterial: cutFill, pens, surface, uiPriority (0-999 as in the UI), thermal properties; Profile: usage; PenTable: activeForModel/Layout; MEPSystem, OperationProfile. detailed: true adds composite skins and skin lines, per-layer states of layer combinations, dash/symbol items of line types, vector hatch lines, all pens of a pen table, profile size, dimension formats. Without type: returns the number of attributes of every type. Layer/LayerCombination results include the active layer combination.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | "Pen" \\| "Layer" \\| "Line" \\| "Fill" \\| "Composite" \\| "Surface" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "ZoneCategory" \\| "Font" \\| "Profile" \\| "PenTable" \\| "DimensionStandard" \\| "ModelViewOption" \\| "MEPSystem" \\| "OperationProfile" \\| "BuildingMaterial" |  | Attribute type to list (omit to get counts per type) |
| `attributes` | integer \\| string \\| object[] |  | Only these attributes (names, indices or {guid}); nameFilter/offset/limit are ignored then |
| `nameFilter` | string |  | Case-insensitive name substring, or a wildcard pattern with * and ? (e.g. 'Бетон*') |
| `detailed` | boolean |  | Include definition details (skins, layer states, dashes, hatch lines, pens...). Default false |
| `offset` | integer |  | Skip this many matches (paging) |
| `limit` | integer |  | Return at most this many (default 300; the response has total/hasMore — narrow with nameFilter instead of paging when possible) |

### `create_layers`

Creates layers (visible and unlocked unless stated; intersection group 1). Then place elements on them with the 'layer' field of any create_*/modify_elements tool. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `layers` | object[] | yes |  |
| `layers[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `layers[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `layers[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `layers[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `layers[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `layers[].hidden` | boolean |  | true = hide the layer (its elements are not shown and cannot be edited) |
| `layers[].visible` | boolean |  | Alternative to hidden: visible: false hides, visible: true shows |
| `layers[].locked` | boolean |  | true = lock the layer (elements stay visible but cannot be selected/edited) |
| `layers[].wireframe` | boolean |  | true = show the layer's elements as wireframe in 3D |
| `layers[].intersectionGroup` | integer |  | Intersection group number: elements on layers with the SAME number join/intersect each other; 0 = never intersect (new layers default to 1) |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `create_layer_combinations`

Creates layer combinations (saved sets of layer visibility/lock/wireframe/intersection states). By default a new combination captures the CURRENT state of every layer, then 'layers' overrides are applied in order, e.g. {name: 'Plan - structure', base: 'allHidden', layers: [{match: 'Structural*', visible: true}]}. Activate one with apply_layer_combination. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `layerCombinations` | object[] | yes |  |
| `layerCombinations[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `layerCombinations[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `layerCombinations[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `layerCombinations[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `layerCombinations[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `layerCombinations[].base` | "current" \\| "allVisible" \\| "allHidden" \\| "unchanged" |  | Starting layer states before 'layers' is applied: 'current' = the layer states in effect now (default for new combinations), 'allVisible', 'allHidden', 'unchanged' = keep the combination's stored states (default when modifying or copying). Layers missing from a combination are hidden in it |
| `layerCombinations[].layers` | object[] |  | Per-layer states stored in the combination, applied in order (e.g. [{match: '*', hidden: true}, {layer: 'Walls', visible: true}]) |
| `layerCombinations[].layers[].layer` | any |  | Layer name (localized, exact) or index |
| `layerCombinations[].layers[].match` | string |  | Instead of 'layer': select many layers by name. With * or ? it is a case-insensitive wildcard pattern ('*' = all layers, 'Structural*'); without wildcards a case-insensitive substring |
| `layerCombinations[].layers[].hidden` | boolean |  | true = hide the layer (its elements are not shown and cannot be edited) |
| `layerCombinations[].layers[].visible` | boolean |  | Alternative to hidden: visible: false hides, visible: true shows |
| `layerCombinations[].layers[].locked` | boolean |  | true = lock the layer (elements stay visible but cannot be selected/edited) |
| `layerCombinations[].layers[].wireframe` | boolean |  | true = show the layer's elements as wireframe in 3D |
| `layerCombinations[].layers[].intersectionGroup` | integer |  | Intersection group number: elements on layers with the SAME number join/intersect each other; 0 = never intersect (new layers default to 1) |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `create_composites`

Creates composite (multi-skin) structures for walls, slabs, roofs and shells. Skins are listed from the outside/reference side to the inside, each {thickness (m), buildingMaterial, core?, finish?}; the total thickness is the sum. Example: {name: 'Wall 380', skins: [{thickness: 0.02, buildingMaterial: '<plaster>', finish: true}, {thickness: 0.25, buildingMaterial: '<brick>', core: true}, {thickness: 0.1, buildingMaterial: '<insulation>'}, {thickness: 0.01, buildingMaterial: '<render>', finish: true}]} (get building material names with get_attributes type BuildingMaterial). Use the composite with the 'composite' field of create_walls / create_slabs / create_roofs. basedOn copies an existing composite. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `composites` | object[] | yes |  |
| `composites[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `composites[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `composites[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `composites[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `composites[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `composites[].skins` | object[] |  | Skins from the OUTSIDE (reference side / top) to the INSIDE (bottom); the total thickness is their sum. When modifying, the list replaces all skins, but omitted fields of skin i keep the current values of skin i |
| `composites[].skins[].thickness` | number |  | Skin thickness in meters (required for new skins) |
| `composites[].skins[].buildingMaterial` | any |  | Building material of the skin (required for new skins; list with get_attributes type BuildingMaterial) |
| `composites[].skins[].core` | boolean |  | Core skin (structural; reference lines like 'CoreOutside' use it) |
| `composites[].skins[].finish` | boolean |  | Finish skin (can be hidden in partial structure display) |
| `composites[].skins[].endLinePen` | integer |  | Pen of the skin's end lines |
| `composites[].skinLines` | object[] |  | Exactly skins.length + 1 lines: outer contour, each separator between skins, inner contour (default: solid line, pen 1) |
| `composites[].skinLines[].lineType` | any |  | Line type (name/index; 1 = solid) |
| `composites[].skinLines[].pen` | any |  | Line pen |
| `composites[].usage` | object |  | Element types that may use the composite (new composites: all) |
| `composites[].usage.walls` | boolean |  |  |
| `composites[].usage.slabs` | boolean |  |  |
| `composites[].usage.roofs` | boolean |  |  |
| `composites[].usage.shells` | boolean |  |  |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `create_building_materials`

Creates building materials (the material of elements and composite skins: cut fill + pens, surface, intersection priority, physical properties). Unspecified settings are copied from 'basedOn' or, when omitted, from the first building material of the project — so give at least cutFill, surface and uiPriority for a meaningful material. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `buildingMaterials` | object[] | yes |  |
| `buildingMaterials[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `buildingMaterials[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `buildingMaterials[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `buildingMaterials[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `buildingMaterials[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `buildingMaterials[].id` | string |  | Building material ID text |
| `buildingMaterials[].manufacturer` | string |  |  |
| `buildingMaterials[].description` | string |  |  |
| `buildingMaterials[].cutFill` | any |  | Cut fill (Fill attribute name/index) shown where elements are cut |
| `buildingMaterials[].cutFillPen` | integer |  | Cut fill foreground pen |
| `buildingMaterials[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | integer |  | Background pen: 1-255, 0 = transparent, -1 = window background color |
| `buildingMaterials[].surface` | any |  | Surface (3D material) of the cut/visible faces |
| `buildingMaterials[].cutFillOrientation` | "ProjectOrigin" \\| "ElementOrigin" \\| "FitToSkin" |  | How the cut fill pattern is aligned |
| `buildingMaterials[].uiPriority` | integer |  | Intersection priority 0-999 exactly as shown in Archicad's Building Material settings (and as 'connectionPriority' in the official JSON API); where elements meet, the higher value wins (e.g. brick 540, concrete 720 — copy values of existing materials from get_attributes to rank a new one between them). Preferred over 'priority' |
| `buildingMaterials[].priority` | integer |  | Advanced: the internal stored priority value (e.g. 5169; converted to/from uiPriority by Archicad). Give only one of priority/uiPriority |
| `buildingMaterials[].thermalConductivity` | number |  | λ in W/(m·K) |
| `buildingMaterials[].density` | number |  | kg/m³ |
| `buildingMaterials[].heatCapacity` | number |  | J/(kg·K) |
| `buildingMaterials[].embodiedEnergy` | number |  | Embodied energy (MJ/kg) |
| `buildingMaterials[].embodiedCarbon` | number |  | Embodied carbon (kgCO2/kg) |
| `buildingMaterials[].showUncutLines` | boolean |  | Show the skin's uncut lines |
| `buildingMaterials[].participatesInCollisionDetection` | boolean |  |  |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `create_surfaces`

Creates surfaces (3D materials: color, reflection, transparency, 3D hatch, texture). Unspecified settings are copied from 'basedOn' or, when omitted, from the first surface of the project (without its texture). Example: {name: 'Red paint', color: '#B22222', transparency: 0}. Glass: {materialType: 'Glass', color: '#9FC5E8', transparency: 70}. Advanced Cineware (CineRender) channels are not accessible through the API. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `surfaces` | object[] | yes |  |
| `surfaces[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `surfaces[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `surfaces[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `surfaces[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `surfaces[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `surfaces[].materialType` | "General" \\| "Simple" \\| "Matte" \\| "Metal" \\| "Plastic" \\| "Glass" \\| "Glowing" \\| "Constant" |  | Basic-engine material type |
| `surfaces[].color` | string \\| object |  | Surface color |
| `surfaces[].color.red` | number | yes |  |
| `surfaces[].color.green` | number | yes |  |
| `surfaces[].color.blue` | number | yes |  |
| `surfaces[].ambient` | number |  | Ambient reflection 0-100 |
| `surfaces[].diffuse` | number |  | Diffuse reflection 0-100 |
| `surfaces[].specular` | number |  | Specular reflection 0-100 |
| `surfaces[].transparency` | number |  | Transparency 0-100 (0 = opaque) |
| `surfaces[].shininess` | number |  | Shininess 0-100 |
| `surfaces[].transparencyAttenuation` | number |  | Transparency attenuation 0-4 |
| `surfaces[].specularColor` | any |  | Color as "#RRGGBB" (e.g. "#B22222") or {red, green, blue} with components 0..1 |
| `surfaces[].emissionColor` | any |  | Color as "#RRGGBB" (e.g. "#B22222") or {red, green, blue} with components 0..1 |
| `surfaces[].emissionAttenuation` | number |  | Emission attenuation 0-327 |
| `surfaces[].fill` | any \\| boolean |  | Vectorial 3D hatch (Fill attribute) shown on the surface; false = none |
| `surfaces[].fillPen` | integer |  | Pen of the 3D hatch (0 = use the surface color) |
| `surfaces[].texture` | object \\| boolean |  | Texture picture; false removes it |
| `surfaces[].texture.name` | string |  | Texture image file name from the loaded libraries (required when adding a texture) |
| `surfaces[].texture.width` | number |  | Texture tile width in meters |
| `surfaces[].texture.height` | number |  | Texture tile height in meters |
| `surfaces[].texture.angle` | number |  | Texture rotation in degrees |
| `surfaces[].texture.mirrorX` | boolean |  |  |
| `surfaces[].texture.mirrorY` | boolean |  |  |
| `surfaces[].texture.randomShift` | boolean |  |  |
| `surfaces[].texture.useAlphaChannel` | boolean |  |  |
| `surfaces[].texture.alphaAffects` | object |  | What the alpha channel modulates |
| `surfaces[].texture.fit` | "none" \\| "fillRectangle" \\| "fitPicture" |  | Natural-aspect fitting mode |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `create_fills`

Creates fill types: Solid (percentage/screen fill with percent: 25, or an explicit bitmapPattern, e.g. '55AA55AA55AA55AA' = 50%), Empty, Vector (hatch lines in mm on paper, e.g. {name: 'Diagonal 2mm', lines: [{angle: 45, spacing: 2}]}; with scaleWithPlan: true the values are meters in the model, e.g. a 0.3 m tile grid [{angle: 0, spacing: 0.3}, {angle: 90, spacing: 0.3}]), LinearGradient / RadialGradient, Image (texture). Symbol fills and any other fill can be copied with basedOn / duplicate_attributes. Use fills in building materials (cutFill), hatches, zone/slab cover fills. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fills` | object[] | yes |  |
| `fills[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `fills[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `fills[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `fills[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `fills[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `fills[].fillType` | "Solid" \\| "Empty" \\| "Vector" \\| "Symbol" \\| "LinearGradient" \\| "RadialGradient" \\| "Image" |  | Fill kind (new fills: Vector when lines are given, else Solid). Symbol fills can only be copied (duplicate_attributes). Cannot be changed on an existing fill |
| `fills[].usage` | object |  | Where the fill can be chosen (new fills: all three) |
| `fills[].usage.drafting` | boolean |  | Available as drafting fill (2D fills, hatches) |
| `fills[].usage.cut` | boolean |  | Available as cut fill (building materials, cut elements) |
| `fills[].usage.cover` | boolean |  | Available as cover fill (slab/zone/mesh surfaces on the plan) |
| `fills[].scaleWithPlan` | boolean |  | true = pattern scales with the drawing (lengths in meters in the model); false = fixed on paper (lengths in millimeters) |
| `fills[].bitmapPattern` | string \\| integer[] |  | 8x8 screen/bitmap pattern: 16 hex digits = 8 rows, first row first ('FFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF' = solid, '55AA55AA55AA55AA' = 50%), or 8 row bytes. Solid fills with a partial pattern are percentage fills |
| `fills[].spacingX` | number |  | Vector/symbol fills: pattern scale factor X — pattern units × spacingX = METERS (on paper for scale-independent fills, in the model with scaleWithPlan). New fills default to 0.001 (line values in mm on paper) or 1 with scaleWithPlan (values in m). Existing fills: real spacing = lines[i].spacing × spacingX |
| `fills[].spacingY` | number |  | Vector/symbol fills: pattern scale factor Y (same meaning and default as spacingX) |
| `fills[].angle` | number |  | Vector/symbol fills: rotation of the whole pattern in degrees |
| `fills[].percent` | number |  | Solid fills only: coverage of a percentage (screen) fill, 0..1 or 0..100 (e.g. 25 = a 25 % fill); without bitmapPattern a matching 8x8 screen pattern is generated |
| `fills[].lines` | object[] |  | Vector fills: families of parallel hatch lines, e.g. 45° diagonal hatch: [{angle: 45, spacing: 2}]; grid: [{angle: 0, spacing: 2}, {angle: 90, spacing: 2}] (mm on paper unless scaleWithPlan) |
| `fills[].lines[].angle` | number |  | Direction of the parallel lines in degrees (0 = horizontal) |
| `fills[].lines[].spacing` | number | yes | Distance between the parallel lines in pattern units (× spacingX/Y = meters; new fills: mm on paper, or m in the model with scaleWithPlan) |
| `fills[].lines[].offset` | object |  | Origin offset of this line family (pattern units) |
| `fills[].lines[].offsetAlongLine` | number |  | Shift of the dash pattern along the lines (pattern units) |
| `fills[].lines[].dashes` | number[] |  | Alternating dash, gap, dash, gap... lengths in pattern units (even count); omit for continuous lines |
| `fills[].texture` | object |  | Image fills: the picture and its size |
| `fills[].texture.name` | string |  | Image file name from the loaded libraries (required for new image fills) |
| `fills[].texture.width` | number |  | Image width in meters |
| `fills[].texture.height` | number |  | Image height in meters |
| `fills[].texture.angle` | number |  | Rotation in degrees |
| `fills[].texture.mirrorX` | boolean |  |  |
| `fills[].texture.mirrorY` | boolean |  |  |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `create_line_types`

Creates line types: Solid, Dashed ({name: 'Dash 3-1.5', dashes: [{dash: 3, gap: 1.5}]} — millimeters on paper unless scaleWithPlan: true, then meters in the model) or Symbol (items; easier: copy an existing symbol line with basedOn). Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lineTypes` | object[] | yes |  |
| `lineTypes[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `lineTypes[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `lineTypes[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `lineTypes[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `lineTypes[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `lineTypes[].lineType` | "Solid" \\| "Dashed" \\| "Symbol" |  | Line type kind (default: Dashed when dashes are given, Symbol when items are given, else Solid) |
| `lineTypes[].scaleWithPlan` | boolean |  | false (default) = scale-independent: lengths are MILLIMETERS on paper; true = scaled with the plan: lengths are METERS in the model (at defineScale) |
| `lineTypes[].defineScale` | number |  | Scale denominator the pattern is defined at (only for scaleWithPlan, e.g. 100 for 1:100) |
| `lineTypes[].dashes` | object[] |  | Dashed pattern, repeated along the line: e.g. [{dash: 3, gap: 1.5}] (mm unless scaleWithPlan). The period is computed |
| `lineTypes[].dashes[].dash` | number | yes | Visible length |
| `lineTypes[].dashes[].gap` | number | yes | Invisible length after the dash |
| `lineTypes[].items` | object[] |  | Symbol line items (advanced; copying an existing symbol line with duplicate_attributes is easier) |
| `lineTypes[].items[].type` | "Separator" \\| "CenterDot" \\| "CenterLine" \\| "Dot" \\| "RightAngle" \\| "Parallel" \\| "Line" \\| "Circle" \\| "Arc" | yes | Symbol item kind |
| `lineTypes[].items[].centerOffset` | number |  | Vertical distance from the line axis (Separator, CenterDot, CenterLine) |
| `lineTypes[].items[].length` | number |  | Item length along the line (CenterLine, RightAngle, Parallel) |
| `lineTypes[].items[].begin` | object |  | Start position (Dot, RightAngle, Parallel, Line, Circle center, Arc center) |
| `lineTypes[].items[].end` | object |  | End position (Line) |
| `lineTypes[].items[].radius` | number |  | Radius (Circle, Arc) |
| `lineTypes[].items[].beginAngle` | number |  | Arc start angle in degrees, measured from the VERTICAL axis |
| `lineTypes[].items[].endAngle` | number |  | Arc end angle in degrees, measured from the vertical axis |
| `lineTypes[].period` | number |  | Symbol lines: length of one repetition |
| `lineTypes[].height` | number |  | Symbol lines: height of the symbol |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `create_zone_categories`

Creates zone categories {name, code, color, stamp?}. Without 'stamp' the zone stamp (and its parameters) is copied from 'basedOn' or from the first zone category of the project. Assign it to zones with the zone tools' category field. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `zoneCategories` | object[] | yes |  |
| `zoneCategories[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `zoneCategories[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `zoneCategories[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `zoneCategories[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `zoneCategories[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `zoneCategories[].code` | string |  | Category code shown in zone stamps, e.g. 'LIV' |
| `zoneCategories[].color` | string \\| object |  | Zone fill color on the plan |
| `zoneCategories[].color.red` | number | yes |  |
| `zoneCategories[].color.green` | number | yes |  |
| `zoneCategories[].color.blue` | number | yes |  |
| `zoneCategories[].stamp` | string \\| integer \\| object |  | Zone stamp library part (localized name, use search_library_parts); resets the stamp parameters to its defaults |
| `zoneCategories[].stamp.name` | string | yes |  |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `create_profiles`

Creates complex profiles (custom cross-sections for walls, beams, columns, handrails) from polygons: each shape is {polygon (m, x horizontal / y vertical, straight edges), buildingMaterial, core?}. Example: a 0.3 x 0.5 m rectangular beam: {name: 'Beam 300x500', usage: {beams: true, columns: true}, shapes: [{polygon: [{x:-0.15,y:0},{x:0.15,y:0},{x:0.15,y:0.5},{x:-0.15,y:0.5}], buildingMaterial: '<concrete>'}]}. Stretch zones/parameters are not supported (edit in Archicad's Profile Manager). basedOn copies an existing profile. Use it with the 'profile' field of walls/columns/beams. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `profiles` | object[] | yes |  |
| `profiles[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new attribute (must be unique within its type) |
| `profiles[].basedOn` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Copy this existing attribute of the same type (all settings); the other given fields are applied on top |
| `profiles[].basedOn.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `profiles[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Attribute folder path in the Attribute Manager, e.g. ['Walls', 'Concrete'] or 'Walls/Concrete' (created when missing; [] = root folder). Only Layer, Composite, Profile, Surface and BuildingMaterial attributes have folders in Archicad 26 |
| `profiles[].ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Per-item override of the tool-level ifExists |
| `profiles[].usage` | object |  | Element types that may use the profile (new profiles: walls, beams, columns) |
| `profiles[].usage.walls` | boolean |  |  |
| `profiles[].usage.beams` | boolean |  |  |
| `profiles[].usage.columns` | boolean |  |  |
| `profiles[].usage.handrails` | boolean |  |  |
| `profiles[].usage.otherObjects` | boolean |  | Other GDL-based objects (railings etc.) |
| `profiles[].shapes` | object[] |  | Cross-section parts (replaces the whole geometry). Required for new profiles unless basedOn is given |
| `profiles[].shapes[].polygon` | object[] \\| object | yes | Cross-section polygon in meters, x = horizontal, y = vertical (for walls y is the height direction); straight edges only; holes allowed |
| `profiles[].shapes[].buildingMaterial` | any | yes | Building material of this part (fill, pens, surface come from it) |
| `profiles[].shapes[].core` | boolean |  | Core part (default true) |
| `profiles[].shapes[].finish` | boolean |  | Finish part (default false) |
| `profiles[].shapes[].contourVisible` | boolean |  | Draw the contour lines (default true) |
| `profiles[].shapes[].contourPen` | integer |  | Contour pen (default: the material's cut fill pen) |
| `profiles[].shapes[].contourLineType` | any |  | Contour line type (default 1 = solid) |
| `profiles[].shapes[].cutEndLinePen` | any |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `profiles[].shapes[].cutEndLineType` | any |  | Attribute by name (exact, localized — list with get_attributes), index, or {guid} |
| `ifExists` | any |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `duplicate_attributes`

Copies existing attributes of any type except Pen/Font (e.g. a pen set, a symbol fill, an MEP system, a model view option, a dimension standard, an operation profile, a composite) under a new name, optionally changing fields on the copy: each item is {source, name, folder?, ...fields of that type as in the create_* tools / modify_attributes}. Runs in one undo step. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid, created: true} | {..., existed: true} | {error}]} in input order; one failing item does not stop the others. Names are localized (Russian Archicad): look existing attributes up with get_attributes first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | "Layer" \\| "Line" \\| "Fill" \\| "Composite" \\| "Surface" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "ZoneCategory" \\| "Profile" \\| "PenTable" \\| "DimensionStandard" \\| "ModelViewOption" \\| "MEPSystem" \\| "OperationProfile" \\| "BuildingMaterial" | yes | Attribute type to copy (every type except Pen and Font) |
| `attributes` | object[] | yes |  |
| `attributes[].source` | integer \\| string \\| object | yes | Attribute to copy (name, index or {guid}) |
| `attributes[].source.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `attributes[].name` | string | yes | Name of the copy |
| `attributes[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Folder of the copy, e.g. ['Imported'] |
| `ifExists` | "error" \\| "skip" \\| "update" |  | Name collision handling for all items: 'error' (default), 'skip' (reuse the existing attribute), 'update' (apply the fields to it) |

### `modify_attributes`

Changes existing attributes of any type in one undo step. Each item is {type, attribute (name/index/{guid}), name? (rename), folder? (move), ...fields to change} with the same field names as get_attributes returns and the create_* tools accept; only the given fields change. Examples: {type: 'Layer', attribute: 'Мебель', locked: true}; {type: 'Surface', attribute: 'Red paint', color: '#AA0000'}; {type: 'Composite', attribute: 'Wall 380', skins: [...]}; {type: 'Pen', attribute: 12, color: '#FF0000', width: 0.5}; {type: 'LayerCombination', attribute: 'Plan', base: 'current'} (re-captures the current layer states). Fonts cannot be modified. Returns {results: [{index, name, guid} | {error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attributes` | object[] | yes |  |
| `attributes[].type` | "Pen" \\| "Layer" \\| "Line" \\| "Fill" \\| "Composite" \\| "Surface" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "ZoneCategory" \\| "Profile" \\| "PenTable" \\| "DimensionStandard" \\| "ModelViewOption" \\| "MEPSystem" \\| "OperationProfile" \\| "BuildingMaterial" | yes | Attribute type (fonts cannot be modified) |
| `attributes[].attribute` | integer \\| string \\| object | yes | The attribute to change: exact name (localized), index, or {guid} |
| `attributes[].attribute.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `attributes[].name` | string |  | Rename to this name |
| `attributes[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Move the attribute into this folder (created when missing; [] = root) |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step |

### `delete_attributes`

Deletes attributes of one type (one undo step). Archicad reassigns elements that used a deleted attribute (check them afterwards). WARNING: deleting a LAYER deletes every element on it — the tool refuses layers that still hold elements unless force: true (move elements with modify_elements first). Pens, fonts, the Archicad layer and some built-in attributes (the last one of a type, Solid/Empty fills, line type 1) cannot be deleted. Returns {results: [{index, name, deleted: true} | {error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | "Layer" \\| "Line" \\| "Fill" \\| "Composite" \\| "Surface" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "ZoneCategory" \\| "Profile" \\| "PenTable" \\| "DimensionStandard" \\| "ModelViewOption" \\| "MEPSystem" \\| "OperationProfile" \\| "BuildingMaterial" | yes | Attribute type (pens and fonts cannot be deleted) |
| `attributes` | integer \\| string \\| object[] | yes | Attributes to delete: names (localized), indices or {guid} |
| `force` | boolean |  | Layers only: also delete layers that still hold elements (the elements are deleted too) |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `modify_pens`

Changes pen colors, weights and descriptions (pens cannot be created: there are always 255). Without penTable the pens in effect in the model are changed; with penTable a stored pen set is edited (it does not become active). Read the current pens with get_attributes {type: 'Pen'} or {type: 'PenTable', detailed: true}. Returns {results: [{index, color, width} | {error}], activePenTable | penTable}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pens` | object[] | yes | [{index 1-255, color?: '#RRGGBB', width?: mm on paper, description?}] |
| `pens[].index` | integer | yes | Pen index 1-255 |
| `pens[].color` | string \\| object |  | Color as "#RRGGBB" (e.g. "#B22222") or {red, green, blue} with components 0..1 |
| `pens[].color.red` | number | yes |  |
| `pens[].color.green` | number | yes |  |
| `pens[].color.blue` | number | yes |  |
| `pens[].width` | number |  | Pen weight in millimeters on paper (e.g. 0.25) |
| `pens[].description` | string |  | Pen description text |
| `penTable` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Pen set (PenTable attribute) to edit instead of the active pens |
| `penTable.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `apply_layer_combination`

Makes a saved layer combination active in the current model window (sets the visibility/lock/wireframe of all layers). List combinations with get_attributes {type: 'LayerCombination'}. Returns {ok, layerCombination, activeLayerCombination}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `layerCombination` | integer \\| string \\| object | yes | Layer combination name (localized), index or {guid} |
| `layerCombination.index` | integer | yes |  |

### `set_layer_states`

Shows/hides, locks/unlocks, switches wireframe or the intersection group of layers directly (the active layer settings; saved combinations are not changed — use modify_attributes type LayerCombination for those). Items run in order in one undo step, so 'isolate' works as [{match: '*', hidden: true}, {layer: 'Стены', visible: true}]. The Archicad layer cannot be hidden/locked (skipped for patterns). Returns per item {changed: [{index, name}], changedCount, alreadyInStateCount, skipped?} | {error}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `layers` | object[] | yes |  |
| `layers[].layer` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Layer name (localized, exact) or index |
| `layers[].layer.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `layers[].match` | string |  | Instead of 'layer': select many layers by name. With * or ? it is a case-insensitive wildcard pattern ('*' = all layers, 'Structural*'); without wildcards a case-insensitive substring |
| `layers[].hidden` | boolean |  | true = hide the layer (its elements are not shown and cannot be edited) |
| `layers[].visible` | boolean |  | Alternative to hidden: visible: false hides, visible: true shows |
| `layers[].locked` | boolean |  | true = lock the layer (elements stay visible but cannot be selected/edited) |
| `layers[].wireframe` | boolean |  | true = show the layer's elements as wireframe in 3D |
| `layers[].intersectionGroup` | integer |  | Intersection group number: elements on layers with the SAME number join/intersect each other; 0 = never intersect (new layers default to 1) |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |


## element-query

### `find_elements`

Finds elements in the open project with combinable filters (type, story, layer, renovation status, visibility/editability, selection, Element ID pattern, library part name, group, hotlink, lock state, spatial region) and returns a paginated list [{guid, type, storyIndex, layer: {index, name}, elementId, boundingBox?, libraryPart?}] plus total/hasMore. All filters are optional and AND-combined; with no filter every main element is listed. Use the GUIDs with get_element_details, get_element_quantities, modify_elements, set_selection etc. For counts only use get_element_counts. Coordinates in meters.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `types` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Dimension" \\| "RadialDimension" \\| "LevelDimension" \\| "AngleDimension" \\| "Text" \\| "Label" \\| "Zone" \\| "Hatch" \\| "Line" \\| "PolyLine" \\| "Arc" \\| "Circle" \\| "Spline" \\| "Hotspot" \\| "CutPlane" \\| "Camera" \\| "CamSet" \\| "Group" \\| "SectElem" \\| "Drawing" \\| "Picture" \\| "Detail" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Hotlink" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "CurtainWallSegment" \\| "CurtainWallFrame" \\| "CurtainWallPanel" \\| "CurtainWallJunction" \\| "CurtainWallAccessory" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "ChangeMarker" \\| "Stair" \\| "Riser" \\| "Tread" \\| "StairStructure" \\| "Railing" \\| "RailingToprail" \\| "RailingHandrail" \\| "RailingRail" \\| "RailingPost" \\| "RailingInnerPost" \\| "RailingBaluster" \\| "RailingPanel" \\| "RailingSegment" \\| "RailingNode" \\| "RailingBalusterSet" \\| "RailingPattern" \\| "RailingToprailEnd" \\| "RailingHandrailEnd" \\| "RailingRailEnd" \\| "RailingToprailConnection" \\| "RailingHandrailConnection" \\| "RailingRailConnection" \\| "RailingEndFinish" \\| "BeamSegment" \\| "ColumnSegment" \\| "Opening"[] |  | Only these element types. Default: every main type (sub-elements such as curtain wall panels, stair treads or railing posts are only included when listed here or with includeSubelements=true) |
| `excludeTypes` | any[] |  | Skip these element types |
| `includeSubelements` | boolean |  | Also list sub-elements (curtain wall/stair/railing parts, beam/column segments) when 'types' is not given, and the hidden GDL part Objects/Lamps owned by curtain walls, railings and stairs (skipped otherwise, counted in stats.ownedPartObjectsSkipped). Default false |
| `storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Only elements whose HOME story is this one (index or localized name, see get_stories) |
| `storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `stories` | any[] |  | Only elements whose home story is one of these |
| `layers` | integer \\| string \\| object[] |  | Only elements on these layers (layer names are localized, e.g. Russian — take them from get_attributes or earlier results) |
| `renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default"[] |  | Only elements with one of these renovation statuses |
| `filters` | "OnVisibleLayer" \\| "Editable" \\| "OnActiveStory" \\| "In3D" \\| "InMyWorkspace" \\| "Independent" \\| "OnActiveLayout" \\| "InCroppedView" \\| "HasAccessRight" \\| "VisibleByRenovation" \\| "Overridden" \\| "InStructureDisplay" \\| "FromFloorPlan"[] |  | Archicad visibility/editability filters; an element must pass ALL of them |
| `selectedOnly` | boolean |  | Only elements in the current selection |
| `withinElements` | any \\| object[] |  | Restrict the search to these elements (e.g. to refine an earlier result) |
| `elementId` | string |  | Element ID (the ID shown in the Info Box) wildcard pattern, case-insensitive: '*' = any characters, '?' = one character; without wildcards the ID must match exactly. Examples: 'W-*', '*01', 'D-0??' |
| `libraryPart` | string |  | Library part name contains this text (case-insensitive). Applies to objects, lamps, windows, doors, skylights and zone stamps; other types are excluded. Names are localized |
| `groupGuid` | any |  | Only members of this group (nested groups included); groupGuid comes from get_element_details |
| `grouped` | boolean |  | true = only grouped elements, false = only ungrouped ones |
| `hotlinkGuid` | any |  | Only elements belonging to this hotlink instance |
| `inHotlink` | boolean |  | true = only elements that come from a hotlinked module, false = only own elements |
| `locked` | boolean |  | true = only locked elements, false = only unlocked ones |
| `region` | object |  | Spatial filter on the element's 3D bounding box (2D elements have a flat box at z=0). Give any subset of the limits; e.g. {xMin:0, yMin:0, xMax:10, yMax:8} for a plan rectangle |
| `region.xMin` | number |  | West limit, m |
| `region.yMin` | number |  | South limit, m |
| `region.xMax` | number |  | East limit, m |
| `region.yMax` | number |  | North limit, m |
| `region.zMin` | number |  | Lower limit, m, ABSOLUTE (relative to project zero, not to the story) |
| `region.zMax` | number |  | Upper limit, m, ABSOLUTE |
| `region.mode` | "Intersects" \\| "Inside" |  | Intersects (default): element bounding box touches the region; Inside: box lies completely inside |
| `includeBoundingBox` | boolean |  | Add boundingBox {xMin,yMin,zMin,xMax,yMax,zMax} (m, z absolute) to each element. Default false |
| `includeLibraryPart` | boolean |  | Add the library part name of objects/lamps/doors/windows/skylights/zones. Default false |
| `includeElementId` | boolean |  | Include the Element ID string. Default true |
| `offset` | integer |  | Skip this many matches (pagination). Default 0 |
| `limit` | integer |  | Return at most this many matches. Default 500 |

### `get_element_counts`

Counts elements per type — optionally also per story, per layer and per renovation status — with the same filters as find_elements. Output: {total, byType: {Wall: 12, ...}, byStory?: [{storyIndex, storyName, total, byType}], byLayer?: [{layer, total, byType}] (largest first), byRenovationStatus?}. Good first call to get an overview of a project.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `types` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Dimension" \\| "RadialDimension" \\| "LevelDimension" \\| "AngleDimension" \\| "Text" \\| "Label" \\| "Zone" \\| "Hatch" \\| "Line" \\| "PolyLine" \\| "Arc" \\| "Circle" \\| "Spline" \\| "Hotspot" \\| "CutPlane" \\| "Camera" \\| "CamSet" \\| "Group" \\| "SectElem" \\| "Drawing" \\| "Picture" \\| "Detail" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Hotlink" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "CurtainWallSegment" \\| "CurtainWallFrame" \\| "CurtainWallPanel" \\| "CurtainWallJunction" \\| "CurtainWallAccessory" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "ChangeMarker" \\| "Stair" \\| "Riser" \\| "Tread" \\| "StairStructure" \\| "Railing" \\| "RailingToprail" \\| "RailingHandrail" \\| "RailingRail" \\| "RailingPost" \\| "RailingInnerPost" \\| "RailingBaluster" \\| "RailingPanel" \\| "RailingSegment" \\| "RailingNode" \\| "RailingBalusterSet" \\| "RailingPattern" \\| "RailingToprailEnd" \\| "RailingHandrailEnd" \\| "RailingRailEnd" \\| "RailingToprailConnection" \\| "RailingHandrailConnection" \\| "RailingRailConnection" \\| "RailingEndFinish" \\| "BeamSegment" \\| "ColumnSegment" \\| "Opening"[] |  | Only these element types. Default: every main type (sub-elements such as curtain wall panels, stair treads or railing posts are only included when listed here or with includeSubelements=true) |
| `excludeTypes` | any[] |  | Skip these element types |
| `includeSubelements` | boolean |  | Also list sub-elements (curtain wall/stair/railing parts, beam/column segments) when 'types' is not given, and the hidden GDL part Objects/Lamps owned by curtain walls, railings and stairs (skipped otherwise, counted in stats.ownedPartObjectsSkipped). Default false |
| `storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Only elements whose HOME story is this one (index or localized name, see get_stories) |
| `storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `stories` | any[] |  | Only elements whose home story is one of these |
| `layers` | integer \\| string \\| object[] |  | Only elements on these layers (layer names are localized, e.g. Russian — take them from get_attributes or earlier results) |
| `renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default"[] |  | Only elements with one of these renovation statuses |
| `filters` | "OnVisibleLayer" \\| "Editable" \\| "OnActiveStory" \\| "In3D" \\| "InMyWorkspace" \\| "Independent" \\| "OnActiveLayout" \\| "InCroppedView" \\| "HasAccessRight" \\| "VisibleByRenovation" \\| "Overridden" \\| "InStructureDisplay" \\| "FromFloorPlan"[] |  | Archicad visibility/editability filters; an element must pass ALL of them |
| `selectedOnly` | boolean |  | Only elements in the current selection |
| `withinElements` | any \\| object[] |  | Restrict the search to these elements (e.g. to refine an earlier result) |
| `elementId` | string |  | Element ID (the ID shown in the Info Box) wildcard pattern, case-insensitive: '*' = any characters, '?' = one character; without wildcards the ID must match exactly. Examples: 'W-*', '*01', 'D-0??' |
| `libraryPart` | string |  | Library part name contains this text (case-insensitive). Applies to objects, lamps, windows, doors, skylights and zone stamps; other types are excluded. Names are localized |
| `groupGuid` | any |  | Only members of this group (nested groups included); groupGuid comes from get_element_details |
| `grouped` | boolean |  | true = only grouped elements, false = only ungrouped ones |
| `hotlinkGuid` | any |  | Only elements belonging to this hotlink instance |
| `inHotlink` | boolean |  | true = only elements that come from a hotlinked module, false = only own elements |
| `locked` | boolean |  | true = only locked elements, false = only unlocked ones |
| `region` | object |  | Spatial filter on the element's 3D bounding box (2D elements have a flat box at z=0). Give any subset of the limits; e.g. {xMin:0, yMin:0, xMax:10, yMax:8} for a plan rectangle |
| `region.xMin` | number |  | West limit, m |
| `region.yMin` | number |  | South limit, m |
| `region.xMax` | number |  | East limit, m |

| `region.zMin` | number |  | Lower limit, m, ABSOLUTE (relative to project zero, not to the story) |

### `get_element_quantities`

Calculated quantities of elements, as Archicad lists them: every field of the element type's quantity record with descriptive names. Units: lengths m, areas m², volumes m³, angles degrees, counts integers. Examples — Wall: volume, grossVolume, surfaceReferenceSide, surfaceOppositeSide, length, area (plan), minHeight/maxHeight, windowsSurface, doorsSurface; Slab: volume, topSurface, bottomSurface, edgeSurface, perimeter, holesSurface; Zone: area, netArea, calculatedArea, volume, perimeter, wallsSurface; Column/Beam: core/veneer volumes & surfaces; Window/Door: surface, width/height per side, sill/head heights; Roof/Shell/Mesh/Morph/Object/CurtainWall/Stair/Railing and their parts are supported too. 'Conditional' values follow the project's calculation rules. Also returns composite skins per building material and per-type totals (sums of additive fields) + building material volume totals. Get GUIDs from find_elements first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to measure (max 500 per call) |
| `includeComposites` | boolean |  | List skin volumes / projected areas per building material (walls, slabs, roofs, shells...). Default true |
| `includeParts` | boolean |  | Also return per-part quantities (e.g. each roof plane of a multi-plane roof, each story of a morph). Default false |
| `includeTotals` | boolean |  | Add 'totals' per element type (count + sums of lengths, areas, volumes, counts) and 'buildingMaterialTotals'. Default true |
| `minOpeningSize` | number |  | m²: openings smaller than this do not reduce wall surfaces/volumes (default 0 = every opening reduces them) |
| `includeExposedSurfaces` | boolean |  | Also compute exposed (uncovered) surface areas per surface material. Default false |
| `coverElements` | any[] |  | Elements that cover the measured ones for the exposed-surface calculation |

### `get_connected_elements`

Elements attached to or hosted by each given element: windows/doors of a wall, skylights of a roof/shell, openings cut into walls/slabs/beams, labels attached to the element; plus the owner/host (wall of a door, roof of a skylight, element of a label, curtain wall of a panel...). Optionally solid element operations (operators cutting this element / targets it cuts) and roof/shell trims. Output: [{guid, type, connectedCount, connected: {Window: [guid...], Door: [...], ...}, owner?, solidOperations?, trims?}]. For zone boundaries and wall joins use get_element_relations; for curtain wall/stair/railing parts use get_subelements.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `types` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Dimension" \\| "RadialDimension" \\| "LevelDimension" \\| "AngleDimension" \\| "Text" \\| "Label" \\| "Zone" \\| "Hatch" \\| "Line" \\| "PolyLine" \\| "Arc" \\| "Circle" \\| "Spline" \\| "Hotspot" \\| "CutPlane" \\| "Camera" \\| "CamSet" \\| "Group" \\| "SectElem" \\| "Drawing" \\| "Picture" \\| "Detail" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Hotlink" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "CurtainWallSegment" \\| "CurtainWallFrame" \\| "CurtainWallPanel" \\| "CurtainWallJunction" \\| "CurtainWallAccessory" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "ChangeMarker" \\| "Stair" \\| "Riser" \\| "Tread" \\| "StairStructure" \\| "Railing" \\| "RailingToprail" \\| "RailingHandrail" \\| "RailingRail" \\| "RailingPost" \\| "RailingInnerPost" \\| "RailingBaluster" \\| "RailingPanel" \\| "RailingSegment" \\| "RailingNode" \\| "RailingBalusterSet" \\| "RailingPattern" \\| "RailingToprailEnd" \\| "RailingHandrailEnd" \\| "RailingRailEnd" \\| "RailingToprailConnection" \\| "RailingHandrailConnection" \\| "RailingRailConnection" \\| "RailingEndFinish" \\| "BeamSegment" \\| "ColumnSegment" \\| "Opening"[] |  | Connected element types to look for. Default: Window, Door, Skylight, Opening, Label |
| `includeOwner` | boolean |  | Return the host/owner element. Default true |
| `includeSolidOperations` | boolean |  | Return solid element operation links. Default false |
| `includeTrims` | boolean |  | Return trim relations (elements trimmed to roofs/shells). Default false |
| `includeTypes` | boolean |  | Return connected elements as {guid, type} instead of plain GUIDs. Default false |

### `get_element_relations`

Topological relations computed by Archicad. Zone: relatedElementsByType (walls, columns, slabs, doors, windows, curtain walls ... bounding or inside the zone), wallParts/beamParts/curtainWallSegmentParts (boundary pieces: zoneEdgeIndex, tBegin, tEnd), niches (height, polygon). Wall: connectionPolygon (real plan outline after joins) and the walls connected at its begin/end, to its reference line, with their ends, or crossing it. Beam: the same for beams, plus per segment. Window/Door/Skylight/CurtainWallPanel: fromZone/toZone (the zones on both sides). Roof/Shell: zones below. Other types return an error item.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `includePolygons` | boolean |  | Include wall/beam connection polygons and zone niche polygons ({points, arcs?, holes?}, m). Default true |

### `get_subelements`

Parts of hierarchical elements, grouped by type: CurtainWall → CurtainWallSegment/Frame/Panel/Junction/Accessory (frames and panels with className, begin/end or centroid, hidden/degenerate flags); Stair → Riser/Tread/StairStructure (sequenceNumber, landing flag); Railing → RailingSegment/Node/Post/InnerPost/Toprail/Handrail/Rail/Panel/BalusterSet/Baluster/Pattern and rail ends/connections; Beam → BeamSegment; Column → ColumnSegment. Passing a sub-element GUID returns its owner. The part GUIDs work with get_element_details, get_element_quantities and get_element_3d_geometry.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `types` | "CurtainWallSegment" \\| "CurtainWallFrame" \\| "CurtainWallPanel" \\| "CurtainWallJunction" \\| "CurtainWallAccessory" \\| "Riser" \\| "Tread" \\| "StairStructure" \\| "RailingToprail" \\| "RailingHandrail" \\| "RailingRail" \\| "RailingPost" \\| "RailingInnerPost" \\| "RailingBaluster" \\| "RailingPanel" \\| "RailingSegment" \\| "RailingNode" \\| "RailingBalusterSet" \\| "RailingPattern" \\| "RailingToprailEnd" \\| "RailingHandrailEnd" \\| "RailingRailEnd" \\| "RailingToprailConnection" \\| "RailingHandrailConnection" \\| "RailingRailConnection" \\| "RailingEndFinish" \\| "BeamSegment" \\| "ColumnSegment"[] |  | Only these sub-element types |
| `maxPerType` | integer |  | List at most this many parts per type (counts are always complete). Default 500 |
| `includeDetails` | boolean |  | Include the type-specific fields of each part. Default true |

### `get_selection`

Returns what is currently selected in Archicad: {selectionType: None|Elements|MarqueePolygon|MarqueeBox|MarqueeRotatedBox, total, editableCount, elements: [{guid, type, storyIndex, layer, elementId, partial?}], marquee?: {box, polygon, boxRotationAngle, multiStory}}. Use it when the user refers to 'the selected elements' or 'this'.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `onlyEditable` | boolean |  | Only editable selected elements. Default false |
| `includePartial` | boolean |  | Include partially selected elements (e.g. with the marquee). Default false |
| `marqueeRelation` | "InsidePartially" \\| "InsideEntirely" \\| "OutsidePartially" \\| "OutsideEntirely" |  | When a marquee is active: which elements count as selected relative to it. Default InsidePartially |
| `includeElementId` | boolean |  | Include Element IDs. Default true |
| `offset` | integer |  | Skip this many selected elements. Default 0 |
| `limit` | integer |  | Return at most this many. Default 1000 |

### `set_selection`

Changes the Archicad selection (shows the user which elements you mean, or prepares a selection-based command). mode 'set' replaces the selection (no elements = clear), 'add' adds, 'remove' deselects the given elements, 'clear' deselects everything. Elements on hidden/locked layers or on another story than the active floor plan may fail — see 'failed'. Output: {mode, requested, applied, failed: [{guid, error}], selectionCount}. Selection changes are not undo steps.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | "set" \\| "add" \\| "remove" \\| "clear" |  | Default 'set' |
| `elements` | string \\| object[] |  | Elements to (de)select; required for 'add' and 'remove' |

### `get_element_2d_geometry`

The 2D drawing primitives Archicad draws for elements in the active window (floor plan, section, layout...): lines, arcs/circles/ellipses, polylines, polygons (with holes; fills), texts and pictures, with pen numbers and a role for special parts (fill, openingDimension, arrow, drawingBorder). Helps to understand what a plan looks like (e.g. a door's swing, an object's symbol). Output per element: {guid, type, total, counts, extent {xMin,yMin,xMax,yMax}, primitives: [{kind, pen, ...}], truncated?, hotspots?}. Coordinates are [x, y] arrays in meters; angles in degrees; arcs in polygons use {index, angle} like polygon inputs. Hatch pattern lines are only counted unless includeFillPatterns=true. Use summaryOnly for large elements.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `maxPrimitives` | integer |  | Max primitives listed per element (all are counted). Default 300 |
| `maxPoints` | integer |  | Max coordinates listed per element. Default 20000 |
| `kinds` | "point" \\| "line" \\| "arc" \\| "polyline" \\| "polygon" \\| "text" \\| "picture"[] |  | Only list these primitive kinds ('arc' includes circles and ellipses) |
| `summaryOnly` | boolean |  | Only counts and extent, no primitive list. Default false |
| `includeFillPatterns` | boolean |  | Also list the individual hatch pattern lines of fills. Default false |
| `includeHotspots` | boolean |  | Also return the element's hotspots (snap points) as [x, y, z] (m; z of 2D hotspots is 0). Default false |

### `get_element_3d_geometry`

3D model data of elements as generated by Archicad. mode 'summary' (default): bodyCount, vertexCount, edgeCount, polygonCount, world boundingBox {xMin..zMax} (m, z absolute) and the materials used (surface attribute or GDL material name, polygon count). mode 'mesh': additionally bodies: [{source (element/part GUID), bodyIndex, closed, material, vertices: [[x,y,z], ...], polygons: [{v: [0-based vertex indices of the outer contour], holes?, material?, normal?}]}] within a vertex/polygon budget. Curtain walls, stairs, railings, beams and columns include their parts. 2D elements have no 3D model (error item).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `mode` | "summary" \\| "mesh" |  | Default 'summary' |
| `includeSubelements` | boolean |  | Include parts of curtain walls/stairs/railings/beams/columns. Default true |
| `includeMaterials` | boolean |  | Report materials with polygon counts. Default true |
| `includeNormals` | boolean |  | mesh mode: add world-space polygon normals. Default false |
| `maxVertices` | integer |  | mesh mode: vertex budget per element; bodies beyond it are summarized only. Default 5000 |
| `maxPolygons` | integer |  | mesh mode: polygon budget per element. Default 5000 |


## element-edit

### `move_elements`

Moves elements of ANY type (walls, slabs, objects, zones, lines, dimensions, ...) by a displacement vector, like Edit > Move > Drag. Pass `elements` + `vector` to move them all by the same offset, or `moves` to move different sets by different vectors (all in one undo step). A z component moves model elements vertically (use elevate_elements for pure vertical moves). Windows/doors move along their host wall; connected dimensions and labels follow automatically. Returns {results: [{guid, newGuid?, warning?} | {guid, error}], warnings?} in input order (a 'warning' on an item means Archicad may have ignored it); with copy=true the copy-style result {results: [{guid, copies: [new GUIDs]}], createdCount, additionalCreated?}. Locked elements, elements on locked/hidden layers, hotlinked elements and elements outside your Teamwork workspace are reported with an actionable error instead of being edited. With `moves` the result is {operations: [<result per move>]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] |  | Elements to move (together with `vector`) |
| `vector` | object |  | Displacement applied to `elements`, in meters |
| `moves` | object[] |  | Alternative to elements+vector: several independent moves [{elements, vector}] in one undo step |
| `moves[].elements` | any | yes |  |
| `moves[].vector` | any | yes |  |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `copy_elements`

Duplicates elements of ANY type displaced by `vector` (Edit > Move > Drag a Copy); the originals stay. `count` > 1 makes a linear array (copy k at k × vector, e.g. 10 columns every 6 m: vector {x: 6, y: 0}, count 10). Copies keep all settings, story, layer and properties of their original; copying a wall also copies its windows/doors. To duplicate onto other stories use copy_elements_to_stories. Returns {results: [{guid (original), copies: [new GUIDs]} | {guid, error}], createdCount, createdGroups? (GUIDs of the new groups holding the copies when whole groups were copied), additionalCreated?: [{guid, type, storyIndex}] (dependent elements created with the copies: openings of copied walls, copied group members, associative dimensions/labels), subElementsCreated? (curtain wall / stair / railing parts), warnings?}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `vector` | object | yes | Offset of the (first) copy from the original, in meters |
| `count` | integer |  | Number of copies (default 1). With count N, copy k is placed at k × the transformation (linear/polar array, like Archicad's Multiply) |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `rotate_elements`
