Rotates elements of ANY type in plan around a centre point (Edit > Move > Rotate). Angle in DEGREES, positive = counter-clockwise. Without `center` the centre of the elements' combined bounding box is used (reported in warnings). With copy=true the originals stay and `count` copies are made at k × angle (polar array, e.g. 6 chairs around a table: angle 60, count 5, copy true). Returns move-style results, or copy-style results ({results: [{guid, copies}], createdCount, additionalCreated?}) when copy=true.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `angle` | number | yes | Rotation angle in degrees; positive = counter-clockwise, negative = clockwise |
| `center` | object |  | Pivot point in meters (default: centre of the elements' bounding box) |
| `copy` | boolean |  | true = keep the originals and transform copies (default false = transform the originals) |
| `count` | integer |  | Only with copy=true: number of rotated copies, copy k rotated by k × angle (default 1) |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `mirror_elements`

Mirrors elements of ANY type across a line in plan (Edit > Move > Mirror). Give the mirror line either by two points (`axisStart`, `axisEnd`) or as `axis`: 'Vertical' | 'Horizontal' through `through` (default: centre of the elements' bounding box). copy=true keeps the originals and creates mirrored copies (e.g. the other half of a symmetric plan). Mirroring flips wall/door opening sides and object handedness like Archicad does. Returns {results: [{guid, newGuid?, warning?} | {guid, error}], warnings?} in input order (a 'warning' on an item means Archicad may have ignored it); with copy=true the copy-style result {results: [{guid, copies: [new GUIDs]}], createdCount, additionalCreated?}. Locked elements, elements on locked/hidden layers, hotlinked elements and elements outside your Teamwork workspace are reported with an actionable error instead of being edited.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `axisStart` | object |  | First point of the mirror line (with axisEnd) |
| `axisEnd` | any |  | Second point of the mirror line (with axisStart) |
| `axis` | "Vertical" \\| "Horizontal" |  | Alternative to axisStart/axisEnd: a vertical (parallel to Y) or horizontal (parallel to X) mirror line |
| `through` | any |  | Point the vertical/horizontal `axis` passes through (default: bounding-box centre) |
| `copy` | boolean |  | true = keep the originals and transform copies (default false = transform the originals) |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `elevate_elements`

Moves model elements vertically by `deltaZ` meters (Edit > Move > Elevate); positive = up. Heights/offsets relative to the home story change accordingly (e.g. a wall's bottom offset, an object's elevation); the home story does not change (use modify_elements storyIndex or copy_elements_to_stories for that). 2D elements (lines, fills, texts, labels, dimensions, drawings...) have no elevation and are reported as per-item errors. copy=true with `count` stacks copies at k × deltaZ. Returns {results: [{guid, newGuid?, warning?} | {guid, error}], warnings?} in input order (a 'warning' on an item means Archicad may have ignored it); with copy=true the copy-style result {results: [{guid, copies: [new GUIDs]}], createdCount, additionalCreated?}. Locked elements, elements on locked/hidden layers, hotlinked elements and elements outside your Teamwork workspace are reported with an actionable error instead of being edited.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `deltaZ` | number | yes | Vertical displacement in meters (positive = up) |
| `copy` | boolean |  | true = keep the originals and transform copies (default false = transform the originals) |
| `count` | integer |  | Only with copy=true: number of stacked copies (default 1) |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `resize_elements`

Scales elements in plan by `ratio` around a centre point (Edit > Reshape > Resize): 2 = double size, 0.5 = half. Default centre: the elements' bounding-box centre. Model element heights are not scaled; text/labels scale according to Archicad rules. Returns {results: [{guid, newGuid?, warning?} | {guid, error}], warnings?} in input order (a 'warning' on an item means Archicad may have ignored it); with copy=true the copy-style result {results: [{guid, copies: [new GUIDs]}], createdCount, additionalCreated?}. Locked elements, elements on locked/hidden layers, hotlinked elements and elements outside your Teamwork workspace are reported with an actionable error instead of being edited.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `ratio` | number | yes | Scale factor (> 0, not 1) |
| `center` | object |  | Fixed point of the scaling in meters (default: bounding-box centre) |
| `copy` | boolean |  | true = keep the originals and transform copies (default false = transform the originals) |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `delete_elements`

Deletes elements of ANY type (one undo step: undo restores them). Dependent elements are removed by Archicad too and are reported: windows/doors of deleted walls, associative dimensions and labels, etc. -> `alsoDeleted: [{guid, type}]`, `subElementsDeleted` (curtain wall / stair / railing parts). Only delete elements you created or were asked to delete. Group GUIDs delete the whole group. Returns {results: [{guid, deleted: true} | {guid, error}], deletedCount, alsoDeleted?, subElementsDeleted?, warnings?}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `copy_elements_to_stories`

Duplicates elements onto other stories at the same plan position, keeping their elevation RELATIVE to the home story (like copy + Paste to stories: a wall with bottom offset 0 on story 0 becomes a wall with bottom offset 0 on story 2). Top-linked walls/columns stay linked relative to their new home story. Copy walls/roofs/slabs rather than their windows/doors: hosted openings travel with their host. List stories with get_stories. Returns {results: [{guid (original), copies: [{storyIndex, guid} | {storyIndex, error}]}], additionalCreated?, subElementsCreated?, warnings?}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `stories` | integer \\| string \\| object[] | yes | Target stories: story indices (0 = ground floor) or story names |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `group_elements`

Groups elements (Edit > Grouping > Group) so they move/select together. Elements that already belong to a group bring their whole top-level group, which becomes a nested sub-group (same as Archicad). All elements must be on the same story. Pass `elements` for one group, or `groups: [{elements, parentGroup?}]` for several in one undo step. Returns {groupGuid, members, elementCount} (with `groups`: {results: [...]}). The group GUID can be passed to move_elements, delete_elements, ungroup_elements, ... to address the whole group.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] |  | At least two elements and/or group GUIDs (all on the same story) |
| `parentGroup` | any |  | Optional existing group GUID to nest the new group into |
| `groups` | object[] |  | Several groups to create in one undo step |
| `groups[].elements` | any | yes |  |
| `groups[].parentGroup` | any |  |  |

### `ungroup_elements`

Dissolves groups (Edit > Grouping > Ungroup). Pass group GUIDs, or any member element (its top-level group is dissolved). One level per call like Archicad: nested sub-groups survive unless completely=true. Returns {results: [{guid, dissolvedGroups: [guid], elementCount, remainingGroup?} | {guid, error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Group GUIDs or member element GUIDs |
| `completely` | boolean |  | true = also dissolve all nested sub-groups (default false: one level) |

### `set_suspend_groups`

Reads or switches Archicad's 'Suspend Groups' mode (Edit > Grouping). When ON, members of groups can be selected and edited individually. The edit tools of this connector already handle groups via includeGroupMembers, so this is only needed for manual workflows. Omit `suspend` to just read the state. Returns {suspendGroups, changed}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `suspend` | boolean |  | true = suspend groups (ON), false = groups active (OFF); omit to read |

### `lock_elements`

Locks elements (Edit > Locking > Lock): locked elements cannot be edited, moved or deleted until unlocked (unlock_elements). Returns {results: [{guid, locked: true} | {guid, error}], warnings?}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `unlock_elements`

Unlocks locked elements (Edit > Locking > Unlock). Call this when another tool reports 'is locked'. Elements on a LOCKED LAYER need the layer unlocked instead (layer attribute tools). Returns {results: [{guid, locked: false} | {guid, error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `set_draw_order`

Changes the 2D stacking (display) order of elements on the plan (Edit > Display Order): which element covers which (e.g. a fill behind lines). Give `action` ('BringToFront' | 'BringForward' | 'SendBackward' | 'SendToBack' | 'Reset' = default order of the type; `steps` repeats BringForward/SendBackward) OR a target `level` 1 (bottom) .. 14 (top). Type defaults: drawings 5, fills/zones 6, walls/slabs/roofs/columns/doors/windows 7, objects/lamps 8, lines/arcs/polylines/beams 9, texts/labels/dimensions 10. Windows/doors cannot go below their host. Returns {results: [{guid, drawIndexBefore, drawIndex, warning?}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to edit: GUID strings (or {guid}) of any element type, e.g. from get_element_details / element search tools. A group GUID stands for all elements of that group. |
| `action` | "BringToFront" \\| "BringForward" \\| "SendBackward" \\| "SendToBack" \\| "Reset" |  | Relative change (give this OR level) |
| `steps` | integer |  | Repeat BringForward/SendBackward this many levels (default 1) |
| `level` | integer |  | Absolute target drawing order level 1..14 (give this OR action) |
| `includeGroupMembers` | boolean |  | Default false: ONLY the listed elements are affected even when they belong to groups (Suspend Groups is switched on for the duration of the call and restored afterwards). true: all members of their (top-level) groups are affected as well. |

### `trim_elements`

Trims construction elements (walls, columns, beams, slabs, roofs, shells, curtain walls, windows/doors/skylights) to a Roof or Shell (Design > Connect > Trim Elements to Roof/Shell), e.g. cut gable walls to the roof slope. With `trimWith` (a roof/shell GUID) every element in `elements` is trimmed by it using `trimType`; without it the roofs/shells contained in `elements` trim the other elements (default: keep the part below/inside). Returns {results: [{guid, trimmedBy: [{guid, trimType}]} | {guid, error}]}; undo with remove_trim.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Elements to trim (and, without trimWith, the trimming roofs/shells) |
| `trimWith` | any |  | The Roof or Shell that trims the elements |
| `trimType` | "KeepInside" \\| "KeepOutside" \\| "KeepAll" |  | Needs trimWith. KeepInside (default) = keep the part inside/below the roof or shell (typical: walls under a roof); KeepOutside = keep the part outside/above; KeepAll = keep all parts |

### `remove_trim`

Removes trim-to-roof/shell connections, restoring the untrimmed elements. Give explicit `pairs` [{element, trimmingElement}] and/or `elements` (all trim connections of these elements are removed, whether they are trimmed or trimming). Inspect trims with get_element_edit_relations. Returns {results: [{element, trimmingElement, removed: true} | {element, trimmingElement, error}], removedCount}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pairs` | object[] |  |  |
| `pairs[].element` | string \\| object | yes | Trimmed element |
| `pairs[].element.guid` | any | yes |  |
| `pairs[].trimmingElement` | any | yes | Roof/shell that trims it |
| `elements` | any[] |  | Remove every trim connection of these elements |

### `merge_elements`

Merges construction elements (Design > Connect > Merge) so their 3D bodies and sections are displayed as one without contour lines between them (e.g. slabs of the same material). Needs at least two elements. Undo with unmerge_elements. Returns {merged, results: [{guid, mergedWith: [guid]}]} (merged=false with per-element errors when some element is not editable).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | At least two construction elements to merge |

### `unmerge_elements`

Removes merge connections created by merge_elements. Give `pairs` [{element, otherElement}] and/or `elements` (all merges of these). Returns {results: [{element, otherElement, removed: true} | {..., error}], removedCount}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pairs` | object[] |  |  |
| `pairs[].element` | string \\| object | yes | Element GUID string, or {"guid": "..."} |
| `pairs[].element.guid` | any | yes |  |
| `pairs[].otherElement` | any | yes |  |
| `elements` | any[] |  | Remove every merge connection of these elements |

### `solid_operation`

Solid Element Operations (Design > Solid Element Operations): cut or add 3D volumes between construction elements - e.g. subtract a morph/slab/object from walls to make a niche, cut walls with SubtractUpwards/SubtractDownwards against a roof or slab. By default (permanent=false) a LIVE link is created: it updates when elements move, operators are usually put on a hidden layer afterwards, and it can be removed with remove_solid_operation. permanent=true performs a destructive Boolean on MORPHS only: target and operators are replaced by the result morph(s). Give target/operators/operation for one operation or `operations` for several (one undo step). Returns {results: [{target, links: [{operator, operation} | {operator, error}]}]} or, when permanent, [{target, resultMorphs: [guid]}].

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `target` | string \\| object |  | Target element that is cut/added to (keeps its identity) |
| `target.guid` | any | yes |  |
| `operators` | any[] |  | Operator element(s) that cut/add to the target |
| `operation` | "Subtract" \\| "SubtractUpwards" \\| "SubtractDownwards" \\| "Intersect" \\| "Add" |  | Subtract (default: remove the operator's volume from the target), SubtractUpwards / SubtractDownwards (remove the operator's volume extruded upwards/downwards, e.g. cut a wall under a roof), Intersect (keep only the common part), Add (union) |
| `inheritOperatorAttributes` | boolean |  | New cut surfaces take the operator's surface/attributes (default false) |
| `skipOperatorHoles` | boolean |  | Ignore holes of slab/roof operators (default false) |
| `operations` | object[] |  | Several operations [{target, operators, operation?, ...}] |
| `operations[].target` | any | yes |  |
| `operations[].operators` | any | yes |  |
| `operations[].operation` | any |  |  |
| `operations[].inheritOperatorAttributes` | any |  |  |
| `operations[].skipOperatorHoles` | any |  |  |
| `permanent` | boolean |  | true = destructive morph Boolean (morphs only, inputs replaced); default false = live link |

### `remove_solid_operation`

Removes live solid element operation links (created by solid_operation or in Archicad). Give `links` [{target, operator}] and/or `elements` (every link where the element is target or operator). Inspect links with get_element_edit_relations. Returns {results: [{target, operator, removed: true} | {target, operator, error}], removedCount}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `links` | object[] |  |  |
| `links[].target` | string \\| object | yes | Element GUID string, or {"guid": "..."} |
| `links[].target.guid` | any | yes |  |
| `links[].operator` | any | yes |  |
| `elements` | any[] |  | Remove every solid operation link of these elements |

### `get_element_edit_relations`

Read-only. For each element returns what affects editing it: type, storyIndex, locked, editable, drawIndex, group membership (groupGuid, rootGroupGuid, groupElements), hotlinkGuid, trimmedBy / trims (trim-to-roof connections with trimType), mergedWith, solidOperators (elements cutting/adding to it) and solidTargets (elements it cuts). Use it to verify group/trim/merge/solid operations or to find out why an edit was refused.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |


## columns-beams

### `create_columns`

Creates columns (one undo step): rectangular, circular, complex-profile, tapered, slanted and multi-segment columns, with veneer, wall wrapping and surface overrides. Coordinates in meters, angles in degrees, on the given story (default: current story). Unspecified settings come from the Column tool defaults — give 'height' (unlinked) or 'topLinkedStory' (+ topOffset) so the height is what you expect. Typical: {origin: {x: 0, y: 0}, height: 3, width: 0.4, depth: 0.4, buildingMaterial: '<name>'}. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}] in input order; read the result back with get_element_details (segments, cuts, elevations).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `columns` | object[] | yes | Columns to create |
| `columns[].origin` | object | yes | Column position on the plan (m): the point where the section's anchor point sits (default anchor Center = the column axis). get_element_details returns this same 'origin' plus 'center' (the section centre) |
| `columns[].height` | number |  | Column height in m. Giving a height unlinks the top unless topLinkedStory is also given (tool defaults are often top-linked) |
| `columns[].bottomOffset` | number |  | Base elevation relative to the home story level (m, e.g. -0.3 to start below the floor) |
| `columns[].topLinkedStory` | integer |  | Link the top to the story N levels above the home story (1 = next story; 0 = unlinked, use height) |
| `columns[].topOffset` | number |  | Offset of the top from the linked story (m, e.g. -0.25 to stop under the slab) |
| `columns[].rotationAngle` | number |  | Rotation of the column around its own axis on the plan, degrees counter-clockwise |
| `columns[].slanted` | boolean |  | Slanted column (tilted axis). Usually set implicitly by slantAngle |
| `columns[].slantAngle` | number |  | Slant angle of the axis in degrees measured from the horizontal plane as in the Column Settings dialog (90 = vertical). Other values make the column slanted |
| `columns[].slantDirection` | number |  | Direction the slanted column leans towards on the plan, degrees counter-clockwise from the x axis |
| `columns[].flipped` | boolean |  | Mirror the column (relevant for asymmetric complex profiles) |
| `columns[].anchor` | "TopLeft" \\| "TopCenter" \\| "TopRight" \\| "MiddleLeft" \\| "Center" \\| "MiddleRight" \\| "BottomLeft" \\| "BottomCenter" \\| "BottomRight" \\| integer |  | Which point of the section sits on 'origin': 3x3 grid in the column's local axes (before rotationAngle; Top = +y side, Left = -x side): 0 TopLeft, 1 TopCenter, 2 TopRight, 3 MiddleLeft, 4 Center (default), 5 MiddleRight, 6 BottomLeft, 7 BottomCenter, 8 BottomRight. E.g. BottomLeft puts the section's min-x/min-y corner on origin. On modify without 'origin' the column body stays in place |
| `columns[].wrapping` | boolean |  | Wall wrapping: walls meeting the column wrap around it (skins continue around the column) |
| `columns[].zoneRelation` | "Boundary" \\| "ReduceArea" \\| "None" |  | Relation to zones: zone boundary, reduce zone area, or no effect |
| `columns[].floorPlanSymbol` | "Plain" \\| "Slash" \\| "X" \\| "Crosshair" |  | Core symbol shown on the floor plan |
| `columns[].floorPlanDisplay` | "Projected" \\| "ProjectedWithOverhead" \\| "CutOnly" \\| "OutlinesOnly" \\| "OverheadAll" \\| "SymbolicCut" |  | Floor plan display option (Floor Plan and Section > Show on Floor Plan) |
| `columns[].viewDepthLimitation` | "ToFloorPlanRange" \\| "ToAbsoluteLimit" \\| "EntireElement" |  | How far the element is shown on the floor plan (Show Projection) |
| `columns[].showOnStories` | "Auto" \\| "HomeOnly" \\| "AllStories" \\| object |  | Floor plan story visibility: 'Auto' (Archicad shows it on every story it physically reaches), 'HomeOnly', 'AllStories', or an explicit {homeStory, allAbove, allBelow, storiesAbove, storiesBelow} |
| `columns[].showOnStories.homeStory` | boolean |  | Show on the home story |
| `columns[].showOnStories.allAbove` | boolean |  | Show on all stories above |
| `columns[].showOnStories.allBelow` | boolean |  | Show on all stories below |
| `columns[].showOnStories.storiesAbove` | integer |  | Show on this many stories above the home story |
| `columns[].showOnStories.storiesBelow` | integer |  | Show on this many stories below the home story |
| `columns[].cutFillPen` | integer \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (1-255); false = use the pen of the building material/profile |
| `columns[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `columns[].coverFill` | boolean \\| object |  | Floor plan cover fill of the uncut (projected) part; true/false toggles it |
| `columns[].coverFill.enabled` | boolean |  | Show the cover fill (default true when fill is given) |
| `columns[].coverFill.fill` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Fill type name/index (localized, list with get_attributes type Fill) |
| `columns[].coverFill.pen` | any |  | Fill foreground pen |
| `columns[].coverFill.backgroundPen` | any |  | Fill background pen |
| `columns[].coverFill.fromSurface` | boolean |  | Use the fill of the element's surface instead of 'fill' |
| `columns[].coverFill.orientationFrom3D` | boolean |  | Orient the fill as in the 3D texture |
| `columns[].lines` | object |  | Floor plan line pens / line types |
| `columns[].lines.contour` | object |  | Core contour lines (cut) |
| `columns[].lines.uncut` | any |  | Uncut (below/projected) lines |
| `columns[].lines.overhead` | any |  | Overhead lines |
| `columns[].lines.hidden` | any |  | Hidden lines |
| `columns[].lines.veneer` | any |  | Veneer contour lines |
| `columns[].lines.symbol` | object |  | Core symbol (slash/X/crosshair) pen |
| `columns[].shape` | "Rectangular" \\| "Circular" |  | Cross-section shape (default: Column tool setting). width/depth imply Rectangular, diameter implies Circular |
| `columns[].width` | number |  | Rectangular section size along the column's local x axis (m, before rotationAngle) |
| `columns[].depth` | number |  | Rectangular section size along the column's local y axis (m) |
| `columns[].diameter` | number |  | Circular section diameter (m); makes the section circular |
| `columns[].tapered` | boolean |  | Tapered segment: the section changes linearly from bottom to top (give endWidth/endDepth or endDiameter) |
| `columns[].endWidth` | number |  | Tapered: width at the top of the segment (m) |
| `columns[].endDepth` | number |  | Tapered: depth at the top of the segment (m) |
| `columns[].endDiameter` | number |  | Tapered circular: diameter at the top of the segment (m) |
| `columns[].buildingMaterial` | any |  | Basic structure: building material name/index (localized, list with get_attributes type BuildingMaterial). Composites are not possible for columns/beams |
| `columns[].profile` | any |  | Complex profile structure: profile attribute name/index (the profile must be enabled for this element type; list with get_attributes type Profile). Makes the section follow the profile; give buildingMaterial instead to go back to a basic section |
| `columns[].surface` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the surface of all faces (sides and ends); false removes the overrides |
| `columns[].sideSurface` | any |  | Override the surface of the column sides (extrusion faces) |
| `columns[].endsSurface` | any |  | Override the surface of the top and bottom faces |
| `columns[].surfacesChained` | boolean |  | Chain side and end surfaces (one surface for all). Giving differing sideSurface/endsSurface unchains automatically |
| `columns[].veneerType` | "Core" \\| "Finish" \\| "Other" |  | Veneer (cover) skin type |
| `columns[].veneerThickness` | number |  | Veneer thickness in m around the core (0 = no veneer) |
| `columns[].veneerBuildingMaterial` | any |  | Building material of the veneer |
| `columns[].segments` | object[] |  | Segments from bottom to top (multi-segment columns). Setting a different number of items changes the number of segments (new segments copy the last one). Use {} for a segment you do not want to change. Each item accepts the section/surface fields of this tool plus length \| lengthProportion; they override the top-level section fields for that segment. Proportional lengths are normalized so they add up to 1. |
| `columns[].segments[].shape` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].width` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].depth` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].diameter` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].tapered` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].endWidth` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].endDepth` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].endDiameter` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].buildingMaterial` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].profile` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].surface` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].sideSurface` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].endsSurface` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].surfacesChained` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].veneerType` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].veneerThickness` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].veneerBuildingMaterial` | any |  |  |
| `columns[].segments[].length` | number |  | Fixed segment length in m (measured along the element axis) |
| `columns[].segments[].lengthProportion` | number |  | Proportional segment: relative share of the length left after the fixed segments (weights, normalized to add up to 1) |
| `columns[].cuts` | object[] |  | Segment cuts, exactly segmentCount + 1 items: [start cut, cut between segment 0 and 1, ..., end cut] (read the current ones with get_element_details) — for columns: bottom cut, inner cuts, top cut |
| `columns[].cuts[].type` | "Horizontal" \\| "Vertical" \\| "Custom" |  | Cut plane type. Keep the tool default (columns normally Horizontal) unless you need a Custom angle |
| `columns[].cuts[].angle` | number |  | Custom cut angle in degrees (setting it switches the cut to Custom) |
| `columns[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `columns[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `columns[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `columns[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `columns[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `create_beams`

Creates beams (one undo step): straight, horizontally or vertically curved, slanted, tapered, complex-profile and multi-segment beams, with holes and surface overrides. Coordinates in meters, angles in degrees, on the given story (default: current story). 'level' is the height of the reference axis (by default the beam top) above the home story — e.g. story height 3 m and a beam under the slab: level 2.8. Unspecified settings come from the Beam tool defaults. Chain beams end-to-start so Archicad connects them. Typical: {begin: {x: 0, y: 0}, end: {x: 6, y: 0}, level: 3, width: 0.3, height: 0.5}. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}] in input order.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `beams` | object[] | yes | Beams to create |
| `beams[].begin` | object | yes | Start point of the beam reference axis on the plan (m) |
| `beams[].end` | object | yes | End point of the beam reference axis on the plan (m) |
| `beams[].level` | number |  | Height of the beam reference axis above the home story level (m) — Archicad's 'Offset to Home Story'; with the default anchor this is the TOP of the beam |
| `beams[].offset` | number |  | Horizontal offset of the beam body from its reference axis (m; sign = side) |
| `beams[].anchor` | "TopLeft" \\| "TopCenter" \\| "TopRight" \\| "MiddleLeft" \\| "Center" \\| "MiddleRight" \\| "BottomLeft" \\| "BottomCenter" \\| "BottomRight" \\| integer |  | Which point of the cross-section the reference axis passes through: 3x3 grid, 0 TopLeft, 1 TopCenter, 2 TopRight, 3 MiddleLeft, 4 Center, 5 MiddleRight, 6 BottomLeft, 7 BottomCenter, 8 BottomRight (left = left looking from begin to end) |
| `beams[].arcAngle` | number |  | Horizontally curved beam: central angle of the arc from begin to end in degrees (positive = counter-clockwise; 0 = straight) |
| `beams[].verticalCurveHeight` | number |  | Vertically curved (arched) beam: rise of the arc above the chord in m (0 = straight) |
| `beams[].beamShape` | "Straight" \\| "HorizontallyCurved" \\| "VerticallyCurved" |  | Beam geometry type; usually derived from arcAngle / verticalCurveHeight |
| `beams[].slantAngle` | number |  | Slanted beam: angle of the axis from the horizontal in degrees (positive rises from begin to end; 0 = horizontal) |
| `beams[].profileRotationAngle` | number |  | Rotation (twist) of the cross-section around the beam axis in degrees |
| `beams[].flipped` | boolean |  | Mirror the beam cross-section (profiled beams only) |
| `beams[].sequence` | integer |  | Intersection priority sequence (0-999) used when beams meet in a junction |
| `beams[].showContourLines` | "Always" \\| "Never" \\| "ByModelViewOptions" |  | Show the beam contour lines on the floor plan |
| `beams[].showReferenceAxis` | "Always" \\| "Never" \\| "ByModelViewOptions" |  | Show the beam reference axis on the floor plan |
| `beams[].floorPlanDisplay` | "Projected" \\| "ProjectedWithOverhead" \\| "CutOnly" \\| "OutlinesOnly" \\| "OverheadAll" \\| "SymbolicCut" |  | Floor plan display option (Floor Plan and Section > Show on Floor Plan) |
| `beams[].viewDepthLimitation` | "ToFloorPlanRange" \\| "ToAbsoluteLimit" \\| "EntireElement" |  | How far the element is shown on the floor plan (Show Projection) |
| `beams[].showOnStories` | "Auto" \\| "HomeOnly" \\| "AllStories" \\| object |  | Floor plan story visibility: 'Auto' (Archicad shows it on every story it physically reaches), 'HomeOnly', 'AllStories', or an explicit {homeStory, allAbove, allBelow, storiesAbove, storiesBelow} |
| `beams[].showOnStories.homeStory` | boolean |  | Show on the home story |
| `beams[].showOnStories.allAbove` | boolean |  | Show on all stories above |
| `beams[].showOnStories.allBelow` | boolean |  | Show on all stories below |
| `beams[].showOnStories.storiesAbove` | integer |  | Show on this many stories above the home story |
| `beams[].showOnStories.storiesBelow` | integer |  | Show on this many stories below the home story |
| `beams[].cutFillPen` | integer \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (1-255); false = use the pen of the building material/profile |
| `beams[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `beams[].coverFill` | boolean \\| object |  | Floor plan cover fill of the uncut (projected) part; true/false toggles it |
| `beams[].coverFill.enabled` | boolean |  | Show the cover fill (default true when fill is given) |
| `beams[].coverFill.fill` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Fill type name/index (localized, list with get_attributes type Fill) |
| `beams[].coverFill.pen` | any |  | Fill foreground pen |
| `beams[].coverFill.backgroundPen` | any |  | Fill background pen |
| `beams[].coverFill.fromSurface` | boolean |  | Use the fill of the element's surface instead of 'fill' |
| `beams[].coverFill.orientationFrom3D` | boolean |  | Orient the fill as in the 3D texture |
| `beams[].lines` | object |  | Floor plan line pens / line types |
| `beams[].lines.reference` | object |  | Reference axis line |
| `beams[].lines.cutContour` | any |  | Cut contour lines |
| `beams[].lines.uncut` | any |  | Uncut (below/projected) lines |
| `beams[].lines.overhead` | any |  | Overhead lines |
| `beams[].lines.hidden` | any |  | Hidden lines |
| `beams[].shape` | "Rectangular" \\| "Circular" |  | Cross-section shape (default: Beam tool setting). width/height imply Rectangular, diameter implies Circular |
| `beams[].width` | number |  | Section width in m (horizontal, across the beam) |
| `beams[].height` | number |  | Section height in m (vertical size of the beam cross-section) |
| `beams[].diameter` | number |  | Circular section diameter (m); makes the section circular |
| `beams[].tapered` | boolean |  | Tapered segment: the section changes linearly from start to end (give endWidth/endHeight or endDiameter) |
| `beams[].endWidth` | number |  | Tapered: width at the end of the segment (m) |
| `beams[].endHeight` | number |  | Tapered: height at the end of the segment (m) |
| `beams[].endDiameter` | number |  | Tapered circular: diameter at the end of the segment (m) |
| `beams[].buildingMaterial` | any |  | Basic structure: building material name/index (localized, list with get_attributes type BuildingMaterial). Composites are not possible for columns/beams |
| `beams[].profile` | any |  | Complex profile structure: profile attribute name/index (the profile must be enabled for this element type; list with get_attributes type Profile). Makes the section follow the profile; give buildingMaterial instead to go back to a basic section |
| `beams[].surface` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the surface of every face (left, right, top, bottom, ends); false removes the overrides |
| `beams[].leftSurface` | any |  | Override the left side surface (left looking from begin to end) |
| `beams[].rightSurface` | any |  | Override the right side surface |
| `beams[].topSurface` | any |  | Override the top face surface |
| `beams[].bottomSurface` | any |  | Override the bottom face surface |
| `beams[].endsSurface` | any |  | Override the surface of both end faces |
| `beams[].surfacesChained` | boolean |  | Chain all face surfaces (one surface for all). Giving differing individual face surfaces unchains automatically |
| `beams[].segments` | object[] |  | Segments from begin to end (multi-segment beams). Setting a different number of items changes the number of segments (new segments copy the last one). Use {} for a segment you do not want to change. Each item accepts the section/surface fields of this tool plus length \| lengthProportion; they override the top-level section fields for that segment. Proportional lengths are normalized so they add up to 1. |
| `beams[].segments[].shape` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].width` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].height` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].diameter` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].tapered` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].endWidth` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].endHeight` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].endDiameter` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].buildingMaterial` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].profile` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].surface` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].leftSurface` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].rightSurface` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].topSurface` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].bottomSurface` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].endsSurface` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].surfacesChained` | any |  |  |
| `beams[].segments[].length` | number |  | Fixed segment length in m (measured along the element axis) |
| `beams[].segments[].lengthProportion` | number |  | Proportional segment: relative share of the length left after the fixed segments (weights, normalized to add up to 1) |
| `beams[].cuts` | object[] |  | Segment cuts, exactly segmentCount + 1 items: [start cut, cut between segment 0 and 1, ..., end cut] (read the current ones with get_element_details) — for beams: begin cut, inner cuts, end cut |
| `beams[].cuts[].type` | "Horizontal" \\| "Vertical" \\| "Custom" |  | Cut plane type. Keep the tool default (columns normally Horizontal) unless you need a Custom angle |
| `beams[].cuts[].angle` | number |  | Custom cut angle in degrees (setting it switches the cut to Custom) |
| `beams[].holes` | object[] |  | Holes in the beam. Replaces ALL existing holes ([] removes them); hole ids are renumbered 1..n |
| `beams[].holes[].shape` | "Rectangular" \\| "Circular" |  | Hole shape (default: Beam tool hole setting; diameter implies Circular) |
| `beams[].holes[].distanceFromBegin` | number | yes | Position of the hole centre along the beam axis, measured from the begin point (m) |
| `beams[].holes[].depthBelowTop` | number |  | Vertical position of the hole centre: distance below the top of the beam (m), as the 'Position' in Beam hole settings. Default: the tool's hole level |
| `beams[].holes[].width` | number |  | Hole width along the beam (m); for circular holes the diameter |
| `beams[].holes[].height` | number |  | Rectangular hole height (m) |
| `beams[].holes[].diameter` | number |  | Circular hole diameter (m) |
| `beams[].holes[].showContour` | boolean |  | Show the hole contour on the floor plan |
| `beams[].addHoles` | any[] |  | Holes to add to the existing ones (modify) / to the default ones (create) |
| `beams[].removeHoles` | integer[] |  | Ids of holes to remove (the 'id' values returned by get_element_details) |
| `beams[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `beams[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `beams[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `beams[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `beams[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `modify_columns`

Changes existing columns (one undo step). Each item is {guid, ...fields to change} using exactly the fields of create_columns (origin, height, topLinkedStory, width/depth/diameter, buildingMaterial/profile, surfaces, veneer, slant, anchor, lines, ...); only the given fields change and they are validated like create_columns. Top-level section/surface/veneer fields apply to ALL segments; use 'segments' (full list, {} = unchanged segment; a different length changes the segment count) for single segments and 'cuts' for segment cuts. Read the current values first with get_element_details. Non-column GUIDs are rejected per item. Returns [{guid} | {error}] in input order.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `columns` | object[] | yes | Patches: {guid, <create_columns field>: newValue, ...} |
| `columns[].guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `modify_beams`

Changes existing beams (one undo step). Each item is {guid, ...fields to change} using exactly the fields of create_beams (begin, end, level, width/height/diameter, arcAngle, slantAngle, buildingMaterial/profile, surfaces, anchor, lines, ...); only the given fields change and they are validated like create_beams. Top-level section/surface fields apply to ALL segments; use 'segments' (full list, {} = unchanged segment) for single segments or the segment count. Holes: 'holes' replaces all, 'addHoles' appends, 'removeHoles' deletes by id. Read the current values first with get_element_details. Non-beam GUIDs are rejected per item. Returns [{guid} | {error}] in input order.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `beams` | object[] | yes | Patches: {guid, <create_beams field>: newValue, ...} |
| `beams[].guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |


## slabs-roofs

### `create_slabs`

Creates slabs (floors, ceilings, flat roofs, terraces) from a plan polygon with optional curved edges (arcs) and holes. 'level' is the elevation of the slab's reference plane above the home story — pass referencePlane: 'Top' to make level = top of the slab (the tool default may differ; get_element_details shows referencePlane and offsetFromTop), 'thickness' in m, structure by 'buildingMaterial' or 'composite'. Edge trims (Vertical or CustomAngle with edgeAngle) for all edges or per edge via 'edges', surface overrides, floor plan pens/fills. Example: {polygon: [{x:0,y:0},{x:8,y:0},{x:8,y:6},{x:0,y:6}], thickness: 0.25, level: 0}. A self-intersecting polygon is regularized automatically when it stays one piece. Units: meters and degrees; coordinates are project coordinates on the home story (default: current story; 'storyIndex' to choose). Unspecified settings come from the tool defaults. Attribute names (building materials, composites, surfaces, fills, line types, layers) are LOCALIZED — look them up with get_attributes. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}] in input order (one undo step; a failing item does not stop the others). Read results back with get_element_details (same field names).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `slabs` | object[] | yes | Slabs to create |
| `slabs[].polygon` | object[] \\| object | yes | Slab outline in plan (m, project coordinates): array of points, or {points, arcs?, holes?}. Do not repeat the first point. Holes must lie inside the outline |
| `slabs[].polygon.points` | any[] | yes | Vertices in order, do NOT repeat the first point at the end |
| `slabs[].polygon.arcs` | object[] |  | Curved edges |
| `slabs[].polygon.holes` | object[] |  | Inner holes, each {points, arcs?} |
| `slabs[].thickness` | number |  | Thickness in m (ignored for composite slabs: the composite defines it) |
| `slabs[].level` | number |  | Elevation of the reference plane relative to the home story level (m). With referencePlane 'Top' (usual) this is the slab TOP; e.g. 0 = top at story level |
| `slabs[].referencePlane` | "Top" \\| "CoreTop" \\| "CoreBottom" \\| "Bottom" |  | Which plane of the slab 'level' refers to |
| `slabs[].buildingMaterial` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Basic structure: building material name/index (localized — get_attributes type 'BuildingMaterial') |
| `slabs[].buildingMaterial.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `slabs[].composite` | any |  | Composite structure: composite name/index (get_attributes type 'Composite'; its 'usage' must include this element type, e.g. usage.slabs for slabs); the composite defines the layers and total thickness |
| `slabs[].topSurface` | any \\| boolean |  | Top surface override (surface name/index, false = use the structure's surface) |
| `slabs[].bottomSurface` | any |  | Bottom surface override |
| `slabs[].sideSurface` | any |  | Edge/side surface override (applies to all edges; per-edge surfaces via 'edges') |
| `slabs[].surfacesChained` | boolean |  | Chain the surface overrides (Archicad UI chain icon) |
| `slabs[].edgeTrim` | "Vertical" \\| "Perpendicular" \\| "CustomAngle" |  | Trim of ALL slab edges (per-edge overrides via 'edges'): Vertical, Perpendicular or CustomAngle (+ edgeAngle) |
| `slabs[].edgeAngle` | number |  | Custom edge angle in degrees (0-180 exclusive, 90 = vertical edge); implies edgeTrim 'CustomAngle' |
| `slabs[].edges` | object[] |  | Per-edge trim / surface overrides |
| `slabs[].edges[].contour` | integer |  | 0 = outline (default), 1..N = holes in the order given |
| `slabs[].edges[].index` | integer | yes | Edge index: edge i runs from point i to point i+1 (the last edge closes the contour) |
| `slabs[].edges[].trim` | any |  | Slab edge trim: Vertical (default), Perpendicular (same as Vertical on a flat slab) or CustomAngle (with an angle). Horizontal/AlignWithCut exist only for roofs and shells |
| `slabs[].edges[].angle` | any |  | Custom edge angle in degrees (0-180 exclusive, 90 = vertical edge); implies edgeTrim 'CustomAngle' |
| `slabs[].edges[].surface` | any |  | Surface of this edge (false = structure's surface) |
| `slabs[].contourPen` | integer |  | Pen of the uncut contour on the floor plan |
| `slabs[].contourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the uncut contour (get_attributes type 'Line') |
| `slabs[].cutContourPen` | any |  | Pen of the cut contour |
| `slabs[].cutFillPen` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `slabs[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `slabs[].showCoverFill` | boolean |  | Show the floor plan cover fill |
| `slabs[].coverFill` | any |  | Cover fill type name/index (get_attributes type 'Fill') |
| `slabs[].coverFillPen` | any |  | Cover fill foreground pen |
| `slabs[].coverFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Cover fill background pen |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility` | object |  | Floor plan story visibility of the contour and cover fill (only the given flags change) |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.onHomeStory` | boolean |  | Show on the home story |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.allAbove` | boolean |  | Show on all stories above the home story |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.allBelow` | boolean |  | Show on all stories below the home story |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.storiesAbove` | integer |  | Show on this many stories above the home story |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.storiesBelow` | integer |  | Show on this many stories below the home story |
| `slabs[].cutContourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the cut contour |
| `slabs[].hiddenContourPen` | any |  | Pen of hidden contour lines |
| `slabs[].hiddenContourLineType` | any |  | Line type of hidden contour lines |
| `slabs[].coverFillFromSurface` | boolean |  | Use the vectorial 3D hatch of the top surface as cover fill |
| `slabs[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `slabs[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `slabs[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `slabs[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `slabs[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `create_roofs`

Creates roofs. SinglePlane: one sloped plane — 'polygon' (roof outline in plan incl. overhang), 'pivotLine' {begin, end} (the horizontal line at elevation 'level' the plane pivots around, usually the eaves), 'slopeAngle' (degrees); the plane rises towards the polygon unless risesToLeft is given. Two single-plane roofs with opposite pivot lines make a gable roof. MultiPlane: a hip roof over any closed 'pivotPolygon' (usually the outer wall outline at the wall top: level = wall height) with 'slopeAngle' or 'levels' (pitch breaks, e.g. mansard), 'eavesOverhang', and per-plane 'pivotEdges' overrides (gable: true turns that side into a vertical gable end, angle changes its pitch). 'thickness', 'buildingMaterial'/'composite', surfaces and floor plan attributes as for slabs. Example hip roof: {pivotPolygon: [{x:0,y:0},{x:10,y:0},{x:10,y:8},{x:0,y:8}], level: 3, slopeAngle: 30, eavesOverhang: 0.5, thickness: 0.3}. Units: meters and degrees; coordinates are project coordinates on the home story (default: current story; 'storyIndex' to choose). Unspecified settings come from the tool defaults. Attribute names (building materials, composites, surfaces, fills, line types, layers) are LOCALIZED — look them up with get_attributes. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}] in input order (one undo step; a failing item does not stop the others). Read results back with get_element_details (same field names).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `roofs` | object[] | yes | Roofs to create |
| `roofs[].roofClass` | "SinglePlane" \\| "MultiPlane" |  | SinglePlane (polygon + pivotLine + slopeAngle) or MultiPlane (pivotPolygon + slopeAngle/levels). Default: MultiPlane when pivotPolygon is given |
| `roofs[].polygon` | object[] \\| object |  | SinglePlane: roof outline projected to plan (m) incl. overhangs: array of points or {points, arcs?, holes?} |
| `roofs[].polygon.points` | any[] | yes | Vertices in order, do NOT repeat the first point at the end |
| `roofs[].polygon.arcs` | object[] |  | Curved edges |
| `roofs[].polygon.holes` | object[] |  | Inner holes, each {points, arcs?} |
| `roofs[].pivotPolygon` | any |  | MultiPlane: closed pivot polygon (usually the outer face of the walls at 'level'); every edge gets a roof plane. {points, arcs?, holes?} |
| `roofs[].level` | number |  | Elevation of the pivot line / pivot polygon relative to the home story level (m), e.g. the wall top height |
| `roofs[].slopeAngle` | number |  | Roof pitch in degrees (0-90). Multi-plane: sets a single roof level with this pitch |
| `roofs[].pivotLine` | object |  | SinglePlane: horizontal line the plane pivots around (usually the eaves line), at elevation 'level' |
| `roofs[].pivotLine.begin` | object | yes | 2D point in meters (project coordinates) |
| `roofs[].pivotLine.end` | any | yes |  |
| `roofs[].risesToLeft` | boolean |  | SinglePlane: true = the plane rises on the left side of pivotLine begin→end. Default on create: towards the polygon |
| `roofs[].edges` | object[] |  | SinglePlane: per-edge trim / surface / edge type overrides of the roof outline |
| `roofs[].edges[].contour` | integer |  | 0 = outline (default), 1..N = holes in the order given |
| `roofs[].edges[].index` | integer | yes | Edge index: edge i runs from point i to point i+1 (the last edge closes the contour) |
| `roofs[].edges[].trim` | "Vertical" \\| "Perpendicular" \\| "Horizontal" \\| "CustomAngle" \\| "AlignWithCut" |  | Edge trim: Vertical, Perpendicular (to the element plane), Horizontal, CustomAngle (with an angle), AlignWithCut |
| `roofs[].edges[].angle` | number |  | Custom edge angle in degrees (0-180 exclusive, 90 = vertical edge); implies edgeTrim 'CustomAngle' |
| `roofs[].edges[].surface` | integer \\| string \\| object \\| boolean |  | Surface of this edge (false = structure's surface) |
| `roofs[].edges[].edgeType` | "Undefined" \\| "Ridge" \\| "Valley" \\| "Gable" \\| "Hip" \\| "Eaves" \\| "Peak" \\| "SideWall" \\| "EndWall" \\| "Dome" \\| "Hollow" |  | Roof edge type (used by roof accessories/schedules) |
| `roofs[].levels` | object[] |  | MultiPlane: roof levels from the pivot polygon upwards, e.g. mansard [{angle: 70, height: 2}, {angle: 25}] = 70° up to 2 m above the pivot polygon, then 25° to the ridge |
| `roofs[].levels[].angle` | number | yes | Slope of this roof level in degrees |
| `roofs[].levels[].height` | number |  | Height above the pivot polygon (m) where this level ENDS and the next level begins (the pitch break). Required for every level except the last; the top level always continues to the ridge. Strictly increasing |
| `roofs[].eavesOverhang` | number |  | MultiPlane: eaves overhang beyond the pivot polygon (m); the roof contour is the pivot polygon offset by this |
| `roofs[].arcSegments` | integer |  | MultiPlane: segments per curved pivot edge (resolution by arc) |
| `roofs[].circleSegments` | integer |  | MultiPlane: segments per full circle (resolution by circle) |
| `roofs[].fitSkylightsToCurve` | boolean |  | MultiPlane: place skylights tangentially on curved planes |
| `roofs[].pivotEdges` | object[] |  | MultiPlane: per-plane overrides (slope, gable end, overhang) by pivot polygon edge |
| `roofs[].pivotEdges[].contour` | integer |  | 0 = pivot polygon outline (default), 1..N = holes |
| `roofs[].pivotEdges[].index` | integer | yes | Pivot edge index: edge i runs from pivotPolygon point i to point i+1 |
| `roofs[].pivotEdges[].angle` | number |  | Slope of this roof plane in degrees (all levels) |
| `roofs[].pivotEdges[].angles` | number[] |  | Slope per roof level (index 0 = lowest level) |
| `roofs[].pivotEdges[].gable` | boolean |  | true = vertical gable end on this edge (no roof plane), false = sloped plane |
| `roofs[].pivotEdges[].eavesOverhang` | number |  | Eaves overhang of this plane beyond the pivot edge (m) |
| `roofs[].thickness` | number |  | Thickness in m (ignored for composite structures) |
| `roofs[].buildingMaterial` | any |  | Basic structure: building material name/index (localized — get_attributes type 'BuildingMaterial') |
| `roofs[].composite` | any |  | Composite structure: composite name/index (get_attributes type 'Composite'; its 'usage' must include this element type, e.g. usage.slabs for slabs); the composite defines the layers and total thickness |
| `roofs[].topSurface` | any |  | Top surface override (surface name/index, false = use the structure's surface) |
| `roofs[].bottomSurface` | any |  | Bottom surface override |
| `roofs[].sideSurface` | any |  | Edge/side surface override (applies to all edges; per-edge surfaces via 'edges') |
| `roofs[].surfacesChained` | boolean |  | Chain the surface overrides (Archicad UI chain icon) |
| `roofs[].edgeTrim` | any |  | Default trim of the edges |
| `roofs[].edgeAngle` | any |  | Custom edge angle in degrees (0-180 exclusive, 90 = vertical edge); implies edgeTrim 'CustomAngle' |
| `roofs[].connectionBody` | "Editable" \\| "ContoursDown" \\| "PivotLinesDown" \\| "UpwardsExtrusion" \\| "DownwardsExtrusion" |  | Body extension used for connections/trims (Archicad 'Extend body for connections') |
| `roofs[].floorPlanDisplay` | "Projected" \\| "ProjectedWithOverhead" \\| "CutOnly" \\| "OutlinesOnly" \\| "OverheadAll" \\| "CutAll" |  | Floor plan display option |
| `roofs[].viewDepth` | "FloorPlanRange" \\| "AbsoluteLimit" \\| "EntireElement" |  | Show projection: to floor plan range, to absolute limit, entire element |
| `roofs[].contourPen` | integer |  | Pen of the uncut contour on the floor plan |
| `roofs[].contourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the uncut contour (get_attributes type 'Line') |
| `roofs[].cutContourPen` | any |  | Pen of the cut contour |
| `roofs[].cutFillPen` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `roofs[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `roofs[].showCoverFill` | boolean |  | Show the floor plan cover fill |
| `roofs[].coverFill` | any |  | Cover fill type name/index (get_attributes type 'Fill') |
| `roofs[].coverFillPen` | any |  | Cover fill foreground pen |
| `roofs[].coverFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Cover fill background pen |
| `roofs[].storyVisibility` | object |  | Floor plan story visibility of the contour and cover fill (only the given flags change) |
| `roofs[].storyVisibility.onHomeStory` | boolean |  | Show on the home story |
| `roofs[].storyVisibility.allAbove` | boolean |  | Show on all stories above the home story |
| `roofs[].storyVisibility.allBelow` | boolean |  | Show on all stories below the home story |
| `roofs[].storyVisibility.storiesAbove` | integer |  | Show on this many stories above the home story |
| `roofs[].storyVisibility.storiesBelow` | integer |  | Show on this many stories below the home story |
| `roofs[].cutContourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the cut contour |
| `roofs[].overheadLinePen` | any |  | Pen of overhead lines |
| `roofs[].overheadLineType` | any |  | Line type of overhead lines |
| `roofs[].coverFillFromSurface` | boolean |  | Use the fill of the surface (vectorial 3D hatch) as cover fill |
| `roofs[].coverFillAlignedToPivot` | boolean |  | Align the cover fill with the pivot line (roofs) |
| `roofs[].coverFillDistorted` | boolean |  | Distort the cover fill with the slope |
| `roofs[].autoStoryVisibility` | boolean |  | Let Archicad compute the story visibility from the vertical extent |
| `roofs[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `roofs[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `roofs[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `roofs[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `roofs[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `create_shells`

Creates shells (free-form roofs/vaults/domes/canopies). Extruded: an open 'profile' polyline drawn in the profile plane (x across, y up) swept from 'begin' along the 3D 'extrusion' vector — e.g. a barrel vault: {profile: {points: [{x:-3,y:0},{x:3,y:0}], arcs: [{index:0, angle:-180}]}, begin: {x:0,y:0,z:3}, extrusion: {x:0,y:12,z:0}}. Revolved: 'profile' {x = distance from the axis, y = height} revolved by 'revolutionAngle' (default 360) around the vertical axis through 'axisOrigin' — e.g. a dome. Ruled: surface between 'profile' on 'plane1' and 'profile2' on 'plane2'. 'closedProfile': true for closed sections (tubes). 'thickness', 'flipped' (side of the thickness), structure, surfaces, edge trim and floor plan attributes as for roofs. Check the result in 3D and read the stored geometry with get_element_details (basePlane, profile, extrusion ...) — copy those values from an existing shell for exact placement. Units: meters and degrees; coordinates are project coordinates on the home story (default: current story; 'storyIndex' to choose). Unspecified settings come from the tool defaults. Attribute names (building materials, composites, surfaces, fills, line types, layers) are LOCALIZED — look them up with get_attributes. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}] in input order (one undo step; a failing item does not stop the others). Read results back with get_element_details (same field names).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `shells` | object[] | yes | Shells to create |
| `shells[].shellClass` | "Extruded" \\| "Revolved" \\| "Ruled" |  | Extruded (profile swept along 'extrusion'), Revolved (profile revolved around a vertical axis) or Ruled (surface between two profiles). Default Extruded |
| `shells[].profile` | object[] \\| object | yes | Profile polyline (m). Extruded: points in the profile plane {x across, y up}. Revolved: {x = distance from the axis, y = height}. Ruled: first profile in plane1 |
| `shells[].profile.points` | any[] | yes |  |
| `shells[].profile.arcs` | object[] |  |  |
| `shells[].closedProfile` | boolean |  | true = closed profile (e.g. a tube); false (default) = open profile (arch, vault, dome) |
| `shells[].flipped` | boolean |  | Apply the thickness to the other side of the reference surface |
| `shells[].level` | number |  | Base level offset of the shell from its home story (m) |
| `shells[].basePlane` | object |  | Advanced: the shell's local coordinate system (read it from get_element_details of an existing shell) |
| `shells[].basePlane.matrix` | number[] | yes | Row-major 3x4 matrix; translation at indices 3, 7, 11 |
| `shells[].defaultEdgeType` | "Undefined" \\| "Ridge" \\| "Valley" \\| "Gable" \\| "Hip" \\| "Eaves" \\| "Peak" \\| "SideWall" \\| "EndWall" \\| "Dome" \\| "Hollow" |  | Default contour edge type |
| `shells[].begin` | any |  | Extruded: start point of the extrusion (m; z relative to the home story, default 0) |
| `shells[].extrusion` | any |  | Extruded: extrusion vector (direction and length, m), e.g. {x: 10, y: 0, z: 0} for a 10 m long vault along X |
| `shells[].profileDirection` | any |  | Extruded, advanced: 2D direction of the profile's x axis |
| `shells[].slantAngle` | number |  | Extruded/Revolved: slant of the extrusion / revolution in degrees |
| `shells[].profilePlaneTilt` | number |  | Extruded: tilt of the profile plane relative to the extrusion vector (degrees) |
| `shells[].beginPlaneTilt` | number |  | Extruded: tilt of the start cap plane (degrees, 90 = perpendicular) |
| `shells[].endPlaneTilt` | number |  | Extruded: tilt of the end cap plane (degrees) |
| `shells[].axisOrigin` | any |  | Revolved: point on the vertical revolution axis (m), default 0,0,0 |
| `shells[].profileRotation` | number |  | Revolved: direction (degrees from +X in plan) of the profile's x axis (radial direction) |
| `shells[].axisBase` | any |  | Revolved, advanced: frame of the revolution axis relative to basePlane (z axis = axis) |
| `shells[].revolutionAngle` | number |  | Revolved: revolution angle in degrees (360 = full dome/tower, default 360 on create) |
| `shells[].beginAngle` | number |  | Revolved: start angle of the revolution in degrees |
| `shells[].distortionAngle` | number |  | Revolved: shear angle in degrees (90 = none) |
| `shells[].segmentedSurfaces` | boolean |  | Revolved: build the surface from planar segments |
| `shells[].arcSegments` | integer |  | Revolved: segments of the revolution arc |
| `shells[].circleSegments` | integer |  | Revolved: segments per full circle |
| `shells[].profile2` | any |  | Ruled: second profile |
| `shells[].plane1` | any |  | Ruled: plane (frame) of the first profile |
| `shells[].plane2` | any |  | Ruled: plane (frame) of the second profile |
| `shells[].morphingRule` | "Paired" \\| "Smooth" |  | Ruled: how the two profiles are connected |
| `shells[].thickness` | number |  | Thickness in m (ignored for composite structures) |
| `shells[].buildingMaterial` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Basic structure: building material name/index (localized — get_attributes type 'BuildingMaterial') |
| `shells[].buildingMaterial.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `shells[].composite` | any |  | Composite structure: composite name/index (get_attributes type 'Composite'; its 'usage' must include this element type, e.g. usage.slabs for slabs); the composite defines the layers and total thickness |
| `shells[].topSurface` | any \\| boolean |  | Top surface override (surface name/index, false = use the structure's surface) |
| `shells[].bottomSurface` | any |  | Bottom surface override |
| `shells[].sideSurface` | any |  | Edge/side surface override (applies to all edges; per-edge surfaces via 'edges') |
| `shells[].surfacesChained` | boolean |  | Chain the surface overrides (Archicad UI chain icon) |
| `shells[].edgeTrim` | "Vertical" \\| "Perpendicular" \\| "Horizontal" \\| "CustomAngle" \\| "AlignWithCut" |  | Default trim of the edges |
| `shells[].edgeAngle` | number |  | Custom edge angle in degrees (0-180 exclusive, 90 = vertical edge); implies edgeTrim 'CustomAngle' |
| `shells[].connectionBody` | "Editable" \\| "ContoursDown" \\| "PivotLinesDown" \\| "UpwardsExtrusion" \\| "DownwardsExtrusion" |  | Body extension used for connections/trims (Archicad 'Extend body for connections') |
| `shells[].floorPlanDisplay` | "Projected" \\| "ProjectedWithOverhead" \\| "CutOnly" \\| "OutlinesOnly" \\| "OverheadAll" \\| "CutAll" |  | Floor plan display option |
| `shells[].viewDepth` | "FloorPlanRange" \\| "AbsoluteLimit" \\| "EntireElement" |  | Show projection: to floor plan range, to absolute limit, entire element |
| `shells[].contourPen` | integer |  | Pen of the uncut contour on the floor plan |
| `shells[].contourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the uncut contour (get_attributes type 'Line') |
| `shells[].cutContourPen` | any |  | Pen of the cut contour |
| `shells[].cutFillPen` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `shells[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `shells[].showCoverFill` | boolean |  | Show the floor plan cover fill |
| `shells[].coverFill` | any |  | Cover fill type name/index (get_attributes type 'Fill') |
| `shells[].coverFillPen` | any |  | Cover fill foreground pen |
| `shells[].coverFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Cover fill background pen |
| `shells[].storyVisibility` | object |  | Floor plan story visibility of the contour and cover fill (only the given flags change) |
| `shells[].storyVisibility.onHomeStory` | boolean |  | Show on the home story |
| `shells[].storyVisibility.allAbove` | boolean |  | Show on all stories above the home story |
| `shells[].storyVisibility.allBelow` | boolean |  | Show on all stories below the home story |
| `shells[].storyVisibility.storiesAbove` | integer |  | Show on this many stories above the home story |
| `shells[].storyVisibility.storiesBelow` | integer |  | Show on this many stories below the home story |
| `shells[].cutContourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the cut contour |
| `shells[].overheadLinePen` | any |  | Pen of overhead lines |
| `shells[].overheadLineType` | any |  | Line type of overhead lines |
| `shells[].coverFillFromSurface` | boolean |  | Use the fill of the surface (vectorial 3D hatch) as cover fill |
| `shells[].coverFillAlignedToPivot` | boolean |  | Align the cover fill with the pivot line (roofs) |
| `shells[].coverFillDistorted` | boolean |  | Distort the cover fill with the slope |
| `shells[].autoStoryVisibility` | boolean |  | Let Archicad compute the story visibility from the vertical extent |
| `shells[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `shells[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `shells[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `shells[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `shells[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `create_meshes`

Creates meshes (terrain / site surfaces). 'polygon' points carry heights: {x, y, z} with z relative to the mesh base 'level'; add inner 'levelLines' (ridges / contour lines, each point with z) to shape the surface inside the outline; holes allowed. 'skirt': SolidBody | SkirtWithoutBottom | SurfaceOnly, 'skirtLevel' = depth of the body below the base plane. 'ridges' controls smooth/sharp display. Example: {polygon: [{x:0,y:0,z:0},{x:30,y:0,z:1},{x:30,y:20,z:2.5},{x:0,y:20,z:0.5}], level: -0.1, skirt: 'SolidBody', skirtLevel: 1}. Units: meters and degrees; coordinates are project coordinates on the home story (default: current story; 'storyIndex' to choose). Unspecified settings come from the tool defaults. Attribute names (building materials, composites, surfaces, fills, line types, layers) are LOCALIZED — look them up with get_attributes. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}] in input order (one undo step; a failing item does not stop the others). Read results back with get_element_details (same field names).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `meshes` | object[] | yes | Meshes to create |
| `meshes[].polygon` | object[] \\| object | yes | Mesh outline {points: [{x, y, z}], arcs?, holes?}: x/y in plan (m), z = height of the point above the mesh 'level' (omitted z = 0). Do not repeat the first point |
| `meshes[].polygon.points` | any[] | yes | Vertices in order, do NOT repeat the first point at the end |
| `meshes[].polygon.arcs` | object[] |  | Curved edges |
| `meshes[].polygon.holes` | object[] |  | Inner holes, each {points, arcs?} |
| `meshes[].level` | number |  | Elevation of the mesh base plane relative to the home story (m); point z values are relative to it |
| `meshes[].skirt` | "SolidBody" \\| "SkirtWithoutBottom" \\| "SurfaceOnly" |  | Body type: SolidBody (skirt + bottom), SkirtWithoutBottom, SurfaceOnly |
| `meshes[].skirtLevel` | number |  | Distance of the mesh bottom (skirt) from the base plane (m) |
| `meshes[].ridges` | "AllSharp" \\| "AllSmooth" \\| "UserDefined" |  | Ridge display: all sharp, all smooth, or only user-defined ridges sharp |
| `meshes[].showAllLines` | boolean |  | Show all (also secondary) mesh lines on the floor plan |
| `meshes[].levelLines` | object[] |  | Inner level lines (replace existing ones); [] removes all level lines |
| `meshes[].levelLines[].points` | object[] | yes | Points of the level line; z = height above the mesh level (m) |
| `meshes[].buildingMaterial` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Building material name/index (meshes have no composites) |
| `meshes[].buildingMaterial.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `meshes[].topSurface` | any \\| boolean |  | Top surface override (surface name/index, false = use the structure's surface) |
| `meshes[].bottomSurface` | any |  | Bottom surface override |
| `meshes[].sideSurface` | any |  | Edge/side surface override (applies to all edges; per-edge surfaces via 'edges') |
| `meshes[].surfacesChained` | boolean |  | Chain the surface overrides (Archicad UI chain icon) |
| `meshes[].contourPen` | integer |  | Pen of the uncut contour on the floor plan |
| `meshes[].contourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the uncut contour (get_attributes type 'Line') |
| `meshes[].cutContourPen` | any |  | Pen of the cut contour |
| `meshes[].cutFillPen` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `meshes[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `meshes[].showCoverFill` | boolean |  | Show the floor plan cover fill |
| `meshes[].coverFill` | any |  | Cover fill type name/index (get_attributes type 'Fill') |
| `meshes[].coverFillPen` | any |  | Cover fill foreground pen |
| `meshes[].coverFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Cover fill background pen |
| `meshes[].storyVisibility` | object |  | Floor plan story visibility of the contour and cover fill (only the given flags change) |
| `meshes[].storyVisibility.onHomeStory` | boolean |  | Show on the home story |
| `meshes[].storyVisibility.allAbove` | boolean |  | Show on all stories above the home story |
| `meshes[].storyVisibility.allBelow` | boolean |  | Show on all stories below the home story |
| `meshes[].storyVisibility.storiesAbove` | integer |  | Show on this many stories above the home story |
| `meshes[].storyVisibility.storiesBelow` | integer |  | Show on this many stories below the home story |
| `meshes[].levelLinePen` | any |  | Pen of the level lines |
| `meshes[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `meshes[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `meshes[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `meshes[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `meshes[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `modify_slabs`

Changes existing slabs: polygon (outline incl. holes — replaces the shape and resets per-edge data), thickness, level, referencePlane, structure, surfaces, edge trims (all edges or per edge via 'edges'), floor plan attributes, layer, story, renovation status, element ID. Fields as in create_slabs. Only the given fields change; the others keep their values. Each item must be an element of this type (checked first; other items still run). Returns [{guid} | {error}] in input order, one undo step.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `slabs` | object[] | yes | Patches {guid, ...fields to change} |
| `slabs[].guid` | string | yes | Slab GUID |
| `slabs[].polygon` | object[] \\| object |  | New outline incl. holes (replaces the whole shape; per-edge data is reset to the slab-wide trim/surface) |
| `slabs[].polygon.points` | any[] | yes | Vertices in order, do NOT repeat the first point at the end |
| `slabs[].polygon.arcs` | object[] |  | Curved edges |
| `slabs[].polygon.holes` | object[] |  | Inner holes, each {points, arcs?} |
| `slabs[].thickness` | number |  | Thickness in m (ignored for composite slabs: the composite defines it) |
| `slabs[].level` | number |  | Elevation of the reference plane relative to the home story level (m). With referencePlane 'Top' (usual) this is the slab TOP; e.g. 0 = top at story level |
| `slabs[].referencePlane` | "Top" \\| "CoreTop" \\| "CoreBottom" \\| "Bottom" |  | Which plane of the slab 'level' refers to |
| `slabs[].buildingMaterial` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Basic structure: building material name/index (localized — get_attributes type 'BuildingMaterial') |
| `slabs[].buildingMaterial.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `slabs[].composite` | any |  | Composite structure: composite name/index (get_attributes type 'Composite'; its 'usage' must include this element type, e.g. usage.slabs for slabs); the composite defines the layers and total thickness |
| `slabs[].topSurface` | any \\| boolean |  | Top surface override (surface name/index, false = use the structure's surface) |
| `slabs[].bottomSurface` | any |  | Bottom surface override |
| `slabs[].sideSurface` | any |  | Edge/side surface override (applies to all edges; per-edge surfaces via 'edges') |
| `slabs[].surfacesChained` | boolean |  | Chain the surface overrides (Archicad UI chain icon) |
| `slabs[].edgeTrim` | "Vertical" \\| "Perpendicular" \\| "CustomAngle" |  | Trim of ALL slab edges (per-edge overrides via 'edges'): Vertical, Perpendicular or CustomAngle (+ edgeAngle) |
| `slabs[].edgeAngle` | number |  | Custom edge angle in degrees (0-180 exclusive, 90 = vertical edge); implies edgeTrim 'CustomAngle' |
| `slabs[].edges` | object[] |  | Per-edge trim / surface overrides |
| `slabs[].edges[].contour` | integer |  | 0 = outline (default), 1..N = holes in the order given |
| `slabs[].edges[].index` | integer | yes | Edge index: edge i runs from point i to point i+1 (the last edge closes the contour) |
| `slabs[].edges[].trim` | any |  | Slab edge trim: Vertical (default), Perpendicular (same as Vertical on a flat slab) or CustomAngle (with an angle). Horizontal/AlignWithCut exist only for roofs and shells |
| `slabs[].edges[].angle` | any |  | Custom edge angle in degrees (0-180 exclusive, 90 = vertical edge); implies edgeTrim 'CustomAngle' |
| `slabs[].edges[].surface` | any |  | Surface of this edge (false = structure's surface) |
| `slabs[].contourPen` | integer |  | Pen of the uncut contour on the floor plan |
| `slabs[].contourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the uncut contour (get_attributes type 'Line') |
| `slabs[].cutContourPen` | any |  | Pen of the cut contour |
| `slabs[].cutFillPen` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `slabs[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (1-255); false = use the structure's pen |
| `slabs[].showCoverFill` | boolean |  | Show the floor plan cover fill |
| `slabs[].coverFill` | any |  | Cover fill type name/index (get_attributes type 'Fill') |
| `slabs[].coverFillPen` | any |  | Cover fill foreground pen |
| `slabs[].coverFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Cover fill background pen |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility` | object |  | Floor plan story visibility of the contour and cover fill (only the given flags change) |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.onHomeStory` | boolean |  | Show on the home story |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.allAbove` | boolean |  | Show on all stories above the home story |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.allBelow` | boolean |  | Show on all stories below the home story |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.storiesAbove` | integer |  | Show on this many stories above the home story |
| `slabs[].storyVisibility.storiesBelow` | integer |  | Show on this many stories below the home story |
| `slabs[].cutContourLineType` | any |  | Line type of the cut contour |
| `slabs[].hiddenContourPen` | any |  | Pen of hidden contour lines |
| `slabs[].hiddenContourLineType` | any |  | Line type of hidden contour lines |
