Read the current values with get_element_details. Returns [{guid, ...summary} | {error}].

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dimensions` | object[] | yes |  |
| `dimensions[].guid` | string | yes | Dimension GUID (Dimension, LevelDimension, RadialDimension or AngleDimension) |
| `dimensions[].points` | object[] |  | Linear: REPLACE all points (same format as create_dimensions) |
| `dimensions[].points[].x` | number |  | X in m. Alone with y: a static point. With 'element': a point NEAR the element point to link (snapped within snapTolerance) |
| `dimensions[].points[].y` | number |  | Y in m (see x) |
| `dimensions[].points[].element` | string \\| object |  | Link this point to an element (associative: the dimension follows when the element moves) |
| `dimensions[].points[].at` | "begin" \\| "end" |  | With a Wall / Beam / Line 'element': link to the begin or end of its reference line (no x,y needed) |
| `dimensions[].points[].inIndex` | integer |  | EXPERT: explicit Archicad reference (API_Base) — copy element/inIndex/line/special from an existing dimension's details or get_dimension_anchors; needs x,y too (approximate location). Wall thickness (DevKit convention): {element: wall, inIndex: 11, line: true, x, y} and {element: wall, inIndex: 21, line: true, x, y} — the two wall faces — with a direction perpendicular to the wall |
| `dimensions[].points[].line` | boolean |  | EXPERT (with inIndex): the reference is a line/edge of the element, not a point |
| `dimensions[].points[].special` | boolean |  | EXPERT (with inIndex): Archicad's 'special' reference flag (wall corners, opening holes) |
| `dimensions[].points[].nodeId` | integer |  | EXPERT (with inIndex): polygon vertex id |
| `dimensions[].points[].witnessForm` | "None" \\| "Small" \\| "Large" \\| "Fixed" |  | Witness line form of this point only |
| `dimensions[].points[].witnessVal` | number |  | Witness line value: the gap between the dimensioned point and the witness line (form 'Large') or the witness line length (form 'Fixed'), in the unit Archicad stores — read witnessVal of an existing dimension (get_element_details) or of the tool defaults first |
| `dimensions[].points[].text` | string \\| boolean |  | Custom text shown at this point: in a 'Normal' chain the text of the segment ENDING here (e.g. 'EQ'; meaningless on the first point), in 'Cumulative'/'Elevation' chains the point's own value |
| `dimensions[].direction` | "Horizontal" \\| "Vertical" \\| number \\| object |  | Linear: new dimension line direction |
| `dimensions[].linePoint` | object |  | Linear: MOVE the dimension line so that it passes through this point |
| `dimensions[].offset` | number |  | Linear: move the dimension line to this distance (m) from the first point (+ = left of the direction) |
| `dimensions[].makeStatic` | boolean |  | Linear: detach every point from its element (points stay where they are) |
| `dimensions[].pointTexts` | object[] |  | Linear: set custom texts of single points WITHOUT replacing the points, e.g. [{index: 2, text: 'EQ'}] (in a Normal chain point i carries the text of the segment from point i-1 to i); text ""/false restores the measured value |
| `dimensions[].pointTexts[].index` | integer | yes | 0-based index into the dimension's points (as listed by get_element_details) |
| `dimensions[].pointTexts[].text` | any | yes |  |
| `dimensions[].associative` | boolean |  | Linear, with points: link element points (default true) |
| `dimensions[].snapTolerance` | number |  | Linear, with points: see create_dimensions |
| `dimensions[].appearance` | "Normal" \\| "Cumulative" \\| "CumulativeSV" \\| "Elevation" |  | Chain type: 'Normal' (segment lengths, default), 'Cumulative' (running distances from the first point), 'CumulativeSV' (Archicad's other cumulative variant), 'Elevation' (elevation-type values) |
| `dimensions[].horizontalText` | boolean |  | Keep the texts horizontal |
| `dimensions[].layout` | "Legacy" \\| "Flexible" \\| "Centered" \\| "Off" |  | Text layout algorithm for crowded chains ('Flexible' moves colliding texts) |
| `dimensions[].clipOtherSide` | boolean |  | Do not draw the witness line part beyond the dimensioned point |
| `dimensions[].position` | any |  | Level: move the marker |
| `dimensions[].element` | any \\| string \\| boolean |  | Level: show this element's level; "" or false = back to the story level |
| `dimensions[].level` | number |  | Static level value in m (makes the marker static: it no longer follows the story/element) |
| `dimensions[].static` | boolean |  | Static marker (keeps its value); set false to follow the story/element again |
| `dimensions[].elevationReference` | "ProjectZero" \\| "ReferenceLevel1" \\| "ReferenceLevel2" \\| "StoredOrigin" \\| "SeaLevel" |  | What the shown elevation is measured from: 'ProjectZero', 'ReferenceLevel1'/'ReferenceLevel2' (the project's reference levels), 'StoredOrigin', 'SeaLevel' (altitude). Default from the Level Dimension tool (usually ProjectZero) |
| `dimensions[].markerStyle` | integer |  | Marker form 0..9 (Archicad's level dimension marker types) |
| `dimensions[].pen` | integer |  | Pen of the marker |
| `dimensions[].markerAngle` | number |  | Rotation of the marker in degrees |
| `dimensions[].showPlusSign` | boolean |  | Show '+' before positive values |
| `dimensions[].secondText` | any |  | Second note (marker styles 8 and 9 only) |
| `dimensions[].at` | any |  | Radial: move the point where the dimension touches the arc |
| `dimensions[].lineEnd` | any |  | Radial: new end of the radial line |
| `dimensions[].showCenter` | boolean |  | Mark the arc center |
| `dimensions[].prefix` | string |  | Text before the value, e.g. 'R' or 'R=' |
| `dimensions[].line1` | object |  | Angle: new first line (makes the dimension static) |
| `dimensions[].line1.begin` | any | yes |  |
| `dimensions[].line1.end` | any | yes |  |
| `dimensions[].line2` | any |  | Angle: new second line (makes the dimension static) |
| `dimensions[].arcPoint` | any |  | A point the dimension arc passes through: picks the measured sector and the arc radius |
| `dimensions[].radius` | number |  | Arc radius in m (default 1; with arcPoint: keeps its direction, sets the distance) |
| `dimensions[].smallArc` | boolean |  | Measure the smaller angle between the lines (default true) |
| `dimensions[].textPosition` | "Above" \\| "In" \\| "Below" |  | Where the value text sits relative to the dimension line: 'Above' (default of most standards), 'In' (breaks the line), 'Below' |
| `dimensions[].textDirection` | "Parallel" \\| "Horizontal" \\| "Vertical" \\| "Radial" \\| "Perpendicular" |  | Linear: Parallel\|Horizontal\|Vertical; Radial: Horizontal\|Vertical\|Radial; Angle: Parallel\|Horizontal\|Perpendicular |
| `dimensions[].linePen` | any |  | Pen of the dimension lines (linear, radial, angle) |
| `dimensions[].witnessForm` | any |  | Linear (applied to every point) and angle |
| `dimensions[].witnessVal` | any |  | Witness line value: the gap between the dimensioned point and the witness line (form 'Large') or the witness line length (form 'Fixed'), in the unit Archicad stores — read witnessVal of an existing dimension (get_element_details) or of the tool defaults first |
| `dimensions[].onlyDimensionText` | boolean |  |  |
| `dimensions[].text` | any |  | Level / radial / angle: custom text ("" = measured value). Linear: use pointTexts (or points[].text) |
| `dimensions[].markerType` | "CrossLine" \\| "EmptyCircle" \\| "SlashLine60" \\| "OpenArrow30" \\| "ClosedArrow30" \\| "FullArrow30" \\| "SlashLine45" \\| "CrossCircle" \\| "OpenArrow90" \\| "ClosedArrow90" \\| "FullArrow90" \\| "FullCircle" \\| "PepitaCircle" \\| "BandArrow" \\| "OpenArrow60" \\| "ClosedArrow60" \\| "FullArrow60" \\| "SlashLine75" |  | Dimension line end marker: ticks ('SlashLine45', 'SlashLine60', 'SlashLine75', 'CrossLine'), arrows ('OpenArrow30', 'FullArrow30', ...), dots ('FullCircle', 'EmptyCircle', 'PepitaCircle', 'CrossCircle'), 'BandArrow' |
| `dimensions[].markerPen` | any |  | Pen of the markers |
| `dimensions[].markerSize` | number |  | Marker size in PAPER millimeters (e.g. 2) |
| `dimensions[].textPen` | any |  | Pen (color) of the dimension text, 1-255 |
| `dimensions[].textSize` | number |  | Text height in PAPER millimeters (e.g. 2.5), independent of the drawing scale |
| `dimensions[].textFont` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Font by name (e.g. 'Arial') or font attribute index |
| `dimensions[].textFont.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `dimensions[].textBold` | boolean |  |  |
| `dimensions[].textItalic` | boolean |  |  |
| `dimensions[].textUnderline` | boolean |  |  |
| `dimensions[].textFrame` | boolean |  | Draw a frame around the text |
| `dimensions[].textOpaque` | boolean |  | Opaque (filled) text background |
| `dimensions[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `dimensions[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `dimensions[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `dimensions[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step |

### `get_dimension_anchors`

Lists the points of elements that dimensions can link to (hotspots: wall ends and corners, column corners, opening points, slab vertices, object hotspots...), with coordinates in m. Use a point's x,y with {element, x, y} in create_dimensions to create an associative point. Pass 'near' to sort by distance (and 'radius' to filter). Only anchors with usableAsPoint = true can be linked. Model elements (walls, columns, slabs, openings...) are read on the Floor Plan (plan coordinates) whatever window is active. Returns {elements: [{guid, type, anchorCount, anchors: [{x, y, z, neig, inIndex, line, special, usableAsPoint, source, distance?}]} | {error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `near` | object |  | Sort anchors by distance to this point |
| `radius` | number |  | With near: only anchors within this distance (m) |
| `limit` | integer |  | Max anchors per element (default 100) |


## complex-elements

### `create_morphs`

Creates morphs (free-form 3D bodies) in one undo step. Each item needs exactly ONE geometry: 'box' {origin {x,y,z}, size {x,y,z}, rotation?}; 'extrusion' {polygon, zBottom, zTop} (prism of a plan polygon with holes/arcs); or 'mesh' {vertices: [{x,y,z}], faces: [[i,j,k,...]]} (any polyhedron; faces counter-clockwise seen from outside, planar). x/y are project coordinates, z is relative to the home story level. Closed bodies become Solid, open meshes Surface. Per-face surfaces: box/extrusion top/bottom/sideSurface, mesh face {vertices, surface}; 'surface' covers the rest. Coordinates in meters, angles in degrees, on the given story (default: current story); unspecified settings come from the tool defaults. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}] in input order. Read back with get_element_details (body counts, bounds) or get_morph_geometry; change with modify_morphs; combine with solid_operation.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `morphs` | object[] | yes | Morphs to create |
| `morphs[].box` | object |  | Rectangular box |
| `morphs[].box.origin` | object | yes | Box corner with the smallest local x/y/z (m; z relative to the home story) |
| `morphs[].box.size` | object | yes | Box dimensions in m along its local x (after rotation), y and the vertical |
| `morphs[].box.rotation` | number |  | Rotation in degrees, counter-clockwise seen from above, around the vertical axis through origin (default 0) |
| `morphs[].box.topSurface` | integer \\| string \\| object \\| boolean |  | Surface of the top face |
| `morphs[].box.bottomSurface` | any |  | Surface of the bottom face |
| `morphs[].box.sideSurface` | any |  | Surface of the side faces |
| `morphs[].extrusion` | object |  | Vertical extrusion of a plan polygon between zBottom and zTop (prism with holes) |
| `morphs[].extrusion.polygon` | object[] \\| object | yes | Plan polygon (m): points, optional arcs (curved edges, tessellated) and holes |
| `morphs[].extrusion.zBottom` | number | yes | Bottom elevation in m relative to the home story level |
| `morphs[].extrusion.zTop` | number | yes | Top elevation in m relative to the home story level (must be > zBottom) |
| `morphs[].extrusion.arcSegmentAngle` | number |  | Max angle in degrees per straight segment when tessellating arcs (default 10) |
| `morphs[].extrusion.topSurface` | any |  |  |
| `morphs[].extrusion.bottomSurface` | any |  |  |
| `morphs[].extrusion.sideSurface` | any |  |  |
| `morphs[].mesh` | object |  | Arbitrary polyhedral mesh (BREP). get_morph_geometry returns existing morphs in this format |
| `morphs[].mesh.vertices` | object \\| any[][] | yes | Vertex list; faces reference it by 0-based index |
| `morphs[].mesh.faces` | integer[] \\| object[] | yes | Faces. For a closed solid every edge must be shared by exactly two faces traversed in opposite directions; open meshes become Surface bodies |
| `morphs[].bodyType` | "Solid" \\| "Surface" |  | Solid (closed volume) or Surface (open shell). Default: Solid when the mesh is closed, else Surface |
| `morphs[].edgeType` | "SoftHidden" \\| "HardHidden" \\| "HardVisible" |  | Default edge display of the body |
| `morphs[].surface` | any |  | Surface of all faces without their own surface (false = building material surface) |
| `morphs[].faceSurface` | any |  | Per-face surface given to EVERY face (on create/replace: faces without their own 'surface'); false = clear per-face overrides |
| `morphs[].buildingMaterial` | any |  | Building material name/index (localized — get_attributes type BuildingMaterial) |
| `morphs[].castShadow` | boolean |  | Body casts shadows |
| `morphs[].receiveShadow` | boolean |  | Body receives shadows |
| `morphs[].floorPlanDisplay` | "Projected" \\| "ProjectedWithOverhead" \\| "CutOnly" \\| "OutlinesOnly" \\| "OverheadAll" \\| "SymbolicCut" |  | Floor plan display option (Floor Plan and Section > Show on Floor Plan) |
| `morphs[].viewDepth` | "ToFloorPlanRange" \\| "ToAbsoluteLimit" \\| "EntireElement" |  | How far the element is shown on the floor plan (Show Projection) |
| `morphs[].cutLinePen` | integer |  | Pen of cut lines |
| `morphs[].cutLineType` | any |  | Line type name/index (localized — list with get_attributes type Line) |
| `morphs[].uncutLinePen` | any |  | Pen of uncut (projected) lines |
| `morphs[].uncutLineType` | any |  | Line type name/index (localized — list with get_attributes type Line) |
| `morphs[].overheadLinePen` | any |  | Pen of overhead lines |
| `morphs[].overheadLineType` | any |  | Line type name/index (localized — list with get_attributes type Line) |
| `morphs[].cutFillPen` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (false = pen of the building material) |
| `morphs[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (false = pen of the building material) |
| `morphs[].showCoverFill` | boolean |  | Show a cover fill on the uncut part |
| `morphs[].coverFill` | any |  | Cover fill type name/index (turns showCoverFill on) |
| `morphs[].coverFillPen` | any |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `morphs[].coverFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `morphs[].coverFillFromSurface` | boolean |  | Use the fill of the surface as cover fill |
| `morphs[].outlineContourDisplay` | boolean |  | Show only the outline contour on the floor plan |
| `morphs[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `morphs[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `morphs[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `morphs[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `morphs[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `modify_morphs`

Changes existing morphs in one undo step: replace the whole body (box / extrusion / mesh — e.g. edit the output of get_morph_geometry and send it back), move it (offset {x,y,z}, level), set every face's surface (faceSurface) or the default surface, building material, body/edge type, shadows and floor plan display. Only the given fields change. Returns [{guid} | {error}] in input order.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `morphs` | object[] | yes | Morph patches |
| `morphs[].guid` | string | yes | GUID of the morph to change |
| `morphs[].box` | object |  | Rectangular box |
| `morphs[].box.origin` | object | yes | Box corner with the smallest local x/y/z (m; z relative to the home story) |
| `morphs[].box.size` | object | yes | Box dimensions in m along its local x (after rotation), y and the vertical |
| `morphs[].box.rotation` | number |  | Rotation in degrees, counter-clockwise seen from above, around the vertical axis through origin (default 0) |
| `morphs[].box.topSurface` | integer \\| string \\| object \\| boolean |  | Surface of the top face |
| `morphs[].box.bottomSurface` | any |  | Surface of the bottom face |
| `morphs[].box.sideSurface` | any |  | Surface of the side faces |
| `morphs[].extrusion` | object |  | Vertical extrusion of a plan polygon between zBottom and zTop (prism with holes) |
| `morphs[].extrusion.polygon` | object[] \\| object | yes | Plan polygon (m): points, optional arcs (curved edges, tessellated) and holes |
| `morphs[].extrusion.zBottom` | number | yes | Bottom elevation in m relative to the home story level |
| `morphs[].extrusion.zTop` | number | yes | Top elevation in m relative to the home story level (must be > zBottom) |
| `morphs[].extrusion.arcSegmentAngle` | number |  | Max angle in degrees per straight segment when tessellating arcs (default 10) |
| `morphs[].extrusion.topSurface` | any |  |  |
| `morphs[].extrusion.bottomSurface` | any |  |  |
| `morphs[].extrusion.sideSurface` | any |  |  |
| `morphs[].mesh` | object |  | Arbitrary polyhedral mesh (BREP). get_morph_geometry returns existing morphs in this format |
| `morphs[].mesh.vertices` | object \\| any[][] | yes | Vertex list; faces reference it by 0-based index |
| `morphs[].mesh.faces` | integer[] \\| object[] | yes | Faces. For a closed solid every edge must be shared by exactly two faces traversed in opposite directions; open meshes become Surface bodies |
| `morphs[].offset` | any |  | Move the morph by this vector (m) |
| `morphs[].level` | number |  | Move the morph vertically so its base is at this height (m, relative to the home story) |
| `morphs[].bodyType` | "Solid" \\| "Surface" |  | Solid (closed volume) or Surface (open shell). Default: Solid when the mesh is closed, else Surface |
| `morphs[].edgeType` | "SoftHidden" \\| "HardHidden" \\| "HardVisible" |  | Default edge display of the body |
| `morphs[].surface` | any |  | Surface of all faces without their own surface (false = building material surface) |
| `morphs[].faceSurface` | any |  | Per-face surface given to EVERY face (on create/replace: faces without their own 'surface'); false = clear per-face overrides |
| `morphs[].buildingMaterial` | any |  | Building material name/index (localized — get_attributes type BuildingMaterial) |
| `morphs[].castShadow` | boolean |  | Body casts shadows |
| `morphs[].receiveShadow` | boolean |  | Body receives shadows |
| `morphs[].floorPlanDisplay` | "Projected" \\| "ProjectedWithOverhead" \\| "CutOnly" \\| "OutlinesOnly" \\| "OverheadAll" \\| "SymbolicCut" |  | Floor plan display option (Floor Plan and Section > Show on Floor Plan) |
| `morphs[].viewDepth` | "ToFloorPlanRange" \\| "ToAbsoluteLimit" \\| "EntireElement" |  | How far the element is shown on the floor plan (Show Projection) |
| `morphs[].cutLinePen` | integer |  | Pen of cut lines |
| `morphs[].cutLineType` | any |  | Line type name/index (localized — list with get_attributes type Line) |
| `morphs[].uncutLinePen` | any |  | Pen of uncut (projected) lines |
| `morphs[].uncutLineType` | any |  | Line type name/index (localized — list with get_attributes type Line) |
| `morphs[].overheadLinePen` | any |  | Pen of overhead lines |
| `morphs[].overheadLineType` | any |  | Line type name/index (localized — list with get_attributes type Line) |
| `morphs[].cutFillPen` | any \\| boolean |  | Override the cut fill foreground pen (false = pen of the building material) |
| `morphs[].cutFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Override the cut fill background pen (false = pen of the building material) |
| `morphs[].showCoverFill` | boolean |  | Show a cover fill on the uncut part |
| `morphs[].coverFill` | any |  | Cover fill type name/index (turns showCoverFill on) |
| `morphs[].coverFillPen` | any |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `morphs[].coverFillBackgroundPen` | any |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `morphs[].coverFillFromSurface` | boolean |  | Use the fill of the surface as cover fill |
| `morphs[].outlineContourDisplay` | boolean |  | Show only the outline contour on the floor plan |

| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `get_morph_geometry`

Returns the editable geometry of morphs: {elements: [{guid, bodyType, vertexCount, faceCount, vertices: [{x,y,z}], faces: [{vertices: [i,...], holes?: [{vertices}], surface?, hidden?}]}]} — the same format as the 'mesh' input of create_morphs / modify_morphs, with the morph transformation applied (x/y project coordinates, z relative to the home story). Per-item errors for non-morphs.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Morph GUIDs |
| `maxVertices` | integer |  | Refuse bodies with more vertices than this (default 20000) |

### `create_curtain_walls`

Creates curtain walls in one undo step along a straight ('begin'/'end') or polyline/curved ('path') base line with the Curtain Wall tool's default scheme (frame/panel classes). Set 'height', the grid (primarySpacing = module width along the wall, secondarySpacing = module height, or full primaryGrid/secondaryGrid patterns), panel surfaces/thickness and frame surface. Coordinates in meters, angles in degrees, on the given story (default: current story); unspecified settings come from the tool defaults. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}]. get_element_details lists segments, grids, frame/panel classes and the GUIDs of every frame and panel (edit single panels/frames with modify_curtain_wall_parts; change height/grid/classes with modify_curtain_walls).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `curtainWalls` | object[] | yes | Curtain walls to create |
| `curtainWalls[].path` | object[] \\| object |  | Base line on the plan (m): points (and arcs for curved segments). Each segment becomes a curtain wall segment; repeat the first point at the end to close it |
| `curtainWalls[].path.points` | any[] | yes |  |
| `curtainWalls[].path.arcs` | object[] |  |  |
| `curtainWalls[].begin` | any |  | Straight curtain wall: start point (instead of path) |
| `curtainWalls[].end` | any |  | Straight curtain wall: end point (instead of path) |
| `curtainWalls[].bottomOffset` | number |  | Base elevation relative to the home story (m, create only) |
| `curtainWalls[].height` | number |  | Height in m |
| `curtainWalls[].flipped` | boolean |  | Mirror the curtain wall to the other side of its base line |
| `curtainWalls[].zoneRelation` | "Boundary" \\| "ReduceArea" \\| "None" |  | How the curtain wall affects zone boundaries |
| `curtainWalls[].boundaryFramePosition` | "Outside" \\| "Center" \\| "Inside" |  | Position of the boundary frames relative to the base line |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid` | object |  | Primary grid = module WIDTHS along the curtain wall length (vertical mullions) |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.logic` | "FixedSizes" \\| "BestDivision" \\| "NumberOfDivisions" |  | FixedSizes: 'sizes' repeat exactly, the remainder goes to the end; BestDivision: 'sizes' are target sizes stretched to fit whole modules; NumberOfDivisions: 'divisions' equal parts (default: keep the current logic) |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.sizes` | number[] |  | Module sizes in m, repeated along the grid direction |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.divisions` | integer |  | Number of equal parts (only for NumberOfDivisions) |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.origin` | "StartWithPattern" \\| "StartFromCenter" \\| "AlignToCenter" \\| "EndWithPattern" |  | Where the pattern starts |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.flexible` | integer[] |  | 0-based indices of 'sizes' items that may stretch to absorb the remainder (default [0]; Archicad needs at least one flexible module when sizes change) |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.endWith` | integer |  | 0-based index of the 'sizes' item the pattern ends with (default: last) |
| `curtainWalls[].secondaryGrid` | any |  | Secondary grid = module HEIGHTS (horizontal transoms) |
| `curtainWalls[].primarySpacing` | number |  | Shorthand: primaryGrid {logic: FixedSizes, sizes: [value]} (m) |
| `curtainWalls[].secondarySpacing` | number |  | Shorthand: secondaryGrid {logic: FixedSizes, sizes: [value]} (m) |
| `curtainWalls[].panelSurface` | integer \\| string \\| object \\| boolean |  | Outer, inner and edge surface of ALL panel classes (e.g. a glass surface) |
| `curtainWalls[].panelOuterSurface` | any |  | Outer surface of all panel classes |
| `curtainWalls[].panelInnerSurface` | any |  | Inner surface of all panel classes |
| `curtainWalls[].panelThickness` | number |  | Thickness of all panel classes (m) |
| `curtainWalls[].frameSurface` | any |  | Surface of all frame classes (incl. corner/boundary frames) |
| `curtainWalls[].frameBuildingMaterial` | any |  | Building material of all frame classes |
| `curtainWalls[].floorPlanDisplay` | "Projected" \\| "ProjectedWithOverhead" \\| "CutOnly" \\| "OutlinesOnly" \\| "OverheadAll" \\| "SymbolicCut" |  | Floor plan display option (Floor Plan and Section > Show on Floor Plan) |
| `curtainWalls[].viewDepth` | "ToFloorPlanRange" \\| "ToAbsoluteLimit" \\| "EntireElement" |  | How far the element is shown on the floor plan (Show Projection) |
| `curtainWalls[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `curtainWalls[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `curtainWalls[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `curtainWalls[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `curtainWalls[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `modify_curtain_walls`

Changes existing curtain walls in one undo step: height, flip, grid patterns (primaryGrid/secondaryGrid or the *Spacing shorthands), surfaces/thickness of all panel classes, surface/building material of all frame classes, zone relation, floor plan display. The base line cannot be changed (use move_elements/rotate_elements or recreate). Returns [{guid} | {error}].

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `curtainWalls` | object[] | yes | Curtain wall patches |
| `curtainWalls[].guid` | string | yes | GUID of the curtain wall to change |
| `curtainWalls[].height` | number |  | Height in m |
| `curtainWalls[].flipped` | boolean |  | Mirror the curtain wall to the other side of its base line |
| `curtainWalls[].zoneRelation` | "Boundary" \\| "ReduceArea" \\| "None" |  | How the curtain wall affects zone boundaries |
| `curtainWalls[].boundaryFramePosition` | "Outside" \\| "Center" \\| "Inside" |  | Position of the boundary frames relative to the base line |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid` | object |  | Primary grid = module WIDTHS along the curtain wall length (vertical mullions) |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.logic` | "FixedSizes" \\| "BestDivision" \\| "NumberOfDivisions" |  | FixedSizes: 'sizes' repeat exactly, the remainder goes to the end; BestDivision: 'sizes' are target sizes stretched to fit whole modules; NumberOfDivisions: 'divisions' equal parts (default: keep the current logic) |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.sizes` | number[] |  | Module sizes in m, repeated along the grid direction |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.divisions` | integer |  | Number of equal parts (only for NumberOfDivisions) |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.origin` | "StartWithPattern" \\| "StartFromCenter" \\| "AlignToCenter" \\| "EndWithPattern" |  | Where the pattern starts |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.flexible` | integer[] |  | 0-based indices of 'sizes' items that may stretch to absorb the remainder (default [0]; Archicad needs at least one flexible module when sizes change) |
| `curtainWalls[].primaryGrid.endWith` | integer |  | 0-based index of the 'sizes' item the pattern ends with (default: last) |
| `curtainWalls[].secondaryGrid` | any |  | Secondary grid = module HEIGHTS (horizontal transoms) |
| `curtainWalls[].primarySpacing` | number |  | Shorthand: primaryGrid {logic: FixedSizes, sizes: [value]} (m) |
| `curtainWalls[].secondarySpacing` | number |  | Shorthand: secondaryGrid {logic: FixedSizes, sizes: [value]} (m) |
| `curtainWalls[].panelSurface` | integer \\| string \\| object \\| boolean |  | Outer, inner and edge surface of ALL panel classes (e.g. a glass surface) |
| `curtainWalls[].panelOuterSurface` | any |  | Outer surface of all panel classes |
| `curtainWalls[].panelInnerSurface` | any |  | Inner surface of all panel classes |
| `curtainWalls[].panelThickness` | number |  | Thickness of all panel classes (m) |
| `curtainWalls[].frameSurface` | any |  | Surface of all frame classes (incl. corner/boundary frames) |
| `curtainWalls[].frameBuildingMaterial` | any |  | Building material of all frame classes |
| `curtainWalls[].floorPlanDisplay` | "Projected" \\| "ProjectedWithOverhead" \\| "CutOnly" \\| "OutlinesOnly" \\| "OverheadAll" \\| "SymbolicCut" |  | Floor plan display option (Floor Plan and Section > Show on Floor Plan) |
| `curtainWalls[].viewDepth` | "ToFloorPlanRange" \\| "ToAbsoluteLimit" \\| "EntireElement" |  | How far the element is shown on the floor plan (Show Projection) |
| `curtainWalls[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `curtainWalls[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `curtainWalls[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `curtainWalls[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `curtainWalls[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `modify_curtain_wall_parts`

Changes individual curtain wall PANELS (outer/inner/cut surface, thickness, building material, delete, class) and FRAMES (surface, building material, class) in one undo step. Setting a property customizes the part (it leaves its class) unless classId is given. Get the part GUIDs from get_element_details of the curtain wall ('panels', 'frames') or get_subelements. Returns [{guid} | {error}].

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `parts` | object[] | yes | Panel / frame patches |
| `parts[].guid` | string | yes | GUID of a CurtainWallPanel or CurtainWallFrame (see 'panels'/'frames' in get_element_details of the curtain wall) |
| `parts[].outerSurface` | integer \\| string \\| object \\| boolean |  | Panel only: outer surface (customizes the panel) |
| `parts[].innerSurface` | any |  | Panel only: inner surface (customizes the panel) |
| `parts[].cutSurface` | any |  | Panel only: edge/cut surface (customizes the panel) |
| `parts[].thickness` | number |  | Panel only: thickness in m (customizes the panel) |
| `parts[].deleted` | boolean |  | Panel only: true = delete the panel (leaves an empty cell) |
| `parts[].surface` | any |  | Frame only: surface (customizes the frame) |
| `parts[].buildingMaterial` | any |  | Panel or frame: building material (customizes it) |
| `parts[].classId` | integer |  | Assign a class: panels 0 = deleted, 1+ = panelClasses[classId-1]; frames 0 Merged, 1 Division, 2 Corner, 3 Boundary, 4+ = frameClasses[classId-4] |
| `parts[].layer` | any |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `create_stairs`

Creates stairs in one undo step from a baseline (bottom to top): 'begin'/'end' for a straight flight or a 'baseline' polyline for turning stairs (landings/winders follow the Stair tool defaults). Typical: {begin: {x:0,y:0}, end: {x:4.5,y:0}, height: 3, width: 1.2, riserCount: 17}. The baseline must be long enough for the treads (≈ (riserCount-1) × treadDepth); if Archicad rejects the geometry because of the stair rules, adjust the values or pass ignoreRules: true. Coordinates in meters, angles in degrees, on the given story (default: current story); unspecified settings come from the tool defaults. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}]. get_element_details shows riser/tread counts, pitch, baseline, boundaries and part GUIDs; add a railing along a boundary with create_railings.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `stairs` | object[] | yes | Stairs to create |
| `stairs[].baseline` | object[] \\| object |  | Stair baseline on the plan (m), from the bottom to the top step: 2 points = straight flight; more points = turns (landings/winders by the tool defaults) |
| `stairs[].baseline.points` | any[] | yes |  |
| `stairs[].baseline.arcs` | object[] |  |  |
| `stairs[].begin` | any |  | Straight stair: bottom point of the baseline (instead of baseline) |
| `stairs[].end` | any |  | Straight stair: top point of the baseline (instead of baseline) |
| `stairs[].baselinePosition` | "Left" \\| "Center" \\| "Right" |  | Where the baseline runs in the flight (create default: Center) |
| `stairs[].baselineOffset` | number |  | Offset of the flight from the baseline (m) |
| `stairs[].height` | number |  | Total stair height (m). Unlinks the top unless topLinkedStory is also given. height = riserCount × riserHeight is always kept: give any two of the three; height alone keeps the current riser height as closely as possible (rounded riser count) |
| `stairs[].topLinkedStory` | integer |  | Link the top to the story N levels above (0 = unlinked, use height) |
| `stairs[].topOffset` | number |  | Offset from the linked top story (m) |
| `stairs[].width` | number |  | Flight width (m) |
| `stairs[].riserCount` | integer |  | Number of risers (riserHeight = height / riserCount unless riserHeight is given; with riserHeight and no height, height = riserCount × riserHeight) |
| `stairs[].riserHeight` | number |  | Target riser height (m). Without riserCount the count is rounded to fit the height and the riser height adjusted to divide it exactly |
| `stairs[].treadDepth` | number |  | Tread (going) depth in m; locks the tread depth unless treadDepthLocked is given |
| `stairs[].treadDepthLocked` | boolean |  | Keep treadDepth fixed when the stair is recalculated |
| `stairs[].totalHeightLocked` | boolean |  | Keep the total height fixed when the stair is recalculated |
| `stairs[].walkingLinePosition` | any |  | Position of the walking line |
| `stairs[].walkingLineOffset` | number |  | Offset of the walking line (m) |
| `stairs[].direction` | "Upward" \\| "Inverse" |  | Upward = the stair climbs from the first to the last baseline point |
| `stairs[].numbering` | "Treads" \\| "Risers" |  | What the step numbering counts |
| `stairs[].extraTopTread` | boolean |  | Add a tread at the top |
| `stairs[].extraBottomTread` | boolean |  | Add a tread at the bottom |
| `stairs[].treadThickness` | number |  | Tread thickness of flights and landings (m) |
| `stairs[].riserThickness` | number |  | Riser thickness (m) |
| `stairs[].riserCrossSection` | "Simple" \\| "Slanted" |  | Riser cross section |
| `stairs[].riserAngle` | number |  | Slant angle of slanted risers (degrees) |
| `stairs[].ignoreRules` | boolean |  | true = switch off the stair rule checks (riser/tread limits, 2R+G, pitch) — use when creation fails because of the rules |
| `stairs[].layer` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `stairs[].layer.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `stairs[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `stairs[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `stairs[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `stairs[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `modify_stairs`

Changes existing stairs in one undo step: height / top link, width, riser count/height, tread depth and locks, baseline/walking line position, direction, numbering, tread/riser thickness, rule checks. The baseline cannot be changed (move/rotate or recreate). Returns [{guid} | {error}].

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `stairs` | object[] | yes | Stair patches |
| `stairs[].guid` | string | yes | GUID of the stair to change |
| `stairs[].baselinePosition` | "Left" \\| "Center" \\| "Right" |  | Where the baseline runs in the flight (create default: Center) |
| `stairs[].baselineOffset` | number |  | Offset of the flight from the baseline (m) |
| `stairs[].height` | number |  | Total stair height (m). Unlinks the top unless topLinkedStory is also given. height = riserCount × riserHeight is always kept: give any two of the three; height alone keeps the current riser height as closely as possible (rounded riser count) |
| `stairs[].topLinkedStory` | integer |  | Link the top to the story N levels above (0 = unlinked, use height) |
| `stairs[].topOffset` | number |  | Offset from the linked top story (m) |
| `stairs[].width` | number |  | Flight width (m) |
| `stairs[].riserCount` | integer |  | Number of risers (riserHeight = height / riserCount unless riserHeight is given; with riserHeight and no height, height = riserCount × riserHeight) |
| `stairs[].riserHeight` | number |  | Target riser height (m). Without riserCount the count is rounded to fit the height and the riser height adjusted to divide it exactly |
| `stairs[].treadDepth` | number |  | Tread (going) depth in m; locks the tread depth unless treadDepthLocked is given |
| `stairs[].treadDepthLocked` | boolean |  | Keep treadDepth fixed when the stair is recalculated |
| `stairs[].totalHeightLocked` | boolean |  | Keep the total height fixed when the stair is recalculated |
| `stairs[].walkingLinePosition` | any |  | Position of the walking line |
| `stairs[].walkingLineOffset` | number |  | Offset of the walking line (m) |
| `stairs[].direction` | "Upward" \\| "Inverse" |  | Upward = the stair climbs from the first to the last baseline point |
| `stairs[].numbering` | "Treads" \\| "Risers" |  | What the step numbering counts |
| `stairs[].extraTopTread` | boolean |  | Add a tread at the top |
| `stairs[].extraBottomTread` | boolean |  | Add a tread at the bottom |
| `stairs[].treadThickness` | number |  | Tread thickness of flights and landings (m) |
| `stairs[].riserThickness` | number |  | Riser thickness (m) |
| `stairs[].riserCrossSection` | "Simple" \\| "Slanted" |  | Riser cross section |
| `stairs[].riserAngle` | number |  | Slant angle of slanted risers (degrees) |
| `stairs[].ignoreRules` | boolean |  | true = switch off the stair rule checks (riser/tread limits, 2R+G, pitch) — use when creation fails because of the rules |
| `stairs[].layer` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `stairs[].layer.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `stairs[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `stairs[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `stairs[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `stairs[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `create_railings`

Creates railings in one undo step along a reference line: 'begin'/'end' or a 'path' polyline (points may carry z to follow a slope; add arcs for curves) with the Railing tool's default posts/rails/panels. Set 'height', 'bottomOffset' and the reference line side. Coordinates in meters, angles in degrees, on the given story (default: current story); unspecified settings come from the tool defaults. Tip: to guard a stair, use its leftBoundary/rightBoundary from get_element_details as the path. Returns [{guid, type} | {error}]; get_element_details lists the segments and the GUIDs of posts, rails, handrails, panels and balusters.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `railings` | object[] | yes | Railings to create |
| `railings[].path` | object[] \\| object |  | Railing reference line (m): points {x, y, z?} (z = elevation of that point above the railing base, e.g. to follow a ramp; all or none) and optional arcs. Repeat the first point at the end for a closed railing |
| `railings[].path.points` | any[] | yes |  |
| `railings[].path.arcs` | object[] |  |  |
| `railings[].begin` | any |  | Straight railing: start point (instead of path) |
| `railings[].end` | any |  | Straight railing: end point (instead of path) |
| `railings[].height` | number |  | Railing (segment) height in m |
| `railings[].bottomOffset` | number |  | Base elevation relative to the home story (m) |
| `railings[].referenceLine` | "Left" \\| "Center" \\| "Right" |  | Where the reference line sits in the railing |
| `railings[].offset` | number |  | Horizontal offset of the segments from the reference line (m) |
| `railings[].postOffset` | number |  | Offset of the posts at the nodes (m) |
| `railings[].referenceLinePen` | integer |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `railings[].contourPen` | any |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `railings[].layer` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `railings[].layer.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `railings[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `railings[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `railings[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `railings[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `modify_railings`

Changes existing railings in one undo step: height of all segments, bottom offset, reference line side, segment offset, post offset, pens. The path cannot be changed (move/rotate or recreate). Returns [{guid} | {error}].

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `railings` | object[] | yes | Railing patches |
| `railings[].guid` | string | yes | GUID of the railing to change |
| `railings[].height` | number |  | Railing (segment) height in m |
| `railings[].bottomOffset` | number |  | Base elevation relative to the home story (m) |
| `railings[].referenceLine` | "Left" \\| "Center" \\| "Right" |  | Where the reference line sits in the railing |
| `railings[].offset` | number |  | Horizontal offset of the segments from the reference line (m) |
| `railings[].postOffset` | number |  | Offset of the posts at the nodes (m) |
| `railings[].referenceLinePen` | integer |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `railings[].contourPen` | any |  | Pen index 1-255 |
| `railings[].layer` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `railings[].layer.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `railings[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `railings[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `railings[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `railings[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |


## views

### `get_current_window`

Returns the active Archicad window: {type: FloorPlan | 3DModel | Section | Elevation | InteriorElevation | Detail | Worksheet | Layout | MasterLayout | DocumentFrom3D | ..., database (GUID), name, reference, title, linkedElement, story {index, name, level} (floor plan), drawingScale (N of 1:N), zoom {xMin,yMin,xMax,yMax} (2D, m), projection (3D)}. Call it before capture_view/zoom to know what is shown.

### `list_views`

Lists everything that can be opened: stories (floor plans), viewpoints (sections, elevations, interior elevations, details, worksheets, 3D documents: {type, database, name, reference, title}), layouts, and the View Map (saved views: {guid, name, id, fullName, itemType, folder, windowType, database}). Use the results with open_view (database/name) and go_to_view (guid). Names are localized (Russian).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `include` | "stories" \\| "viewpoints" \\| "layouts" \\| "viewMap" \\| "projectMap" \\| "all"[] |  | Parts to return (default stories, viewpoints, layouts, viewMap). projectMap = the full Project Map tree items |
| `types` | "Section" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Detail" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "DocumentFrom3D" \\| "Layout" \\| "MasterLayout" \\| "FloorPlan" \\| "3D" \\| "3DModel"[] |  | Only these viewpoint / view types |
| `nameContains` | string |  | Case-insensitive filter on names, IDs and folders |
| `limit` | integer |  | Maximum items per list (default 1000) |

### `open_view`

Brings a window to the front: a floor plan story ({window:'FloorPlan', story}), the 3D window ({window:'3D', projection?}), a section/elevation/interior elevation/detail/worksheet/3D document/layout by name ({window:'Section', name:'A-A'}), by marker element ({element}), by database GUID ({database}) or by Navigator item ({navigatorItem}). Returns {opened, window}. Saved views with their layer/scale settings: go_to_view. To look at the result use capture_view.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `window` | "FloorPlan" \\| "3D" \\| "3DModel" \\| "Section" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Detail" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Layout" \\| "MasterLayout" \\| "DocumentFrom3D" |  | Window to open: 'FloorPlan' (+ story), '3D' (+ projection), or a viewpoint type 'Section' \| 'Elevation' \| 'InteriorElevation' \| 'Detail' \| 'Worksheet' \| 'Layout' \| 'MasterLayout' \| 'DocumentFrom3D' (+ name, or the only one of that type) |
| `story` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Floor plan story to show (index or localized name, see get_stories). Implies window 'FloorPlan' |
| `story.name` | string | yes |  |
| `name` | string |  | Name, reference ID or title of the section/elevation/detail/worksheet/layout to open (see list_views; localized) |
| `element` | string \\| object |  | GUID of a section (CutPlane), elevation, interior elevation, detail or worksheet MARKER element: opens its viewpoint |
| `element.guid` | any | yes |  |
| `segmentIndex` | integer |  | Interior elevation marker only: which segment's view to open (default 0) |
| `database` | any |  | Database GUID of the viewpoint/layout (the 'database' field of list_views / get_current_window) |
| `navigatorItem` | string \\| object |  | Project Map / Layout Book / View Map item to open (window only; use go_to_view to also apply a saved view's settings) |
| `navigatorItem.guid` | string | yes | Navigator item GUID (from list_views) |
| `projection` | "perspective" \\| "axonometric" |  | 3D window only: switch the projection mode before opening |

### `go_to_view`

Opens a saved view of the View Map exactly like double-clicking it in the Navigator: switches window/story and applies the view's layer combination, scale, model view options, pen set, graphic overrides and zoom. Accepts the Navigator item GUID or its name (see list_views include ['viewMap']). Project Map / Layout Book items are opened without view settings. Returns {view, method, window}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `view` | string \\| object | yes | Navigator item: GUID string (from list_views / the official Navigator tree), or the view name / '<ID> <name>' as shown in the Navigator (localized, e.g. Russian; exact match first, then case-insensitive, then 'contains'). Ambiguous names return the candidates. |
| `view.guid` | string | yes | Navigator item GUID (from list_views) |

### `zoom`

Zooms the active window: fit (whole drawing/model), box (2D rectangle in m), elements (GUIDs), selection, in/out by a factor, previous (undo zoom) or redraw. margin adds space around 2D zooms. Returns {mode, window: {..., zoom}}. Tip: zoom {mode:'elements', elements, margin: 0.2} and then capture_view to inspect specific elements.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | "fit" \\| "box" \\| "elements" \\| "selection" \\| "in" \\| "out" \\| "previous" \\| "redraw" |  | fit = fit the whole drawing/model in the window (default; 'zoom extents'), box = show the rectangle 'box' (2D windows), elements = zoom to 'elements', selection = zoom to the selected elements, in/out = zoom by 'factor', previous = undo zoom 'steps', redraw = just redraw |
| `box` | object |  | mode 'box': area to show (m) |
| `box.xMin` | number | yes | Left edge (m, project coordinates; paper mm-coordinates in layouts) |
| `box.yMin` | number | yes | Bottom edge |
| `box.xMax` | number | yes | Right edge |
| `box.yMax` | number | yes | Top edge |
| `elements` | string \\| object[] |  | mode 'elements': elements to zoom to |
| `factor` | number |  | mode 'in'/'out': zoom factor (default 2) |
| `center` | object |  | mode 'in'/'out' in 2D: zoom around this point (default: window center) |
| `margin` | number |  | 2D: extra space around fit/box/elements/selection as a fraction of the area (e.g. 0.1 = 10% on each side) |
| `steps` | integer |  | mode 'previous': how many zoom steps to go back (default 1) |

### `get_3d_view`

Returns the 3D view state: projection {mode, perspective: {camera {x,y,z}, target {x,y,z}, viewCone, roll, distance, azimuth, viewDirection, sun} | axonometric: {projection preset, azimuth, tranmat, derived view direction, sun}}, 3D style {current, available, model (Shading/HiddenLine/...), ...}, windowSize, filter {allStories, fromStory, toStory, mode (all/selection/marquee), elementTypes}, cutPlanes, rendering {scenes, imageSize} and modelExtent (3D bounding box, m). Angles in degrees.

### `set_3d_view`

Sets up the 3D window. Easiest overview: {mode:'perspective', azimuth: 225, altitude: 30} (camera south-west of the model, fits the whole model). Exact camera: {mode:'perspective', camera:{x,y,z}, target:{x,y,z}, viewCone}. Axonometry: {mode:'axonometric', projection:'Isometric', azimuth} or {projection:'TopView'}. Also: sun, style (3D style name), styleSettings (shading/hidden line, shadows...), windowSize, stories range, elementTypes filter, cutPlanes. Opens the 3D window unless open3D is false. Returns {changed, camera {verified, warning?} (perspective) | axonometric {viewMatrixChanged, warning?}, projection, window}. Follow with capture_view to see the result.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | "perspective" \\| "axonometric" |  | Projection mode. Default: perspective when camera/target/viewCone/roll/distance are given, axonometric when projection/tranmat are given, otherwise the current mode |
| `camera` | object |  | Perspective: camera (eye) position in m, z = absolute elevation (e.g. 1.6 above a story level for eye height) |
| `target` | any |  | Perspective: point the camera looks at (m). With azimuth/altitude it is the orbit center (default: center of the model) |
| `azimuth` | number |  | Degrees CCW from +X (east; 90 = north). Perspective orbit: direction FROM the target TO the camera, e.g. 225 = camera south-west of the model looking north-east. Axonometric: the camera azimuth of the parallel projection |
| `altitude` | number |  | Degrees above the horizon of the view direction's source: perspective orbit camera elevation angle (default 30); axonometric: makes a free 'Parallel' view from that elevation (experimental) |
| `distance` | number |  | Perspective orbit: camera–target distance in m (default: the whole model fits in view) |
| `viewCone` | number |  | Perspective field of view in degrees (default: current or 60; 35-50 = natural, 70-90 = wide interior shots) |
| `roll` | number |  | Perspective camera roll in degrees (0 = level horizon) |
| `twoPointPerspective` | boolean |  | Perspective: keep vertical lines vertical (2-point perspective) |
| `projection` | "Isometric" \\| "Dimetric" \\| "Monometric" \\| "Frontal" \\| "TopView" \\| "FrontView" \\| "SideView" \\| "BottomView" \\| "FrontalBottom" \\| "MonometricBottom" \\| "IsometricBottom" \\| "DimetricBottom" \\| "Parallel" \\| "CustomAxonometry" |  | Axonometric (parallel) projection preset. TopView = plan from above, FrontView/SideView = elevations, Isometric/Dimetric/Monometric/Frontal = classic axonometries (rotated by azimuth), *Bottom = seen from below, Parallel = free parallel view (use with azimuth + altitude) |
| `tranmat` | number[] |  | Axonometric: raw 3x4 view matrix (row-major, as returned by get_3d_view) — copy it from a view you liked |
| `sun` | object |  | Sun position for shading/shadows (sets 'given by angles') |
| `sun.azimuth` | number |  | Sun azimuth in degrees (CCW from +X) |
| `sun.altitude` | number |  | Sun altitude in degrees |
| `style` | string |  | 3D style name to make current (localized; see get_3d_view style.available) |
| `styleSettings` | object |  | Edits the CURRENT 3D style definition (applied after 'style'; affects every view using that style), e.g. {model:'HiddenLine'} for a line drawing or {model:'Shading', sunShadows:'AllSurfacesContoursOn'}. Current values: get_3d_view style |
| `styleSettings.model` | "Block" \\| "Wireframe" \\| "HiddenLine" \\| "Shading" |  | 3D rendering mode: Shading (colored surfaces), HiddenLine (black lines, good for reading geometry), Wireframe, Block |
| `styleSettings.transparency` | boolean |  | Show transparent surfaces (glass) as transparent in Shading mode |
| `styleSettings.monochrome` | boolean |  | Monochrome (white model) display |
| `styleSettings.contours` | "Draft" \\| "Off" \\| "Best" |  | Shading contours |
| `styleSettings.sunShadows` | "Off" \\| "AllSurfacesContoursOff" \\| "AllSurfacesContoursOn" \\| "OneLevelContoursOff" \\| "OneLevelContoursOn" |  | Vectorial sun shadows |
| `styleSettings.vectorHatching` | boolean |  | Vectorial 3D hatching (surface patterns) |
| `styleSettings.castShadowPercent` | integer |  | Cast shadow intensity % |
| `styleSettings.shadingPercent` | integer |  | Shading intensity % |
| `styleSettings.backgroundAsInRendering` | boolean |  | Use the rendering background |
| `styleSettings.skyColor` | object |  | Sky (background) color, components 0..1 |
| `styleSettings.skyColor.r` | number | yes |  |
| `styleSettings.skyColor.g` | number | yes |  |
| `styleSettings.skyColor.b` | number | yes |  |
| `styleSettings.groundColor` | object |  | Ground color, components 0..1 |
| `styleSettings.groundColor.r` | number | yes |  |
| `styleSettings.groundColor.g` | number | yes |  |
| `styleSettings.groundColor.b` | number | yes |  |
| `windowSize` | object |  | Size of the 3D window image in pixels (persists; capture_view width/height changes it only temporarily) |
| `windowSize.width` | integer |  | px |
| `windowSize.height` | integer |  | px |
| `stories` | object |  | 3D story range filter (Filter and Cut Elements in 3D) |
| `stories.all` | boolean |  | true = show all stories |
| `stories.from` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Lowest story to show |
| `stories.from.name` | string | yes |  |
| `stories.to` | any |  | Highest story to show |
| `stories.trim` | boolean |  | Trim elements to the story range |
| `elementTypes` | string[] |  | Show only these element types in 3D, e.g. ['Wall','Slab','Roof','Column'] (type names as in get_supported_element_types), or ['all'] |
| `cutPlanes` | object |  | 3D cutting planes |
| `cutPlanes.enabled` | boolean |  | Turn the 3D cutting planes on/off |
| `cutPlanes.shapes` | object[] |  | Raw cutting plane shapes (advanced; copy them from get_3d_view cutPlanes.shapes) |
| `cutPlanes.shapes[].a` | number | yes |  |
| `cutPlanes.shapes[].b` | number | yes |  |
| `cutPlanes.shapes[].c` | number | yes |  |
| `cutPlanes.shapes[].d` | number | yes |  |
| `cutPlanes.shapes[].status` | integer |  | Archicad cut status code (default 2) |
| `cutPlanes.shapes[].pen` | integer |  |  |
| `cutPlanes.shapes[].material` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Surface of the cut faces |
| `open3D` | boolean |  | Bring the 3D window to the front afterwards (default true) |

### `show_in_3d`

Shows elements in the 3D window: mode 'all' (Show All in 3D, resets a previous selection filter), 'selection' (the selected elements only) or give 'elements' (GUIDs: they get selected and only they are shown). Returns {mode, window}. Then use set_3d_view / capture_view.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | "all" \\| "selection" \\| "elements" |  | Default: 'elements' when elements are given, else 'all' |
| `elements` | string \\| object[] |  | Elements to show (replaces the current selection) |

### `capture_view`

Claude's eyes: saves the ACTIVE Archicad window (floor plan, section, elevation, detail, worksheet, layout or 3D) as a picture and returns it as an image. Optional preparation in the same call, in this order: goToView (saved view), view (same fields as open_view), threeD (same fields as set_3d_view; switches to 3D), zoom (same fields as zoom). 2D windows are captured as currently zoomed (use zoom {mode:'fit'} or {mode:'elements'} first); 3D captures use the 3D window size — width/height set it temporarily. Images are downscaled to maxSize (default 1600px). Examples: {view:{window:'FloorPlan', story:0}, zoom:{mode:'fit'}}; {threeD:{mode:'perspective', azimuth:225, altitude:25}}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `goToView` | string \\| object |  | First open this saved view (go_to_view) |
| `goToView.guid` | string | yes | Navigator item GUID (from list_views) |
| `view` | object |  | First open this window (open_view fields) |
| `view.window` | "FloorPlan" \\| "3D" \\| "3DModel" \\| "Section" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Detail" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Layout" \\| "MasterLayout" \\| "DocumentFrom3D" |  | Window to open: 'FloorPlan' (+ story), '3D' (+ projection), or a viewpoint type 'Section' \| 'Elevation' \| 'InteriorElevation' \| 'Detail' \| 'Worksheet' \| 'Layout' \| 'MasterLayout' \| 'DocumentFrom3D' (+ name, or the only one of that type) |
| `view.story` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Floor plan story to show (index or localized name, see get_stories). Implies window 'FloorPlan' |
| `view.story.name` | string | yes |  |
| `view.name` | string |  | Name, reference ID or title of the section/elevation/detail/worksheet/layout to open (see list_views; localized) |
| `view.element` | string \\| object |  | GUID of a section (CutPlane), elevation, interior elevation, detail or worksheet MARKER element: opens its viewpoint |
| `view.element.guid` | any | yes |  |
| `view.segmentIndex` | integer |  | Interior elevation marker only: which segment's view to open (default 0) |
| `view.database` | any |  | Database GUID of the viewpoint/layout (the 'database' field of list_views / get_current_window) |
| `view.navigatorItem` | any |  | Project Map / Layout Book / View Map item to open (window only; use go_to_view to also apply a saved view's settings) |
| `view.projection` | "perspective" \\| "axonometric" |  | 3D window only: switch the projection mode before opening |
| `threeD` | object |  | First set up the 3D view (set_3d_view fields) and switch to the 3D window |
| `threeD.mode` | "perspective" \\| "axonometric" |  | Projection mode. Default: perspective when camera/target/viewCone/roll/distance are given, axonometric when projection/tranmat are given, otherwise the current mode |
| `threeD.camera` | object |  | Perspective: camera (eye) position in m, z = absolute elevation (e.g. 1.6 above a story level for eye height) |
| `threeD.target` | any |  | Perspective: point the camera looks at (m). With azimuth/altitude it is the orbit center (default: center of the model) |
| `threeD.azimuth` | number |  | Degrees CCW from +X (east; 90 = north). Perspective orbit: direction FROM the target TO the camera, e.g. 225 = camera south-west of the model looking north-east. Axonometric: the camera azimuth of the parallel projection |
| `threeD.altitude` | number |  | Degrees above the horizon of the view direction's source: perspective orbit camera elevation angle (default 30); axonometric: makes a free 'Parallel' view from that elevation (experimental) |
| `threeD.distance` | number |  | Perspective orbit: camera–target distance in m (default: the whole model fits in view) |
| `threeD.viewCone` | number |  | Perspective field of view in degrees (default: current or 60; 35-50 = natural, 70-90 = wide interior shots) |
| `threeD.roll` | number |  | Perspective camera roll in degrees (0 = level horizon) |
| `threeD.twoPointPerspective` | boolean |  | Perspective: keep vertical lines vertical (2-point perspective) |
| `threeD.projection` | "Isometric" \\| "Dimetric" \\| "Monometric" \\| "Frontal" \\| "TopView" \\| "FrontView" \\| "SideView" \\| "BottomView" \\| "FrontalBottom" \\| "MonometricBottom" \\| "IsometricBottom" \\| "DimetricBottom" \\| "Parallel" \\| "CustomAxonometry" |  | Axonometric (parallel) projection preset. TopView = plan from above, FrontView/SideView = elevations, Isometric/Dimetric/Monometric/Frontal = classic axonometries (rotated by azimuth), *Bottom = seen from below, Parallel = free parallel view (use with azimuth + altitude) |
| `threeD.tranmat` | number[] |  | Axonometric: raw 3x4 view matrix (row-major, as returned by get_3d_view) — copy it from a view you liked |
| `threeD.sun` | object |  | Sun position for shading/shadows (sets 'given by angles') |
| `threeD.sun.azimuth` | number |  | Sun azimuth in degrees (CCW from +X) |
| `threeD.sun.altitude` | number |  | Sun altitude in degrees |
| `threeD.style` | string |  | 3D style name to make current (localized; see get_3d_view style.available) |
| `threeD.styleSettings` | object |  | Edits the CURRENT 3D style definition (applied after 'style'; affects every view using that style), e.g. {model:'HiddenLine'} for a line drawing or {model:'Shading', sunShadows:'AllSurfacesContoursOn'}. Current values: get_3d_view style |
| `threeD.styleSettings.model` | "Block" \\| "Wireframe" \\| "HiddenLine" \\| "Shading" |  | 3D rendering mode: Shading (colored surfaces), HiddenLine (black lines, good for reading geometry), Wireframe, Block |
| `threeD.styleSettings.transparency` | boolean |  | Show transparent surfaces (glass) as transparent in Shading mode |
| `threeD.styleSettings.monochrome` | boolean |  | Monochrome (white model) display |
| `threeD.styleSettings.contours` | "Draft" \\| "Off" \\| "Best" |  | Shading contours |
| `threeD.styleSettings.sunShadows` | "Off" \\| "AllSurfacesContoursOff" \\| "AllSurfacesContoursOn" \\| "OneLevelContoursOff" \\| "OneLevelContoursOn" |  | Vectorial sun shadows |
| `threeD.styleSettings.vectorHatching` | boolean |  | Vectorial 3D hatching (surface patterns) |
| `threeD.styleSettings.castShadowPercent` | integer |  | Cast shadow intensity % |
| `threeD.styleSettings.shadingPercent` | integer |  | Shading intensity % |
| `threeD.styleSettings.backgroundAsInRendering` | boolean |  | Use the rendering background |
| `threeD.styleSettings.skyColor` | object |  | Sky (background) color, components 0..1 |
| `threeD.styleSettings.groundColor` | object |  | Ground color, components 0..1 |
| `threeD.windowSize` | object |  | Size of the 3D window image in pixels (persists; capture_view width/height changes it only temporarily) |
| `threeD.windowSize.width` | integer |  | px |
| `threeD.windowSize.height` | integer |  | px |
| `threeD.stories` | object |  | 3D story range filter (Filter and Cut Elements in 3D) |
| `threeD.stories.all` | boolean |  | true = show all stories |
| `threeD.stories.from` | any |  | Lowest story to show |
| `threeD.stories.to` | any |  | Highest story to show |
| `threeD.stories.trim` | boolean |  | Trim elements to the story range |
| `threeD.elementTypes` | string[] |  | Show only these element types in 3D, e.g. ['Wall','Slab','Roof','Column'] (type names as in get_supported_element_types), or ['all'] |
| `threeD.cutPlanes` | object |  | 3D cutting planes |
| `threeD.cutPlanes.enabled` | boolean |  | Turn the 3D cutting planes on/off |
| `threeD.cutPlanes.shapes` | object[] |  | Raw cutting plane shapes (advanced; copy them from get_3d_view cutPlanes.shapes) |
| `zoom` | object |  | Zoom before capturing (zoom tool fields) |
| `zoom.mode` | "fit" \\| "box" \\| "elements" \\| "selection" \\| "in" \\| "out" \\| "previous" \\| "redraw" |  | fit = fit the whole drawing/model in the window (default; 'zoom extents'), box = show the rectangle 'box' (2D windows), elements = zoom to 'elements', selection = zoom to the selected elements, in/out = zoom by 'factor', previous = undo zoom 'steps', redraw = just redraw |
| `zoom.box` | object |  | mode 'box': area to show (m) |
| `zoom.box.xMin` | number | yes | Left edge (m, project coordinates; paper mm-coordinates in layouts) |
| `zoom.box.yMin` | number | yes | Bottom edge |
| `zoom.box.xMax` | number | yes | Right edge |
| `zoom.box.yMax` | number | yes | Top edge |
| `zoom.elements` | any[] |  | mode 'elements': elements to zoom to |
| `zoom.factor` | number |  | mode 'in'/'out': zoom factor (default 2) |
| `zoom.center` | object |  | mode 'in'/'out' in 2D: zoom around this point (default: window center) |
| `zoom.margin` | number |  | 2D: extra space around fit/box/elements/selection as a fraction of the area (e.g. 0.1 = 10% on each side) |
| `zoom.steps` | integer |  | mode 'previous': how many zoom steps to go back (default 1) |
| `width` | integer |  | 3D: capture width in px (temporarily resizes the 3D window); 2D: max width of the returned image |
| `height` | integer |  | 3D: capture height in px; 2D: max height of the returned image |
| `keepSelectionHighlight` | boolean |  | Keep the selection highlight in the picture (default false) |
| `cropToWindow` | boolean |  | true (default) = only the visible window area; false = the whole drawing |
| `format` | "png" \\| "jpeg" |  | Image format (default png; jpeg is smaller for shaded 3D views and renders) |
| `maxSize` | integer |  | Longest side of the returned image in px (default 1600); larger pictures are downscaled |
| `saveTo` | string |  | Also keep a full-resolution copy at this absolute file path |

### `render_view`

Photo-renders the current 3D view (camera / projection of the 3D window) with Archicad's rendering engine and returns the image. Optionally set up the camera first (threeD, same fields as set_3d_view), pick a rendering scene (names in get_3d_view rendering.scenes) and the output size (width/height px, restored afterwards). Rendering can take minutes (timeoutSeconds, default 600). For quick looks use capture_view of the 3D window instead.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `threeD` | object |  | First set up the 3D view (set_3d_view fields) |
| `threeD.mode` | "perspective" \\| "axonometric" |  | Projection mode. Default: perspective when camera/target/viewCone/roll/distance are given, axonometric when projection/tranmat are given, otherwise the current mode |
| `threeD.camera` | object |  | Perspective: camera (eye) position in m, z = absolute elevation (e.g. 1.6 above a story level for eye height) |
| `threeD.target` | any |  | Perspective: point the camera looks at (m). With azimuth/altitude it is the orbit center (default: center of the model) |
| `threeD.azimuth` | number |  | Degrees CCW from +X (east; 90 = north). Perspective orbit: direction FROM the target TO the camera, e.g. 225 = camera south-west of the model looking north-east. Axonometric: the camera azimuth of the parallel projection |
| `threeD.altitude` | number |  | Degrees above the horizon of the view direction's source: perspective orbit camera elevation angle (default 30); axonometric: makes a free 'Parallel' view from that elevation (experimental) |
| `threeD.distance` | number |  | Perspective orbit: camera–target distance in m (default: the whole model fits in view) |
| `threeD.viewCone` | number |  | Perspective field of view in degrees (default: current or 60; 35-50 = natural, 70-90 = wide interior shots) |
| `threeD.roll` | number |  | Perspective camera roll in degrees (0 = level horizon) |
| `threeD.twoPointPerspective` | boolean |  | Perspective: keep vertical lines vertical (2-point perspective) |
| `threeD.projection` | "Isometric" \\| "Dimetric" \\| "Monometric" \\| "Frontal" \\| "TopView" \\| "FrontView" \\| "SideView" \\| "BottomView" \\| "FrontalBottom" \\| "MonometricBottom" \\| "IsometricBottom" \\| "DimetricBottom" \\| "Parallel" \\| "CustomAxonometry" |  | Axonometric (parallel) projection preset. TopView = plan from above, FrontView/SideView = elevations, Isometric/Dimetric/Monometric/Frontal = classic axonometries (rotated by azimuth), *Bottom = seen from below, Parallel = free parallel view (use with azimuth + altitude) |
| `threeD.tranmat` | number[] |  | Axonometric: raw 3x4 view matrix (row-major, as returned by get_3d_view) — copy it from a view you liked |
| `threeD.sun` | object |  | Sun position for shading/shadows (sets 'given by angles') |
| `threeD.sun.azimuth` | number |  | Sun azimuth in degrees (CCW from +X) |
| `threeD.sun.altitude` | number |  | Sun altitude in degrees |
| `threeD.style` | string |  | 3D style name to make current (localized; see get_3d_view style.available) |
| `threeD.styleSettings` | object |  | Edits the CURRENT 3D style definition (applied after 'style'; affects every view using that style), e.g. {model:'HiddenLine'} for a line drawing or {model:'Shading', sunShadows:'AllSurfacesContoursOn'}. Current values: get_3d_view style |
| `threeD.styleSettings.model` | "Block" \\| "Wireframe" \\| "HiddenLine" \\| "Shading" |  | 3D rendering mode: Shading (colored surfaces), HiddenLine (black lines, good for reading geometry), Wireframe, Block |
| `threeD.styleSettings.transparency` | boolean |  | Show transparent surfaces (glass) as transparent in Shading mode |
| `threeD.styleSettings.monochrome` | boolean |  | Monochrome (white model) display |
| `threeD.styleSettings.contours` | "Draft" \\| "Off" \\| "Best" |  | Shading contours |
| `threeD.styleSettings.sunShadows` | "Off" \\| "AllSurfacesContoursOff" \\| "AllSurfacesContoursOn" \\| "OneLevelContoursOff" \\| "OneLevelContoursOn" |  | Vectorial sun shadows |
| `threeD.styleSettings.vectorHatching` | boolean |  | Vectorial 3D hatching (surface patterns) |
| `threeD.styleSettings.castShadowPercent` | integer |  | Cast shadow intensity % |
| `threeD.styleSettings.shadingPercent` | integer |  | Shading intensity % |
| `threeD.styleSettings.backgroundAsInRendering` | boolean |  | Use the rendering background |
| `threeD.styleSettings.skyColor` | object |  | Sky (background) color, components 0..1 |
| `threeD.styleSettings.groundColor` | object |  | Ground color, components 0..1 |
| `threeD.windowSize` | object |  | Size of the 3D window image in pixels (persists; capture_view width/height changes it only temporarily) |
| `threeD.windowSize.width` | integer |  | px |
| `threeD.windowSize.height` | integer |  | px |
| `threeD.stories` | object |  | 3D story range filter (Filter and Cut Elements in 3D) |
| `threeD.stories.all` | boolean |  | true = show all stories |
| `threeD.stories.from` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Lowest story to show |
| `threeD.stories.to` | any |  | Highest story to show |

| `threeD.elementTypes` | string[] |  | Show only these element types in 3D, e.g. ['Wall','Slab','Roof','Column'] (type names as in get_supported_element_types), or ['all'] |

| `timeoutSeconds` | integer |  | Maximum rendering time to wait (default 600) |
| `format` | "png" \\| "jpeg" |  | Image format (default png; jpeg is smaller for shaded 3D views and renders) |
| `maxSize` | integer |  | Longest side of the returned image in px (default 1600); larger pictures are downscaled |
| `saveTo` | string |  | Also keep a full-resolution copy at this absolute file path |

### `create_sections`

Creates section markers (cut planes) on the floor plan, each with its own new section viewpoint (one undo step). Each item: begin/end of the cut line (m), viewSide (left/right of begin→end, default left), depth (limits how far the section looks), vertical range, name ('Разрез 1-1'), referenceId, storyIndex/layer. Returns [{guid, type, name, referenceId, database, begin, end, viewSide, depth} | {error}]. Open one with open_view {element: guid} and look at it with capture_view.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sections` | object[] | yes |  |
| `sections[].begin` | object | yes | Start of the cut line (m) |
| `sections[].end` | object | yes | End of the cut line (m) |
| `sections[].viewSide` | "left" \\| "right" |  | Which side of the line begin→end the view looks at (default 'left'; e.g. begin (0,0) → end (10,0) with 'left' looks north, +Y) |
| `sections[].depth` | number |  | Horizontal depth: distance of the depth limit line from the cut line in m (default 10 m when limited). Giving it makes the range 'Limited' |
| `sections[].horizontalRange` | "Infinite" \\| "Limited" \\| "ZeroDepth" |  | Horizontal range: Infinite = everything behind the line, Limited = up to 'depth', ZeroDepth = only the cut |
| `sections[].verticalRange` | string \\| object |  | Vertical range of the view: 'Infinite' or {min, max} absolute elevations (m) |
| `sections[].verticalRange.min` | number | yes | Bottom of the vertical range: absolute elevation in m |
| `sections[].verticalRange.max` | number | yes | Top of the vertical range: absolute elevation in m |
| `sections[].name` | string |  | Viewpoint name shown in the Navigator and in the marker (e.g. 'Section A-A') |
| `sections[].referenceId` | string |  | Reference ID shown in the marker (e.g. 'A'); Archicad may auto-number when omitted |
| `sections[].layer` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `sections[].layer.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `sections[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `sections[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `sections[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `sections[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `create_elevations`

Creates elevation markers on the floor plan (one undo step), each with a new elevation viewpoint. begin/end = the elevation line (m) placed OUTSIDE the building, viewSide = the side the elevation looks at (towards the building), depth = how far it sees. E.g. south facade of a building spanning y 0..10: begin (-5,-5), end (20,-5), viewSide 'left' (looks north). Returns [{guid, name, database, ...}].

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elevations` | object[] | yes |  |
| `elevations[].begin` | object | yes | Start of the cut line (m) |
| `elevations[].end` | object | yes | End of the cut line (m) |
| `elevations[].viewSide` | "left" \\| "right" |  | Which side of the line begin→end the view looks at (default 'left'; e.g. begin (0,0) → end (10,0) with 'left' looks north, +Y) |
| `elevations[].depth` | number |  | Horizontal depth: distance of the depth limit line from the cut line in m (default 10 m when limited). Giving it makes the range 'Limited' |
| `elevations[].horizontalRange` | "Infinite" \\| "Limited" \\| "ZeroDepth" |  | Horizontal range: Infinite = everything behind the line, Limited = up to 'depth', ZeroDepth = only the cut |
| `elevations[].verticalRange` | string \\| object |  | Vertical range of the view: 'Infinite' or {min, max} absolute elevations (m) |
| `elevations[].verticalRange.min` | number | yes | Bottom of the vertical range: absolute elevation in m |
| `elevations[].verticalRange.max` | number | yes | Top of the vertical range: absolute elevation in m |
| `elevations[].name` | string |  | Viewpoint name shown in the Navigator and in the marker (e.g. 'Section A-A') |
| `elevations[].referenceId` | string |  | Reference ID shown in the marker (e.g. 'A'); Archicad may auto-number when omitted |
| `elevations[].layer` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `elevations[].layer.index` | integer | yes |  |
| `elevations[].storyIndex` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Home story (defaults to the current story) |
| `elevations[].storyIndex.name` | string | yes |  |
| `elevations[].renovationStatus` | "Existing" \\| "New" \\| "Demolished" \\| "Default" |  | Renovation status |
| `elevations[].elementId` | string |  | Element ID text shown in the Info Box / schedules |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `create_interior_elevations`

Creates interior elevation markers (one undo step): 'points' is a polyline inside a room along its walls; every segment becomes one interior elevation view (closed: true for all walls). depth = view depth per segment (m). If a view looks the wrong way, recreate it with the points in reverse order. Returns [{guid, name, segments: [{index, name, database}]}] — open a segment with open_view {element, segmentIndex}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `interiorElevations` | object[] | yes |  |
| `interiorElevations[].points` | object[] | yes | Polyline of the elevation lines (m), usually along the inside of the walls of a room; each segment becomes one elevation view |
| `interiorElevations[].closed` | boolean |  | Close the polyline (last point back to the first), e.g. all 4 walls of a room |
| `interiorElevations[].depth` | number |  | Depth of each segment's view in m (how far behind the line elements are shown; default tool setting or 1 m) |
| `interiorElevations[].offset` | number |  | Horizontal offset of the segments (m, Archicad's creation offset; default 0) |
| `interiorElevations[].horizontalRange` | "Infinite" \\| "Limited" \\| "ZeroDepth" |  |  |
| `interiorElevations[].verticalRange` | string \\| object |  | Vertical range of the view: 'Infinite' or {min, max} absolute elevations (m) |
| `interiorElevations[].verticalRange.min` | number | yes | Bottom of the vertical range: absolute elevation in m |
| `interiorElevations[].verticalRange.max` | number | yes | Top of the vertical range: absolute elevation in m |
| `interiorElevations[].name` | string |  | Viewpoint name shown in the Navigator and in the marker (e.g. 'Section A-A') |
| `interiorElevations[].referenceId` | string |  | Reference ID shown in the marker (e.g. 'A'); Archicad may auto-number when omitted |
| `interiorElevations[].layer` | integer \\| string \\| object |  | Layer name or index (defaults to the tool's default layer) |
| `interiorElevations[].layer.index` | integer | yes |  |
