| `issues[].tagText` | string |  | Tag text: short label placed as a tag next to the highlighted elements |
| `issues[].tagTextVisible` | boolean |  | Show the tag text element in the model views (default true) |
| `issues[].parentIssue` | string \\| object |  | Make this a sub-issue of an existing issue (GUID or exact name) |
| `issues[].parentIssue.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `issues[].comment` | string \\| object |  | First comment: text, or {text, author?, status?} |
| `issues[].comment.text` | string | yes | Comment text |
| `issues[].comment.author` | string |  | Author shown in the Issue Manager (default: the Teamwork user name, or 'Claude') |
| `issues[].comment.status` | "Error" \\| "Warning" \\| "Info" \\| "Unknown" |  | Comment status shown in the Issue Manager: Error, Warning, Info or Unknown (default) |
| `issues[].comments` | any[] |  | Further comments, added in order after 'comment' |
| `issues[].attach` | object |  | Elements to attach right away, by attachment type |
| `issues[].attach.highlight` | any \\| object[] |  | Elements to highlight (point at) in this issue |
| `issues[].attach.creation` | any |  | Elements proposed as new (Creation) |
| `issues[].attach.deletion` | any |  | Elements proposed for deletion (Deletion) |
| `issues[].attach.modification` | any |  | Elements proposed for modification: Archicad creates a modified copy of each (use attach_elements_to_issue with modificationPairs to pair existing elements) |
| `undoName` | string |  | Name of the undo step shown in Archicad |

### `delete_issue`

Deletes issues (one undo step) together with their comments. By default the proposals attached as Creation/Deletion/Modification are discarded (the model stays as it is); acceptAllElements: true accepts them first. Output: {results: [{guid, name, deleted: true} | {error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `issues` | string \\| object[] | yes | Issues to delete (GUIDs or exact names, see get_issues) |
| `acceptAllElements` | boolean |  | Accept the attached creation/deletion/modification proposals before deleting (default false) |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `add_issue_comment`

Adds comments to issues (one undo step; several comments / issues per call). Output: {results: [{issue: {guid, name}, comment: {guid, author, text, status, created}} | {error}]} in input order.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comments` | object[] | yes | Comments to add |
| `comments[].text` | string | yes | Comment text |
| `comments[].author` | string |  | Author shown in the Issue Manager (default: the Teamwork user name, or 'Claude') |
| `comments[].status` | "Error" \\| "Warning" \\| "Info" \\| "Unknown" |  | Comment status shown in the Issue Manager: Error, Warning, Info or Unknown (default) |
| `comments[].issue` | string \\| object | yes | Issue to comment on (GUID or exact name) |
| `comments[].issue.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `get_issue_comments`

Returns the comments of issues, oldest first. Output: {issues: [{guid, name, comments: [{guid, author, text, status: Error|Warning|Info|Unknown, created (ISO 8601 UTC)}]}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `issues` | string \\| object[] |  | Issues to read (GUIDs or exact names); default: all issues |

### `attach_elements_to_issue`

Attaches elements to an issue (one undo step). type Highlight (default) just points at them; Creation / Deletion mark them as proposed new / to-be-deleted elements; Modification either pairs existing elements (modificationPairs: original → proposed replacement) or, with plain elements, lets Archicad create a modified copy of each. Output: {issue: {guid, name}, type, attached: [guid], missing?: [guid of non-existing elements], modificationPairs?: [{original, modified}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `issue` | string \\| object | yes | Issue (GUID or exact name, see get_issues) |
| `issue.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `elements` | any \\| object[] |  | Elements to attach |
| `type` | "Highlight" \\| "Creation" \\| "Deletion" \\| "Modification" |  | How the elements relate to the issue: Highlight (just point at them; default), Creation (proposed new elements), Deletion (elements proposed for deletion), Modification (proposed changes: pairs original → modified) |
| `modificationPairs` | object[] |  | Only with type Modification (implied when omitted): pairs of original and modified elements |
| `modificationPairs[].original` | any | yes | Existing element |
| `modificationPairs[].modified` | any | yes | Its proposed modified version |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `detach_elements_from_issue`

Detaches elements from an issue whatever their attachment type (one undo step); the elements themselves are not changed. Output: {issue: {guid, name}, detached: [guid], notAttached?: [guid]}. Fails when none of them is attached (see get_issue_elements).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `issue` | string \\| object | yes | Issue (GUID or exact name) |
| `issue.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `elements` | any \\| object[] | yes | Elements to detach |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `get_issue_elements`

Returns the elements attached to issues, by attachment type. Output: {issues: [{guid, name, tagTextElement?, elements: {creation|highlight|deletion|modification: [{guid, type} | {guid, missing: true}]}}]}. Pass the GUIDs to get_element_details, select_elements or zoom tools to inspect them.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `issues` | string \\| object[] |  | Issues to read (GUIDs or exact names); default: all issues |
| `types` | "Highlight" \\| "Creation" \\| "Deletion" \\| "Modification"[] |  | Only these attachment types (default: all four) |

### `export_bcf`

Exports issues to a BCF file (.bcfzip, BCF 2.1) for other BIM tools (Solibri, Revit, BIMcollab, ...). Output: {path, exported, issues: [{guid, name}], fileExists}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | yes | Absolute target file path on the Archicad machine, e.g. '/Users/me/Desktop/review.bcfzip' ('~/' allowed; '.bcfzip' is appended when there is no extension) |
| `issues` | string \\| object[] |  | Issues to export (GUIDs or exact names); default: all issues |
| `useExternalId` | boolean |  | Reference elements by the IFC GlobalIds they had in an imported IFC model (default false = Archicad's own IFC GUIDs) |
| `alignBySurveyPoint` | boolean |  | Use the Survey Point as the coordinate origin (default true) |
| `overwrite` | boolean |  | Replace an existing file (default false: an existing file is an error) |
| `createFolders` | boolean |  | Create missing parent folders (default false) |

### `import_bcf`

Imports the issues (topics, comments, referenced elements) of a BCF file (.bcfzip / .bcf, BCF 2.x) into the Issue Manager without dialogs (one undo step). Elements referenced by IFC GlobalId are matched to the model. Output: {imported, issues: [...the new issues in get_issues format]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | yes | Absolute path of the BCF file on the Archicad machine ('~/' allowed) |
| `alignBySurveyPoint` | boolean |  | The BCF coordinates are relative to the Survey Point (default true) |
| `openIssuePalette` | boolean |  | Open the Issue Manager palette after the import (default false) |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `get_favorites`

Lists the favorites (saved tool settings of the Favorites palette). Output: {favorites: [{name, type, variation?, folder: [..], folderPath, settings?, classifications?, categories?, properties?, notes?} | {error}], count, total/offset/hasMore when paginated}. With includeSettings the settings use the same field names as get_tool_defaults and the create_* tools. Favorite names are localized (Russian templates ship Russian names) — always take them from here. Filter by type/search/folder before using includeSettings.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Dimension" \\| "RadialDimension" \\| "LevelDimension" \\| "AngleDimension" \\| "Text" \\| "Label" \\| "Zone" \\| "Hatch" \\| "Line" \\| "PolyLine" \\| "Arc" \\| "Circle" \\| "Spline" \\| "Hotspot" \\| "CutPlane" \\| "Camera" \\| "CamSet" \\| "Group" \\| "SectElem" \\| "Drawing" \\| "Picture" \\| "Detail" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Hotlink" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "CurtainWallSegment" \\| "CurtainWallFrame" \\| "CurtainWallPanel" \\| "CurtainWallJunction" \\| "CurtainWallAccessory" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "ChangeMarker" \\| "Stair" \\| "Riser" \\| "Tread" \\| "StairStructure" \\| "Railing" \\| "RailingToprail" \\| "RailingHandrail" \\| "RailingRail" \\| "RailingPost" \\| "RailingInnerPost" \\| "RailingBaluster" \\| "RailingPanel" \\| "RailingSegment" \\| "RailingNode" \\| "RailingBalusterSet" \\| "RailingPattern" \\| "RailingToprailEnd" \\| "RailingHandrailEnd" \\| "RailingRailEnd" \\| "RailingToprailConnection" \\| "RailingHandrailConnection" \\| "RailingRailConnection" \\| "RailingEndFinish" \\| "BeamSegment" \\| "ColumnSegment" \\| "Opening" |  | Only favorites of this element type |
| `variation` | "Generic" \\| "Object" \\| "Light" \\| "SymbStair" \\| "GridElement" \\| "WallEnd" \\| "Door" \\| "Skylight" \\| "CornerWindow" |  | Tool variation, only for tools that share an element type (e.g. 'GridElement' vs 'Object' for Object elements). Normally omit it; get_tool_defaults without a type lists the toolbox tools with their variations |
| `search` | string |  | Case-insensitive substring of the favorite name |
| `folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Only favorites in this folder (or its subfolders) |
| `names` | string[] |  | Only these exact names |
| `includeSettings` | boolean |  | Also return each favorite's settings, classifications, categories and properties (default false; slower) |
| `includeGdlParameters` | boolean |  | With includeSettings: include the GDL parameter values of library-part based favorites (default true) |
| `offset` | integer |  | Skip this many results |
| `limit` | integer |  | Return at most this many results (default 500) |

### `apply_favorite`

Applies a favorite. target 'Defaults' (default) makes it the current settings of its tool, like double-clicking it in the Favorites palette — elements created afterwards (in Archicad or with create_* tools without explicit values) get these settings. target 'Elements' injects its settings into existing elements of the SAME type (geometry, position and story are kept). Output: {favorite, type, variation?, target, applied? (Defaults), results?: [{guid, applied: true} | {error}] (Elements), warnings?}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | yes | Exact favorite name (localized, as listed by get_favorites) |
| `target` | "Defaults" \\| "Elements" |  | 'Defaults' (tool settings) or 'Elements' (existing elements). Default: Elements when elements are given, else Defaults |
| `elements` | string \\| object[] |  | target Elements: the elements to change (must be of the favorite's type) |
| `applyProperties` | boolean |  | Also apply the favorite's stored property values (default true) |
| `applyClassifications` | boolean |  | Also apply the favorite's classifications (default true) |
| `applyCategories` | boolean |  | Also apply the favorite's element categories (default true) |
| `undoName` | string |  |  |

### `create_favorite`

Creates favorites in the Favorites palette from placed elements or from the current tool defaults (set them first with set_tool_defaults to build a favorite from scratch). Each item needs exactly one of element / toolDefaults. Output: {results: [{name, type, variation?, folder, replaced?} | {error}]} in input order. In Teamwork, reserve the 'Favorites' object set first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `favorites` | object[] | yes | Favorites to create |
| `favorites[].name` | string | yes | Name of the new favorite |
| `favorites[].element` | string \\| object |  | Copy the settings of this placed element (like 'Save selection as Favorite') |
| `favorites[].element.guid` | any | yes |  |
| `favorites[].toolDefaults` | object |  | Copy the current default settings of this tool (see get_tool_defaults / set_tool_defaults) |
| `favorites[].toolDefaults.type` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Dimension" \\| "RadialDimension" \\| "LevelDimension" \\| "AngleDimension" \\| "Text" \\| "Label" \\| "Zone" \\| "Hatch" \\| "Line" \\| "PolyLine" \\| "Arc" \\| "Circle" \\| "Spline" \\| "Hotspot" \\| "CutPlane" \\| "Camera" \\| "CamSet" \\| "Group" \\| "SectElem" \\| "Drawing" \\| "Picture" \\| "Detail" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Hotlink" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "CurtainWallSegment" \\| "CurtainWallFrame" \\| "CurtainWallPanel" \\| "CurtainWallJunction" \\| "CurtainWallAccessory" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "ChangeMarker" \\| "Stair" \\| "Riser" \\| "Tread" \\| "StairStructure" \\| "Railing" \\| "RailingToprail" \\| "RailingHandrail" \\| "RailingRail" \\| "RailingPost" \\| "RailingInnerPost" \\| "RailingBaluster" \\| "RailingPanel" \\| "RailingSegment" \\| "RailingNode" \\| "RailingBalusterSet" \\| "RailingPattern" \\| "RailingToprailEnd" \\| "RailingHandrailEnd" \\| "RailingRailEnd" \\| "RailingToprailConnection" \\| "RailingHandrailConnection" \\| "RailingRailConnection" \\| "RailingEndFinish" \\| "BeamSegment" \\| "ColumnSegment" \\| "Opening" | yes | Element tool type (case-sensitive), e.g. 'Wall', 'Column', 'Beam', 'Slab', 'Roof', 'Door', 'Window', 'Object', 'Zone', 'Line', 'Text' |
| `favorites[].toolDefaults.variation` | "Generic" \\| "Object" \\| "Light" \\| "SymbStair" \\| "GridElement" \\| "WallEnd" \\| "Door" \\| "Skylight" \\| "CornerWindow" |  | Tool variation, only for tools that share an element type (e.g. 'GridElement' vs 'Object' for Object elements). Normally omit it; get_tool_defaults without a type lists the toolbox tools with their variations |
| `favorites[].folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Favorites palette folder to put it in (default: root). Missing folders are created |
| `favorites[].replace` | boolean |  | Overwrite an existing favorite with the same name (default false = error) |
| `favorites[].includeProperties` | boolean |  | Store the custom property values too (default true) |
| `favorites[].includeClassifications` | boolean |  | Store the classifications too (default true) |
| `favorites[].includeCategories` | boolean |  | Store the element categories (structural function, position, renovation) too (default true) |

### `delete_favorite`

Deletes favorites by exact name (placed elements are not affected). Output: {results: [{name, deleted: true} | {error}]}. In Teamwork, reserve the 'Favorites' object set first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `names` | string[] | yes | Exact names of the favorites to delete (see get_favorites) |

### `rename_favorite`

Renames a favorite. Output: {name, newName, renamed: true}. Fails when newName is already used.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | yes | Exact favorite name (localized, as listed by get_favorites) |
| `newName` | string | yes | New name |

### `export_favorites`

Exports favorites to a preferences file (.prf) that other projects can import with import_favorites (or Archicad's Favorites palette). Output: {path, exported, fileExists}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | yes | Absolute target path, e.g. '/Users/me/Desktop/favorites.prf' ('~/' allowed; '.prf' appended when there is no extension) |
| `names` | string[] |  | Favorites to export (default: all) |
| `overwrite` | boolean |  | Replace an existing file (default false) |
| `createFolders` | boolean |  | Create missing parent folders (default false) |

### `import_favorites`

Imports favorites from a .prf file exported by Archicad / export_favorites. Output: {imported: [new names], count, firstConflict?}. In Teamwork, reserve the 'Favorites' object set first.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | yes | Absolute path of the .prf file ('~/' allowed) |
| `folder` | string[] \\| string |  | Target folder in the Favorites palette (default: root) |
| `importFolders` | boolean |  | Keep the folder structure stored in the file (default true) |
| `conflictPolicy` | "Append" \\| "Overwrite" \\| "Skip" \\| "Error" |  | When a name already exists: Append (default: import under a new name), Overwrite, Skip, or Error (stop) |

### `get_tool_defaults`

Returns the default settings of element tools — what the next element placed in Archicad (or created by a create_* tool without explicit values) gets. Without type/types: lists the toolbox tools {tools: [{type, variation?}], activeTool, hint}. With types: {defaults: [{type, variation?, settings: {layer, renovationStatus, drawIndex, elementId?, ...the same fields as the type's create_* tool (heights, thicknesses, structure/composite/buildingMaterial, surfaces, libraryPart, params {GDL name: value}...), gdlParameterCount?}, classifications?: [{system, systemGuid, itemGuid, itemId, itemName}], categories?: {StructuralFunction: {value, valueGuid, category}, ...}, properties?: [{guid, name, group, value | status}], notes?} | {error}]}. Use it before set_tool_defaults to see the current values and field names.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Dimension" \\| "RadialDimension" \\| "LevelDimension" \\| "AngleDimension" \\| "Text" \\| "Label" \\| "Zone" \\| "Hatch" \\| "Line" \\| "PolyLine" \\| "Arc" \\| "Circle" \\| "Spline" \\| "Hotspot" \\| "CutPlane" \\| "Camera" \\| "CamSet" \\| "Group" \\| "SectElem" \\| "Drawing" \\| "Picture" \\| "Detail" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Hotlink" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "CurtainWallSegment" \\| "CurtainWallFrame" \\| "CurtainWallPanel" \\| "CurtainWallJunction" \\| "CurtainWallAccessory" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "ChangeMarker" \\| "Stair" \\| "Riser" \\| "Tread" \\| "StairStructure" \\| "Railing" \\| "RailingToprail" \\| "RailingHandrail" \\| "RailingRail" \\| "RailingPost" \\| "RailingInnerPost" \\| "RailingBaluster" \\| "RailingPanel" \\| "RailingSegment" \\| "RailingNode" \\| "RailingBalusterSet" \\| "RailingPattern" \\| "RailingToprailEnd" \\| "RailingHandrailEnd" \\| "RailingRailEnd" \\| "RailingToprailConnection" \\| "RailingHandrailConnection" \\| "RailingRailConnection" \\| "RailingEndFinish" \\| "BeamSegment" \\| "ColumnSegment" \\| "Opening" |  | Tool to read (e.g. 'Wall') |
| `variation` | "Generic" \\| "Object" \\| "Light" \\| "SymbStair" \\| "GridElement" \\| "WallEnd" \\| "Door" \\| "Skylight" \\| "CornerWindow" |  | Tool variation, only for tools that share an element type (e.g. 'GridElement' vs 'Object' for Object elements). Normally omit it; get_tool_defaults without a type lists the toolbox tools with their variations |
| `types` | any \\| object[] |  | Several tools at once: type names or {type, variation} |
| `includeGdlParameters` | boolean |  | Library-part based tools: include the GDL parameter values (default true) |
| `gdlParameterNames` | string[] |  | Only these GDL parameters (names as in get_gdl_parameters) |
| `includeHiddenGdlParameters` | boolean |  | Also include hidden GDL parameters (default false) |
| `includeClassifications` | boolean |  | Include the default classifications (default true) |
| `includeCategories` | boolean |  | Include the default element categories (default true) |
| `includeProperties` | boolean |  | Include custom properties whose default value was overridden (default false) |
| `includeAllProperties` | boolean |  | Include ALL custom properties available for the tool, with their values (default false) |

### `set_tool_defaults`

Changes the default settings of element tools (like the tool's Default Settings dialog), e.g. set the Wall tool to 3 m high 0.25 m composite walls on a given layer before drawing many walls, or prepare settings the user will draw with. Every field of the type's create_* tool can be set. Fields are applied independently: an invalid field is reported in 'rejected' without blocking the others. Output: {results: [{type, variation?, applied: [field], ignored?: [geometry fields], rejected?: [{field, error}], notes?, settings? (the resulting defaults)} | {error}]}. Save them as a favorite with create_favorite {toolDefaults: {type}}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `defaults` | object[] | yes | One entry per tool |
| `defaults[].type` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Dimension" \\| "RadialDimension" \\| "LevelDimension" \\| "AngleDimension" \\| "Text" \\| "Label" \\| "Zone" \\| "Hatch" \\| "Line" \\| "PolyLine" \\| "Arc" \\| "Circle" \\| "Spline" \\| "Hotspot" \\| "CutPlane" \\| "Camera" \\| "CamSet" \\| "Group" \\| "SectElem" \\| "Drawing" \\| "Picture" \\| "Detail" \\| "Elevation" \\| "InteriorElevation" \\| "Worksheet" \\| "Hotlink" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "CurtainWallSegment" \\| "CurtainWallFrame" \\| "CurtainWallPanel" \\| "CurtainWallJunction" \\| "CurtainWallAccessory" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "ChangeMarker" \\| "Stair" \\| "Riser" \\| "Tread" \\| "StairStructure" \\| "Railing" \\| "RailingToprail" \\| "RailingHandrail" \\| "RailingRail" \\| "RailingPost" \\| "RailingInnerPost" \\| "RailingBaluster" \\| "RailingPanel" \\| "RailingSegment" \\| "RailingNode" \\| "RailingBalusterSet" \\| "RailingPattern" \\| "RailingToprailEnd" \\| "RailingHandrailEnd" \\| "RailingRailEnd" \\| "RailingToprailConnection" \\| "RailingHandrailConnection" \\| "RailingRailConnection" \\| "RailingEndFinish" \\| "BeamSegment" \\| "ColumnSegment" \\| "Opening" | yes | Element tool type (case-sensitive), e.g. 'Wall', 'Column', 'Beam', 'Slab', 'Roof', 'Door', 'Window', 'Object', 'Zone', 'Line', 'Text' |
| `defaults[].variation` | "Generic" \\| "Object" \\| "Light" \\| "SymbStair" \\| "GridElement" \\| "WallEnd" \\| "Door" \\| "Skylight" \\| "CornerWindow" |  | Tool variation, only for tools that share an element type (e.g. 'GridElement' vs 'Object' for Object elements). Normally omit it; get_tool_defaults without a type lists the toolbox tools with their variations |
| `defaults[].fields` | object | yes | Settings to change, with EXACTLY the field names and units of the type's create_* tool (create_walls, create_columns, create_beams, create_slabs, create_roofs, create_doors, create_windows, create_objects, create_zones, create_lines, create_texts...; meters, degrees). Geometry/identity fields (begin, end, polygon, position, points, storyIndex, guid) are ignored — defaults do not store them. Also accepted for every type: layer (name/index), renovationStatus (Existing\|New\|Demolished\|Default), drawIndex, elementId (ID of the next placed element); library-part based tools: libraryPart (or 'stamp' for zones) + params / gdlParams / stampParameters {name: value}, objects/lamps also sizeA, sizeB, height; classifications: [classification item GUID]; categories: {StructuralFunction\|Position\|RenovationStatus\|RenovationFilter: localized value name or GUID}; properties: {property GUID \| name \| 'Group/Name': value} (custom properties). Examples — Wall: {height: 3, thickness: 0.25, composite: '...', referenceLine: 'Center', layer: '...'}; Slab: {thickness: 0.2, buildingMaterial: '...'}; Door: {libraryPart: '...', width: 0.9, height: 2.1}; Object: {libraryPart: '...', params: {...}} |
| `returnSettings` | boolean |  | Return the resulting default settings of each tool (default true) |

### `get_revisions`

Read-only document revision data (Document > Issue Manager for revisions: revision issues and the layout revisions they contain). Output: {issues?: [{guid, id, description, issued, issueTime, issuedBy, overrideRevisionId, createNewRevision, visibleMarkersInIssues, customFields: {name: value}, documentRevisionCount}], documentRevisions?: [{guid, id, finalId, status: Actual|Issued, owner?, issue?: {guid, id}, layout: {id, name, databaseGuid, masterLayout, width, height, drawingScales, subsetId, subsetName, teamworkOwner?, customFields}}]}. Revision issues are NOT Issue Manager markup issues (get_issues).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `include` | "issues" \\| "documentRevisions"[] |  | What to return (default both) |
| `issue` | string |  | Only the document revisions of this revision issue (its GUID or ID, from issues[]) |

### `get_revision_changes`

Read-only Change Manager data (changes tracked for revisions, shown by change markers). Give ONE mode (default: every change of the project): documentRevision (changes in one layout revision, GUID from get_revisions), layouts / allLayouts (current revision changes of layouts), elements (changes an element belongs to) or changeIds. Output: {changes: [{id, description, lastModified, modifiedBy, issued, archived, customFields, firstIssue?}], count} | {documentRevision, changes} | {layouts: [{layout: {databaseGuid, id, name}, changes | error}]} | {elements: [{guid, changeIds, changes} | {error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `documentRevision` | string |  | Document revision GUID (documentRevisions[].guid of get_revisions) |
| `layouts` | string \\| object[] |  | Layouts: layout database GUID (get_revisions layout.databaseGuid), layout ID, name, or 'ID name' |
| `allLayouts` | boolean |  | Current revision changes of every layout |
| `elements` | any \\| object[] |  | Elements whose changes to return |
| `changeIds` | string[] |  | Change IDs to look up |
| `includeFirstIssue` | boolean |  | Also return the first revision issue each change was issued in (default false) |


## official

### `list_elements`

Lists element GUIDs of the whole project (all stories) through the official JSON API, optionally only some types or only the current selection, with each element's type and the number of elements per type (countsByType). Returns {total, offset, returned, hasMore, countsByType, elements: [{guid, type}]}. For attribute/geometry filters (story, layer, region, ID...) use find_elements; for details of the returned elements use get_element_details, for extents get_bounding_boxes. Works without the Claude Connector add-on.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `types` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Zone" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "Stair" \\| "Railing" \\| "Opening"[] |  | Only these element types. Omit for every element (including types not in this list, reported as 'Unknown') |
| `selectedOnly` | boolean |  | Only the elements currently selected in Archicad (default false) |
| `onlyEditable` | boolean |  | With selectedOnly: skip selected elements that are locked / on locked layers / not reserved |
| `includeTypes` | boolean |  | Return {guid, type} items and countsByType (default true). false returns plain GUID strings — fastest for huge projects |
| `offset` | integer |  | Skip this many results |
| `limit` | integer |  | Return at most this many results (default 500) |

### `get_element_types`

Returns the type of each element (Wall, Slab, Door, Zone, ...) — e.g. to sort GUIDs returned by other tools. Output: {elements: [{guid, type} | {guid, error}]} in input order ('Unknown' for types the official API does not name, e.g. 2D elements).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |

### `get_bounding_boxes`

Returns axis-aligned bounding boxes of elements in project coordinates (meters): 3D boxes {xMin, yMin, zMin, xMax, yMax, zMax} (z absolute, from project zero) and/or 2D floor-plan boxes {xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax}. Also returns `overall`, the union of all boxes — handy to frame a view, place new elements next to existing ones, or check overlaps. Output: {elements: [{guid, box3D?, box2D?} | {guid, error}], overall3D?, overall2D?}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `kind` | "3D" \\| "2D" \\| "both" |  | Which boxes: '3D' (default), '2D' (floor plan) or 'both' |

### `get_elements_related_to_zones`

For each zone (room), returns the elements that bound or belong to it (walls, columns, doors, windows, slabs, objects... as Archicad relates them), grouped by type. Output: {zones: [{zone, total, elements: {Wall: [guid...], Door: [...]}} | {zone, error}]}. Get zone GUIDs with list_elements {types: ['Zone']}. The passed GUIDs must be zones.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `zones` | string \\| object[] | yes | Zone GUIDs |
| `elementTypes` | "Wall" \\| "Column" \\| "Beam" \\| "Window" \\| "Door" \\| "Object" \\| "Lamp" \\| "Slab" \\| "Roof" \\| "Mesh" \\| "Zone" \\| "CurtainWall" \\| "Shell" \\| "Skylight" \\| "Morph" \\| "Stair" \\| "Railing" \\| "Opening"[] |  | Only related elements of these types (default: all types) |
| `groupByType` | boolean |  | Group the related elements by type (default true); false returns a flat GUID list |

### `get_element_components`

Lists the components of elements — the building-material parts that Archicad lists in component schedules (one per skin of a composite, per profile part, per basic structure). By default each component comes with a summary: BuildingMaterial_Name, Component_Thickness (m), Component_NetVolume / Component_GrossVolume (m³), Component_NetProjectedArea / Component_CrossSectionArea (m²). Pass `properties` to read other values instead, or [] for ids only. Output: {elements: [{guid, components: [{component, values: {name: value}, unavailable?: {name: status}}]} | {guid, error}]}. Use get_component_property_values to read more properties of specific components.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `properties` | string \\| object[] |  | Properties to read per component (default: the summary above; [] = component ids only) |

### `get_component_property_values`

Reads property values of element components (building-material parts — see get_element_components): built-in quantities such as Component_Thickness, Component_NetVolume, Component_NetProjectedArea, BuildingMaterial_Name/ID/Manufacturer/Description, or any user-defined property available for components. Lengths m, areas m², volumes m³, angles degrees. Either pass `components` [{element, component}] or `elements` (= all their components). Output: {components: [{element, component, values: {property: value}, unavailable?: {property: 'notAvailable'|'notEvaluated'|'userUndefined'|error}} | {element, component, error}], propertyErrors?}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `components` | object[] |  | Specific components |
| `components[].element` | string \\| object | yes | Element GUID string, or {"guid": "..."} |
| `components[].element.guid` | any | yes |  |
| `components[].component` | string | yes | Component GUID from get_element_components |
| `elements` | any[] |  | Read all components of these elements |
| `properties` | string \\| object[] | yes | Properties to read |

### `get_property_ids_by_name`

Resolves property names to GUIDs and definitions, or searches the property catalog. Built-in properties have stable non-localized names (e.g. 'General_ElementID', 'General_Width', 'Zone_CalculatedArea', 'Component_Thickness'); user-defined ones are 'Group/Name' with localized names (e.g. 'ИНФОРМАЦИЯ О ПРОДУКТЕ/Модель'). Pass `properties` to resolve specific names, or browse with `search` (case-insensitive substring of built-in name, group or name, any language), `group`, `propertyType` and/or `elements` (only the properties those elements have — e.g. which user-defined properties their classification makes available). Returns [{guid, builtInName?, kind: 'BuiltIn'|'UserDefined', group, name, type, editable, description?, enumValues?, defaultValue?}]. The GUIDs work in every tool taking property references. Works without the Claude Connector add-on.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `properties` | string \\| object[] |  | Names/GUIDs to resolve (output in input order, with {input, error} for misses) |
| `search` | string |  | Substring to search in built-in names, group names and property names (e.g. 'Area', 'Площадь', 'Thickness') |
| `group` | string |  | Only properties of this (localized) group, e.g. 'ИНФОРМАЦИЯ О ПРОДУКТЕ' or 'Компоненты' |
| `propertyType` | "BuiltIn" \\| "UserDefined" |  | Browse only built-in or only user-defined properties |
| `elements` | any \\| object[] |  | Browse only properties available for these elements (see elementMatch) |
| `elementMatch` | "any" \\| "all" |  | With elements: 'any' (default) = available for at least one of them, 'all' = available for every one of them |
| `includeDetails` | boolean |  | Include description, enum values and default value (default: true for `properties`, false for browsing) |
| `offset` | integer |  | Skip this many results |
| `limit` | integer |  | Return at most this many results (default 500) |

### `get_classification_systems`

Lists the classification systems of the project (e.g. 'Классификация Archicad', Uniclass, OmniClass) with GUID, name, source, version, date and the number of items. Start here before classifying elements; then browse items with get_classification_tree.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `includeItemCounts` | boolean |  | Also count the items of each system (default true) |

### `get_classification_tree`

Returns the items of a classification system as a tree ({guid, id, name?, children?}) or, with `search` / format 'flat', as a flat list with full paths ('Parent > Child') and depth. Item ids are what Archicad shows (localized in Russian Archicad, e.g. 'Стена', 'Перекрытие'). Use `root` to get one branch and `maxDepth` to limit large systems (childCount tells what was cut). The item GUIDs/ids are used by set_element_classifications, get_elements_by_classification and get_classification_item_details.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `system` | string |  | Classification system name (e.g. 'Классификация Archicad') or GUID; may be omitted when the project has only one system |
| `search` | string |  | Case-insensitive substring of item id, name or description; returns a flat list of matches |
| `root` | string \\| object |  | Return only the branch below this item |
| `root.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `maxDepth` | integer |  | Levels to return (1 = top-level items only). Default: all |
| `format` | "tree" \\| "flat" |  | 'tree' (default) nested children, 'flat' list with paths |
| `includeDescriptions` | boolean |  | Include item descriptions (default false; can be long) |
| `limit` | integer |  | Flat/search results: max items (default 500) |

### `get_classification_item_details`

Returns id, name, description, full path, parent and children of classification items (by GUID, id or path). Output: {items: [{guid, id, name, description, path, system, parent?, children?} | {input, error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `items` | string \\| object[] | yes |  |
| `system` | string |  | Restrict id lookup to this system (default: search all systems) |
| `includeChildren` | boolean |  | List the direct children of each item (default true) |

### `get_element_classifications`

Returns how elements are classified in each classification system: {elements: [{guid, classifications: [{system, systemGuid, item: {guid, id, name?, path} | null (= unclassified)}]} | {guid, error}]}. Default: all systems of the project.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `systems` | string[] |  | Only these systems (names or GUIDs). Default: all |

### `set_element_classifications`

Classifies elements (one undo step per call handled by Archicad). Each assignment gives elements and the classification item (GUID, localized id like 'Стена' / 'Перекрытие', or path 'A > B'); item null makes the elements unclassified in that system. An element can have one item per system. Output: {results: [{guid, item?, system, ok: true} | {guid, error}]} per element. Check the result with get_element_classifications. Classification decides which user-defined properties are available for elements.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `assignments` | object[] | yes |  |
| `assignments[].elements` | string \\| object[] | yes | Element GUIDs |
| `assignments[].item` | string \\| object \\| null | yes | Classification item; null = unclassified in `system` |
| `assignments[].system` | string |  | System of the item (default: top-level `system`, or the system containing the item) |
| `system` | any |  | Default system for all assignments |

### `get_elements_by_classification`

Returns the elements classified with an item (GUID, localized id or path), optionally including all sub-items of the branch. Output: {item, total, countsByType, elements: [{guid, type, item?}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `item` | string \\| object | yes | Classification item: GUID, item id as shown in Archicad (localized, e.g. 'Стена', 'Перекрытие', 'Ss_25_10_30'), or path 'Parent > Child'. Browse with get_classification_tree. |
| `item.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `system` | string |  | Classification system name (e.g. 'Классификация Archicad') or GUID; may be omitted when the project has only one system |
| `includeSubItems` | boolean |  | Also elements classified with any descendant item (default false) |
| `includeTypes` | boolean |  | Add each element's type and countsByType (default true) |

### `get_classification_availability`

Shows which property definitions are available for classification items (user-defined properties appear on an element only when its classification makes them available), and/or for which classification items given properties are available. Pass `items` -> {items: [{item, properties: [{guid, group, name, type}]}]}; pass `properties` -> {properties: [{property, availableForItems: count, items: [{guid, id, path}]}]}. Change availability with modify_property_definitions {addAvailability} (properties family).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `items` | string \\| object[] |  | Classification items to inspect |
| `properties` | string \\| object[] |  | Property definitions to inspect |
| `system` | string |  | System for item id lookup (default: all systems) |
| `limit` | integer |  | Max classification items listed per property (default 200) |

### `get_navigator_tree`

Returns a Navigator tree: Project Map (viewpoints: stories, sections, elevations, interior elevations, worksheets, details, 3D documents, 3D views, schedules, project indexes, lists), View Map (saved views, each with sourceId = its Project Map viewpoint), Layout Book (subsets, layouts with their drawings, master layouts) or a publisher set. Nodes: {id, type, prefix?, name, sourceId?, children?}. With `types` / `nameFilter` or format 'flat' you get a flat list {id, type, prefix, name, path, depth, parentId, sourceId, childCount}. The ids are used by get_navigator_items, rename/move/delete_navigator_items, clone_project_map_item_to_view_map, create_layout, get/set_layout_settings and the view/documentation tools.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tree` | "ProjectMap" \\| "ViewMap" \\| "LayoutBook" \\| "MyViewMap" \\| "PublisherSet" |  | Which tree (default 'ProjectMap') |
| `publisherSet` | string |  | Publisher set name for tree 'PublisherSet' (see get_publisher_sets) |
| `root` | string |  | Only the subtree below this item id |
| `maxDepth` | integer |  | Levels below the root to return (0 = root only). Default: all |
| `types` | "UndefinedItem" \\| "ProjectMapRootItem" \\| "StoryItem" \\| "SectionItem" \\| "ElevationItem" \\| "InteriorElevationItem" \\| "WorksheetItem" \\| "DetailItem" \\| "DocumentFrom3DItem" \\| "Perspective3DItem" \\| "Axonometry3DItem" \\| "CameraSetItem" \\| "CameraItem" \\| "ScheduleItem" \\| "ProjectIndexItem" \\| "TextListItem" \\| "GraphicListItem" \\| "InfoItem" \\| "HelpItem" \\| "FolderItem" \\| "LayoutBookRootItem" \\| "SubsetItem" \\| "LayoutItem" \\| "DrawingItem" \\| "MasterFolderItem" \\| "MasterLayoutItem"[] |  | Only items of these types (flat list), e.g. ['LayoutItem'] or ['StoryItem','SectionItem'] |
| `nameFilter` | string |  | Case-insensitive substring of prefix/ID or name (flat list) |
| `format` | "tree" \\| "flat" |  | 'tree' (default) or 'flat' |
| `limit` | integer |  | Flat list: maximum items (default 2000) |

### `get_navigator_items`

Returns details of navigator items by id: type, name, prefix (ID), location (tree + path + parentId + sourceId), and type-specific data — stories: storyIndex and elevation (m); built-in folders: contents; layouts and master layouts: layoutSettings (paper size in mm, margins, numbering). Type-specific data of viewpoints is only available for Project Map items (a View Map item's sourceId is its viewpoint). Output: {items: [{id, type, ...} | {id, error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ids` | string[] | yes | Navigator item ids |
| `locate` | boolean |  | Find each item in the Project Map / View Map / Layout Book trees to add tree, path, name, parentId, sourceId (default true) |

### `rename_navigator_item`

Renames a navigator item (view, View Map folder, layout, subset, viewpoint...): new name and/or new ID (the prefix shown before the name, e.g. layout number '03' or subset ID 'АР'). Pass at least one of newName / newId. The item is read back from the Layout Book / View Map / Project Map: output {ok, id, tree, prefix, name, warning?} — `warning` tells when Archicad kept a different name/ID (for sheet numbers of layouts prefer set_layout_settings {customLayoutNumbering: true, customLayoutNumber}). A new ID of an auto-numbered SUBSET does not last: Archicad recomputes it on the next Layout Book change. For a lasting subset ID create the subset with create_layout_subset {customNumbering: true, customNumber} and move the layouts into it (move_navigator_item).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `item` | string | yes | Navigator item id (the 'id' GUID from get_navigator_tree) |
| `newName` | string |  | New name |
| `newId` | string |  | New ID / prefix ('' clears it where allowed) |

### `move_navigator_item`

Moves a navigator item (e.g. a view into a View Map folder, a layout into another subset) under `parent`, as its first child or right after the sibling `after`. Works inside one tree (View Map / Layout Book). Get ids with get_navigator_tree. The item is read back: output {ok, id, tree, parent, path}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `item` | string | yes | Item to move |
| `parent` | string | yes | New parent folder / subset id |
| `after` | string |  | Insert after this child of `parent` (default: first position) |

### `delete_navigator_items`

Deletes navigator items: saved views and View Map folders, layouts (with their drawings), subsets, Project Map viewpoints where Archicad allows it. This cannot be undone through this connector — check ids with get_navigator_items first. Output: {results: [{id, ok: true} | {id, error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `items` | string[] | yes | Navigator item ids to delete |

### `clone_project_map_item_to_view_map`

Saves a Project Map viewpoint (story, section, elevation, detail, worksheet, 3D view, schedule, index...) as a new view in the View Map (like 'Clone a view' in the Navigator), with the current view settings. The new view can then be placed on layouts. Output: {viewId} = the new View Map item id.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `item` | string | yes | Project Map item id (get_navigator_tree {tree: 'ProjectMap'}) |
| `parent` | string |  | View Map folder to put the view in (default: the View Map's top folder) |

### `create_view_map_folder`

Creates a folder in the View Map (to organize saved views), under `parent` (default: the View Map's top folder), optionally after the sibling `after`. Output: {folderId}. Move views into it with move_navigator_item.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | yes | Folder name |
| `parent` | string |  | Parent View Map folder id |
| `after` | any |  | Insert after this sibling |

### `create_layout`

Creates a layout (sheet) in the Layout Book from a master layout, inside a subset (or the Layout Book root). The paper size and margins come from the master (they are stored in the master and shared by all its layouts) — pick a master with the wanted size (see get_layout_settings on MasterLayoutItem ids from get_navigator_tree {tree: 'LayoutBook', types: ['MasterLayoutItem']}). Output: {layoutId, name, master, parent, settings, ignored?}. The layout ID/number is assigned by the subset numbering (e.g. subset '08' + own prefix 'T-' + '01' = '08T-01') unless customLayoutNumbering. New layouts are inserted as the FIRST child of the subset (reorder with move_navigator_item). Place drawings on it with the documentation tools.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | yes | Layout name |
| `master` | string |  | Master layout: id or name (e.g. 'А3 - А - Ф3'). Default: the first master layout |
| `parent` | string |  | Subset id or name (e.g. 'Планы'), default: the Layout Book root |
| `customLayoutNumbering` | boolean |  | true = use customLayoutNumber as the layout ID instead of the automatic subset numbering |
| `customLayoutNumber` | string |  | Custom layout ID (used when customLayoutNumbering is true), e.g. 'A-101' |
| `doNotIncludeInNumbering` | boolean |  | Exclude this layout from the automatic ID sequence of its subset |

### `create_layout_subset`

Creates a subset (folder with its own numbering) in the Layout Book, under `parent` (subset id/name, default: root). Layout IDs in the subset are [upper prefix][own prefix][number in numberingStyle, from startAt] (e.g. ownPrefix 'A-', style '01', startAt 1 -> A-01, A-02...), or continue the previous subset's sequence. Output: {subsetId}. Check the resulting IDs with get_navigator_tree {tree: 'LayoutBook'}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | yes | Subset name |
| `parent` | string |  | Parent subset id or name (default: Layout Book root) |
| `includeToIDSequence` | boolean |  | Include the subset's layouts in the ID sequence (default true) |
| `useUpperPrefix` | boolean |  | Prepend the parent subset's prefix (default true) |
| `addOwnPrefix` | boolean |  | Add ownPrefix to layout IDs (default: true when ownPrefix is given) |
| `ownPrefix` | string |  | Own prefix, e.g. 'A-' (default '') |
| `continueNumbering` | boolean |  | Continue the IDs of the previous subset (own prefix and startAt are then not used). Default: false when ownPrefix or startAt is given, else true |
| `startAt` | integer |  | First number when continueNumbering is false (default 1) |
| `numberingStyle` | "1" \\| "01" \\| "001" \\| "0001" \\| "abc" \\| "ABC" \\| "noID" \\| "Undefined" |  | Number style of layout IDs (default '1'; 'noID' = no numbers) |
| `customNumbering` | boolean |  | Use customNumber as the subset ID instead of automatic numbering (default false) |
| `customNumber` | string |  | Custom subset ID (with customNumbering) |
| `autoNumber` | string |  | Automatic number text (advanced, default '') |

### `get_layout_settings`

Returns the settings of layouts or master layouts: paper size (horizontalSize x verticalSize, MILLIMETERS) and margins (mm) — both defined by the master layout —, custom numbering (customLayoutNumbering, customLayoutNumber), doNotIncludeInNumbering, displayMasterLayoutBelow (meaningful on master layouts; always false on layouts), and the read-only page count (layoutPageNumber, actPageIndex) and revision state. Layouts by id or name. Output: {layouts: [{layout, name?, settings} | {layout, error}]}. Change them with set_layout_settings.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `layouts` | string[] | yes |  |

### `set_layout_settings`

Changes settings of layouts or master layouts. Per-layout fields: customLayoutNumbering + customLayoutNumber (custom sheet ID) and doNotIncludeInNumbering. Paper size and margins (MILLIMETERS) belong to the MASTER layout: set them on a MasterLayoutItem id — or on a layout with applyToMaster: true — and every layout using that master changes. displayMasterLayoutBelow works on MasterLayoutItem ids only. Page count / page index / revision state are read-only (see get_layout_settings). Only the given fields change; the result is read back, and fields Archicad did not apply are listed in `ignored`. Output: {results: [{layout, type, settings, ignored?} | {layout, error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `layouts` | object[] | yes | Per-layout changes: {layout, field: value, ...} |
| `layouts[].layout` | string | yes | Layout: navigator item id (GUID from get_navigator_tree {tree: 'LayoutBook'}) or layout name / 'ID name' (e.g. '03 План на отм. ±0.000') |
| `layouts[].horizontalSize` | number |  | Paper width in MILLIMETERS (e.g. A3 landscape = 420) |
| `layouts[].verticalSize` | number |  | Paper height in MILLIMETERS (e.g. A3 landscape = 297) |
| `layouts[].leftMargin` | number |  | Left margin, mm |
| `layouts[].topMargin` | number |  | Top margin, mm |
| `layouts[].rightMargin` | number |  | Right margin, mm |
| `layouts[].bottomMargin` | number |  | Bottom margin, mm |
| `layouts[].displayMasterLayoutBelow` | boolean |  | MASTER layouts only: draw the master's content below (true) or above (false) the layouts' content |
| `layouts[].customLayoutNumbering` | boolean |  | true = use customLayoutNumber as the layout ID instead of the automatic subset numbering |
| `layouts[].customLayoutNumber` | string |  | Custom layout ID (used when customLayoutNumbering is true), e.g. 'A-101' |
| `layouts[].doNotIncludeInNumbering` | boolean |  | Exclude this layout from the automatic ID sequence of its subset |
| `layouts[].applyToMaster` | boolean |  | Required to change size/margins through a normal layout (they are shared with its master and all its layouts) |

### `get_publisher_sets`

Lists the publisher sets of the project (Navigator > Publisher). With `name`, also returns that set's items as a flat list {id, type, name, path, sourceId} (sourceId = the View Map view or layout that is published). Publish with the documentation tools.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | string |  | Publisher set name to expand (exact, case-insensitive or unique substring) |
| `maxDepth` | integer |  | With name: levels to list (default: all) |
| `limit` | integer |  | With name: maximum items (default 1000) |

### `get_attribute_folders`

Browses the folder structure of an attribute type (Attribute Manager folders): a folder's GUID, its attributes {guid, name} and subfolders, recursively up to `depth`. Paths are arrays of folder names from the root (localized). Output: {attributeType, path, guid, attributes, subfolders: [{path, guid, attributes?, subfolders?, subfolderCount?}]}. Use the folder paths/GUIDs with create/delete/rename_attribute_folder(s) and move_attributes_to_folder; attribute details come from get_attributes.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attributeType` | "BuildingMaterial" \\| "Composite" \\| "Fill" \\| "Layer" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "Line" \\| "PenTable" \\| "Profile" \\| "Surface" \\| "ZoneCategory" | yes | Attribute type: BuildingMaterial, Composite, Fill, Layer, LayerCombination, Line, PenTable, Profile, Surface or ZoneCategory |
| `folder` | string[] \\| string \\| object |  | Folder to open (default: root) |
| `folder.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `depth` | integer |  | Subfolder levels to expand (default 1; 0 = only this folder) |
| `includeAttributes` | boolean |  | List attributes {guid, name} of each expanded folder (default true); false = only counts |

### `create_attribute_folders`

Creates attribute folders by full path; missing parent folders are created too (e.g. ['Проект', 'Стены'] creates both). Output: {results: [{folder, guid, ok: true} | {folder, error}]}. Move attributes in with move_attributes_to_folder.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attributeType` | "BuildingMaterial" \\| "Composite" \\| "Fill" \\| "Layer" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "Line" \\| "PenTable" \\| "Profile" \\| "Surface" \\| "ZoneCategory" |  | Attribute type of all folders (or give it per folder) |
| `folders` | string[] \\| string \\| object \\| object[] | yes | Folder paths: ['A','B'] or 'A/B', or {attributeType, path} |

### `delete_attribute_folders`

Deletes attribute folders AND every deletable attribute inside them (attributes in use or built-in ones are kept). Irreversible through this connector — inspect with get_attribute_folders first, and move attributes you want to keep out with move_attributes_to_folder. Output: {results: [{folder, ok: true} | {folder, error}]}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attributeType` | "BuildingMaterial" \\| "Composite" \\| "Fill" \\| "Layer" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "Line" \\| "PenTable" \\| "Profile" \\| "Surface" \\| "ZoneCategory" |  | Attribute type of all folders (or per folder) |
| `folders` | string[] \\| string \\| object \\| object[] | yes | Folders: path ['A','B'] / 'A/B', {guid}, or {attributeType, path} |

### `move_attributes_to_folder`

Moves attributes and/or attribute folders of one type into a target folder (created beforehand with create_attribute_folders; [] = root). Attributes are given by GUID or exact localized name. Output: {ok, target, moved: {attributes, folders}}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attributeType` | "BuildingMaterial" \\| "Composite" \\| "Fill" \\| "Layer" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "Line" \\| "PenTable" \\| "Profile" \\| "Surface" \\| "ZoneCategory" | yes | Attribute type: BuildingMaterial, Composite, Fill, Layer, LayerCombination, Line, PenTable, Profile, Surface or ZoneCategory |
| `target` | string[] \\| string \\| object | yes | Target folder: path (['A','B'] / 'A/B', [] = root) or {guid} |
| `target.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `attributes` | string \\| integer \\| object[] |  | Attributes to move |
| `folders` | any[] |  | Folders to move (with their content) |

### `rename_attribute_folder`

Renames an attribute folder (path or GUID). Output: {ok, folder: {path, guid}}.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attributeType` | "BuildingMaterial" \\| "Composite" \\| "Fill" \\| "Layer" \\| "LayerCombination" \\| "Line" \\| "PenTable" \\| "Profile" \\| "Surface" \\| "ZoneCategory" | yes | Attribute type: BuildingMaterial, Composite, Fill, Layer, LayerCombination, Line, PenTable, Profile, Surface or ZoneCategory |
| `folder` | string[] \\| string \\| object | yes | Folder to rename: path (['A','B'] / 'A/B') or {guid} |
| `folder.guid` | string | yes | Element/attribute GUID |
| `newName` | string | yes | New folder name |

### `get_profile_preview`

Renders preview images (PNG) of Profile attributes (complex profiles used by walls, beams, columns, handrails...) so you can see their cross-section shape, plus each profile's name, usage and nominal size. Profiles by exact localized name or GUID (see get_attributes {type: 'Profile'} or get_attribute_folders {attributeType: 'Profile'}).

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `profiles` | string \\| integer \\| object[] | yes | Profile attributes |
| `width` | integer |  | Image width in pixels (default 400) |
| `height` | integer |  | Image height in pixels (default 400) |
| `background` | string \\| object |  | Background color '#RRGGBB' or {red, green, blue} 0..1 (default white) |
| `background.red` | number | yes |  |
| `background.green` | number | yes |  |
| `background.blue` | number | yes |  |

### `get_active_pen_tables`

Returns the pen tables (pen sets) currently used by model views and by the layout book: {modelView: {guid, name}, layoutBook: {guid, name}}. With includePens, also their 255 pens {index, color '#RRGGBB', weight (mm), description}. Pen colors of elements follow the model-view table. Change pens with modify_pens.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `includePens` | boolean |  | Include the pens of both tables (default false) |
| `pens` | integer[] |  | With includePens: only these pen indices |
